" data-portal-copyright= " ANTHONY WALLACE " **28;2595;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1590593 " /&gt; &lt;h3&gt;COVID-19 : LATEST NEWS&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; -Coronavirus has infected more than &lt;strong&gt;half a million people and killed 20,000 worldwide&lt;/strong&gt; , the head of the World Health Organization ( WHO ) said on Friday , as he appealed again for protective gear for medical staff working to save lives . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ndash ; &lt;strong&gt;Four passengers have died aboard a cruise ship stranded at sea&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;2625;TOOLONG ... 247 Canadians as international ports remain closed&lt;/a&gt; fearing the coronavirus pandemic. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; -Doctors and nurses on the front lines of the U.S. coronavirus crisis pleaded on Friday for more protective gear and equipment to treat waves of patients expected to overwhelm hospitals as the number of known &lt;strong&gt;U.S. infections surpassed 100,000 , with more than 1,500 dead&lt;/strong&gt; . The U.S. House of Representatives on Friday &lt;strong&gt;approved a $2.2 trillion aid package -- the largest in history&lt;/strong&gt; &amp;ndash ; to help cope with the economic downturn inflicted by the intensifying coronavirus pandemic , and President Donald Trump quickly signed it into law . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; -Italy&amp;rsquo ; s recorded 919 deaths from coronavirus on Friday , &lt;strong&gt;the highest daily toll anywhere in the world&lt;/strong&gt; , while the number of confirmed cases eclipsed the total in China where the virus first emerged . The number of confirmed cases rose by 5,959 on Friday to 86,498 , taking Italy past China , with the death toll at 9,134. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; -French health authorities reported 299 new deaths from coronavirus on Thursday , taking the total to 1,995 , as the government has &lt;strong&gt;decided to extend by two weeks the national lockdown , now due to end on April 15 at the earliest&lt;/strong&gt; . The climb in the number of deaths represents a daily rise of 18% , a less-marked increase from the previous day . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; -In a big boost to small and medium-sized businesses , Prime Minister Justin Trudeau announced on Friday that &lt;a href= " https : **33;2660;TOOLONG ... federal government will cover 75 per cent of wages for qualifying businesses , backdated to March 15&lt;/a&gt; . Trudeau also announced the launch of the Canada Emergency Business Account , in which banks will soon offer $40,000 loans guaranteed by the government to qualifying businesses . The loans will be interest-free for the first year , and the first $10,000 of that amount will be forgivable for businesses that meet certain conditions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trudeau also said he will continue to stay in self-isolation , per recommendation from doctors , though today marks his fourteenth day in self-isolation. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; -Ottawa Public Health is reporting 75 confirmed cases of COVID-19 as of Friday morning , &lt;strong&gt;including the first confirmed case in an Ottawa retirement home&lt;/strong&gt; &amp;ndash ; the Promenade retirement residence in Orl&amp;eacute ; ans . There were 17 people in hospital with COVID-17 in Ottawa on Friday . Seven of those patients were in intensive care , public health officials said . The numbers showed an unexpected pattern , with almost all of those in intensive care being in their 50s and 60s and only one individual in their 70s , said Dr. Vera Etches , Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s medical officer of health . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt; " These are not your grandparents . They are you parents and co-workers . NewsNationalWorldGlavin : COVID-19 is n't just challenging health care ; it 's challenging liberal democracy toohttp : **37;10988;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;11027;TOOLONG ... title= " A large monitor shows an image of Chinese president Xi Jinping wearing a mask in the nearly empty Beijing Station on March 13 , 2020 in Beijing , China . " alt= " A large monitor shows an image of Chinese president Xi Jinping wearing a mask in the nearly empty Beijing Station on March 13 , 2020 in Beijing , China . " data-portal-copyright= " Kevin Frayer " **28;11063;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1583445 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Everybody knows that pestilences have a way of recurring in the world ; yet somehow we find it hard to believe in ones that crash down on our heads from a blue sky . &amp;rdquo ; &amp;nbsp ; That&amp;rsquo ; s from Albert Camus&amp;rsquo ; classic 1947 novel &lt;em&gt;The Plague&lt;/em&gt; , an allegory of fascism that eerily describes our current circumstances , up against that deadly microscopic beast that first began devouring people in China&amp;rsquo ; s Wuhan province last November . &amp;ldquo ; There have been as many plagues as wars in history ; yet always plagues and wars take people equally by surprise . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The thing is , there isn&amp;rsquo ; t much in the way of a helpful real-world precedent to draw from , now that we&amp;rsquo ; re all self-isolating and social-distancing , and movie theatres are closing along with the bars and restaurants , and the schools , and the hockey rinks , and almost all the important places where public life ordinarily unfolds . The disruption of global supply chains ; the near-total shutdown of international travel as Europe&amp;rsquo ; s airports are closing and the daily stream of nearly 200,000 people crossing the Canada-U.S. border dwindling to a trickle ; and the radical contraction of the world&amp;rsquo ; s primary economies . All bolts from the blue . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sept. 11 , 2001 was similarly one of those events that &amp;ldquo ; crash down on our heads from a blue sky , &amp;rdquo ; and 10 years later , beginning in Tunisia in January , 2011 , the Arab Spring took everyone equally by surprise , in the same way that the uprisings in Poland and Hungary did in 1989 , when the Berlin Wall was pulled down , and the world was changed utterly . You&amp;rsquo ; d probably have to reach back to the War Measures restrictions of the 1940s to find the closest comparison with the draconian measures adopted across Europe and North America to contain the rising pace and spreading scale of the COVID-19 sickness that has suddenly afflicted more than 200,000 people worldwide , killing more than 8,000. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Where this is all going , and what sort of world we&amp;rsquo ; ll soon be living in , is still very much up to the people of the world&amp;rsquo ; s liberal democracies to decide , or at least to influence . The G7 countries , Canada included , are facing the necessity of economic intervention at a scale unknown in peacetime , just to tide us all through our travails with landlords , grocery bills and the basics . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But we&amp;rsquo ; re just coming off 14 years of democracy&amp;rsquo ; s worldwide retreat . Part and parcel of all that is the impunity afforded Russian President Vladimir Putin&amp;rsquo ; s expansionist gangster state , the NATO capitals&amp;rsquo ; unconscionable indifference to the crucifixion and dismemberment of Syria&amp;rsquo ; s Sunni Arab majority , and the savage hegemony asserted by Iran&amp;rsquo ; s Khomeinist regime throughout the Middle East . But most notable in the prospects for democracy&amp;rsquo ; s survival is Chinese strongman Xi Jinping&amp;rsquo ; s determination to replace the 70-year-old liberal world order entirely with a world remade in the image and likeness of the Chinese Communist Party&amp;rsquo ; s vast military-industrial surveillance state , with its sundry satrapies , its compliant client regimes , its globe-spanning subsidiaries , and its corporate compradors , embedded so noticeably in Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;11093;TOOLONG ... title= " Director General of the World Health Organization ( WHO ) Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus : In China 's thrall ? " alt= " Director General of the World Health Organization ( WHO ) Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus : In China 's thrall ? " data-portal-copyright= " Denis Balibouse " **28;11129;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1583442 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; At the United Nations , China is increasingly asserting its veto powers at the Security Council . Already , China controls four of the UN&amp;rsquo ; s 15 major agencies : the International Telecommunication Union ; the Food and Agriculture Organization ; the Industrial Development Organization ; and the International Civil Aviation Organization . Only last week , China attempted to take over the World Intellectual Property Organization &amp;ndash ; a move too brazen , as it turned out , even for the dictator-dominated UN General Assembly . In 2016 , China took over the International Criminal Police Organization ( Interpol ) , but that project ended badly . Two years ago , Beijing disappeared Interpol boss Meng Hongwei , who was later charged with taking bribes during his tenure as China&amp;rsquo ; s vice-minister of public security . Meng was sentenced in January to a prison term of 13-and-a-half years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But it&amp;rsquo ; s the World Health Organization that&amp;rsquo ; s the trouble these days . Its credibility is shot , and not only because it&amp;rsquo ; s run by Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus , a former minister of the human-rights-abusing Ethiopian government , one of Beijing&amp;rsquo ; s biggest friends on the African continent . Adhanom distinguished himself shortly after taking over the WHO in 2017 by appointing doddering Zimbabwean tyrant Robert Mugabe as a WHO &amp;ldquo ; goodwill ambassador . &amp;rdquo ; The appointment caused such an uproar it had to be immediately rescinded. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Worse is that Adhanom has gone out of his way to flatter China , and in the early goings of the coronavirus outbreak in Wuhan &amp;ndash ; while Chinese medical officials were being silenced , whistleblowers were being rounded up and locked away and information about the outbreak was being suppressed by Communist Party officials who didn&amp;rsquo ; t want Xi Jinping to suffer any international embarrassment &amp;ndash ; &amp;nbsp ; Adhanom went so far as to praise China&amp;rsquo ; s &amp;ldquo ; transparency . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3&gt;STAY INFORMED : &lt;a href= " http : **34;11159;TOOLONG ... HERE TO SUBSCRIBE TO OUR NEW DAILY COVID-19 NEWSLETTER FOR ALL THE LATEST **28;11195;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; For weeks , China kept lying about the coronavirus . Last week , the South China Morning Post , based tenuously in Hong Kong , reported that Chinese officials were aware of 381 cases by the end of December , but by Jan. 11 officials were claiming only 41 cases of infection had been confirmed . China&amp;rsquo ; s senior public Information officials , and its ambassadors , have lately adopted a strategy of circulating a bizarre conspiracy theory that the coronavirus was developed in some sort of American biological weapons laboratory . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Tuesday , reporters with the Wall Street Journal , the Washington Post , the New York Times , Voice of America and Time magazine were informed that they were being deported from China , and barred from Macao and Hong Kong as well . Beijing&amp;rsquo ; s army of censors is working overtime . Citizens who post uncharitable things about the Communist Party on Weibo and WeChat are being arrested . The party has just published a book of writings praising Xi for his handling of the outbreak , and the WHO&amp;rsquo ; s Bruce Aylward , a Canadian , has been lavishing the regime with compliments about its &amp;ldquo ; stunning&amp;rdquo ; and &amp;ldquo ; impressive&amp;rdquo ; and &amp;ldquo ; extraordinary&amp;rdquo ; handling of the crisis . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; From the outset , WHO officials insisted that Beijing was right , and that travel restrictions in response to the crisis were somehow a very , very bad idea . This resulted in Patty Hajdu , Canada&amp;rsquo ; s federal health minister , trying to explain why this was so , as recently as last week . She had a point . Travellers might &amp;ldquo ; become less honest about where they&amp;rsquo ; ve come from . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But here&amp;rsquo ; s another point . As of Tuesday this week , 74 per cent of Canadian cases of the sickness are travellers , and six per cent are people who came in contact with travellers . Another point : The light-touch airport screening that Canada had put in place , which Hajdu was defending , had already been proved ineffective by the SARS outbreak back in 2003 . Another point : By Monday , Canada had sensibly joined more than 70 UN member states in imposing fairly draconian travel restrictions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So we can&amp;rsquo ; t rely for leadership on the World Health Organization , or any other international agency beholden even indirectly to Beijing . As we all peer into the fog ahead , we have little to guide us beyond our own common sense , and the duty of taking care of one another as best we can , washing our hands frequently , not touching our faces so much , avoiding gatherings of 50 people or more , and hunkering down . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **29;11225;TOOLONG Glavin&lt;/strong&gt; is an author and journalist . &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;11256;TOOLONG ... " data-portal-copyright= " Courtesy of Jocy Medina " **28;11482;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1582181 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thousands of Canadians are stranded in Morocco , many uncertain as to when and how they&amp;rsquo ; ll get home . Flights out of the North African kingdom have slowed to a trickle after the government there halted international travel in and out of the country due to the novel coronavirus outbreak . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to an email from Global Affairs Canada , there are currently more than 4,468 Canadians in Morocco who have registered with the voluntary Registration of Canadians Abroad service . &amp;ldquo ; Limited flights are currently still available , &amp;rdquo ; the federal department insists , &amp;ldquo ; and may be offered on some airlines later this week . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We are actively doing all we can to assist Canadians currently in the country , &amp;rdquo ; it added , cold comfort to those looking for help and unable to find any . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Canadian embassy in Morocco , meanwhile , tweeted that all international flights will be suspended by the Moroccan government on Thursday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s Grace Welch , that means her holiday now largely consists of staying in her hotel in Marrakech , on her laptop , trying to find a flight out . She has an EasyJet flight to London booked for Friday , but she suspects that may now be cancelled , leaving her with nowhere to go or stay . In the meantime , restaurants and caf&amp;eacute ; s in Morocco have closed , although hotel food service and supermarkets remain open . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;11512;TOOLONG ... title= " Ottawa residents Auday Edan and Jocy Medina , who are trying to get out of Morocco . Later that day , she says , the tour company with whom she booked her trip told her and her fellow travellers they were cancelling the tour , and that , following breakfast on Monday , Welch and two other couples on the same tour were on their own . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re trying to get the best information we can , &amp;rdquo ; said Welch , &amp;ldquo ; but we really don&amp;rsquo ; t know what&amp;rsquo ; s going on . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re being treated very well , and we love Morocco , &amp;rdquo ; she added , &amp;ldquo ; but this is not how we expected our trip to end . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Stittsville resident Cari Souter , meanwhile , is in Morocco with her husband and two daughters . &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; ve been trying for days to book a ticket home for the four of us , &amp;rdquo ; she wrote in an email Monday night . &amp;ldquo ; I had a lead on a ticket that routed through Paris , but the BBC is reporting that France is locking down its border starting today . I&amp;rsquo ; m so scared that we&amp;rsquo ; re going to have to ride out the pandemic in a tiny hotel room in Morocco . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Send planes , &amp;rdquo ; she added . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;11578;TOOLONG ... title= " Ottawan Grace Welch in her hotel room in Marrakech , Morocco , trying to find a flight out of the country . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;11614;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1582349 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another Ottawan in Morocco described his dire situation via an email : &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m sitting in Riad surrounded by crowded Souks in a Marrakech M&amp;eacute ; dina , &amp;rdquo ; wrote James Moore , 80. &amp;ldquo ; Because restaurants are closed , my wife and I have little access to food and as an insulin-dependant diabetic , supplies are running low ! We have spent most of the time at the airport trying , along with thousands of others , to get a seat on a plane via Ankara , Copenhagen , London or anywhere there is a connection to Canada . We had seats on an Air Canada flight out of Casablanca but this has been cancelled . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; On the bright side , &amp;rdquo ; he added , &amp;ldquo ; the sun is shining and I&amp;rsquo ; m not digging snow ! &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two other Ottawans , meanwhile , Jocy Medina and her boyfriend , Auday Edan , are in Essaoira , between Marrakech and Casablanca , ready in case a repatriation flight becomes available from either airport . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Medina describes being &amp;ldquo ; in self-isolation , terrified at the option of having to fly to Europe where the epidemic is at its worst . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The Prime Minister ( is ) telling us to come back home and all the flights back home are CANCELLED , &amp;rdquo ; she wrote over Facebook Messenger . &amp;ldquo ; I have been trying to get through to foreign affairs and the Embassy here for a flight , for help . All I get are not useful messages encouraging us to travel through the epicentre of the epidemic ( Europe ) to get home , which puts millions of Canadians at risk . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Like many stranded in Morocco , Medina wants the Canadian government to arrange a flight to get its citizens out &amp;ldquo ; while the numbers are low and it is safe to do so . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In lieu of getting much in the way of information or assistance from officials , many stranded Canadian travellers are communicating and staying in touch through social media . A WhatsApp group of Canadians in Morocco has about 150 members , while a Canadians in Morocco Facebook page has close to 300. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s connecting Canadians throughout Morocco , &amp;rdquo ; said New Brunswick resident Kaitlin MacDonald , who , stuck in Casablanca with two friends , says she started the Facebook group after an Air Canada agent at the Casablanca airport told her Tuesday morning that the airline couldn&amp;rsquo ; t help . &amp;ldquo ; Giving them updates when we hear any information that has been validated , allowing them to discuss potential flight options , air their frustrations , etc. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Canadian government urges Canadians in Morocco who need emergency consular assistance to call the Embassy of Canada to Morocco at 212 ( 0 ) 537 54 49 49 or email &lt;a href= " **32;11644;TOOLONG " **39;11678;TOOLONG , or contact the 24/7 Emergency Watch and Response Centre in Ottawa at +1 613-996-8885 or &lt;a href= " **30;11719;TOOLONG " **37;11751;TOOLONG . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **30;11790;TOOLONG " **37;11822;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;11861;TOOLONG ... NewsWorldCohen : Trump does n't have the U.S. presidential election locked uphttp : **38;12013;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;12053;TOOLONG ... title= " Supporters cheer as U.S. Democratic 2020 presidential candidate Sen. " data-portal-copyright= " JASON REDMOND " **28;12089;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1562492 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; WASHINGTON &amp;ndash ; In the way things go in the United States , February brings a new narrative : Donald Trump is going to win re-election in November . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This has been the media line in Washington for the last three weeks . In the news ecosystem it is conventional wisdom , at least for now . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The argument is that the Democrats are disorganized and divided , unable to run a caucus and unlikely to settle on their nominee until the summer &amp;ndash ; after an extended fight that will weaken them in the autumn . The Republicans , for their part , are united and focused . Trump&amp;rsquo ; s popularity , post-impeachment , is rising . The economy is humming . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The argument for Trump&amp;rsquo ; s re-election is persuasive . He continues to have a mystical hold on his party , which is the reason Gallup reported his popularity in one poll at 49 per cent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He has survived impeachment . The na&amp;iuml ; ve Sen. Susan Collins notwithstanding , no one can say he is chastened . Rather than repent , he repeats . If that means weaponizing the Justice Department to go after his enemies ( Joe Biden , James Comey ) or protect his friends ( Roger Stone , Mike Flynn ) , he will . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His theatrical State of the Union address played well , say his boosters . They loved seeing Melania drape the Presidential Medal of Freedom around Rush Limbaugh. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Most of all , the economy remains strong , and Americans are feeling good . The much-anticipated recession is nowhere in sight . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Incumbency has huge advantages , which is why presidents usually ( but not always ) win a second term . It means a president can spend the first half of this year being president , raising money . Trump is expected to have more than $1 billion U.S. to spend . And the Republicans are refining their voter-turnout operation and social media , which haven&amp;rsquo ; t been strengths in the past . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Then there are the Democrats . They couldn&amp;rsquo ; t organize Iowa , a systemic failure worse than imagined . The same fears have been raised about the caucuses later this month in Nevada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The number of candidates still in the running suggests a long race , perhaps one ending in a brokered convention . Or a race won by Bernie Sanders , who Republicans and Democrats alike consider unelectable. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;12119;TOOLONG ... title= " Michael Bloomberg speaks during an event to open a campaign office at Eastern Market in Detroit , Michigan . " alt= " Michael Bloomberg speaks during an event to open a campaign office at Eastern Market in Detroit , Michigan . " data-portal-copyright= " JEFF KOWALSKY " **28;12155;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1562456 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; So , if you love Trump , all of this gives you something to cheer . Forget that much is based on inconclusive evidence , premature judgment and wishful thinking . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Begin with Trump&amp;rsquo ; s popularity . To believe analyst Nate Silver&amp;rsquo ; s numbers , that Gallup poll was an outlier . Trump&amp;rsquo ; s approval rating is 44 per cent , his disapproval is 52 per cent , remaining within a narrow band . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Then again , Trump was elected in 2016 with just 45.9 per cent of the popular vote , which was enough to win the Electoral College . He talks of expanding the electoral map , but it&amp;rsquo ; s hard to see where . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He will be hard-pressed to hold on to Pennsylvania , Michigan and Wisconsin , which he won by a total of some 69,000 votes . Even if the Democrats lose Michigan , for example , they are a real threat to win Arizona . They won a Senate seat there in 2018 and are likely to do the same this year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yes , the Democrats made a mess of Iowa and Trump made sport of it . No one will remember Iowa in the autumn . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And while the Democratic race is chaotic , it will sort itself out , perhaps as early as Super Tuesday on March 3 . If the decision goes to the convention , the winner is unlikely to be Sanders , who has ceiling of 30 per cent or so . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Moderates will come up with someone , however flawed . If it is Michael Bloomberg , who is surging in national polls , he will finance his own campaign . If Sanders is the nominee , Democrats will panic , but he still beats Trump in hypothetical match-ups . Many said that Trump and Ronald Reagan couldn&amp;rsquo ; t win the presidency , either . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The continuous news cycle likes easy assumptions and big declarations . Anything can happen between now and November , including the coronavirus spreading , causing economic disruption . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Let&amp;rsquo ; s remember that the society of hand-wringers often predict the Democrats will lose , including in 2018 when they regained the House of Representatives . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Journalists are paid to write &amp;ndash ; not to be right . Progressives should take a valium and get back to work . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **30;12185;TOOLONG Cohen&lt;/strong&gt; is a journalist , professor and author of Two Days in June : John F. Kennedy and the 48 Hours That Made History . &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN CITIZEN OPINIONS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;12217;TOOLONG ... The Wet'suwet'en deserve them too&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;12287;TOOLONG ... Canada should learn global lessons on wireless regulation&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Andrew CohenTue , 18 Feb 2020 16:55:31 **45;12322;TOOLONG ... would you do with 20 extra years ? Scientists are close to extending a human 's ' healthy lifespan'http : **31;12369;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;12402;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;12438;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1549927 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Twenty years from now , a 60-year-old might be able to buy a drug that will extend healthy life by a decade or two . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The treatment might be taken over a period of six weeks or so . The end result ? A boost to the immune system and protection against a host of age-related diseases , including heart disease , cancer and dementia . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This kind of intervention wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t be the fountain of youth &amp;mdash ; it won&amp;rsquo ; t turn back the clock and make that 60-year-old a 16-year-old again . It wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t offer eternal life . But it would produce an extended &amp;ldquo ; healthspan&amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; extra years spent free of chronic age-related disease and disability . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At least , this is one version of the near future . Some , like gerobiologist Matt Kaeberlein , believe that an extended healthspan is a likely near-future scenario &amp;mdash ; and policy makers , governments and the medical profession should be prepared for it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It has been done with animals like worms , fruit flies and mice . There have even been some promising early results using a drug called rapamycin in a study involving pet dogs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Whether or not it will actually happen with people is still debatable . But the idea and repercussions around it are real enough to warrant an entire issue of the journal Public Policy &amp;amp ; Aging Report , which asked the question : Is aging a disease ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;12468;TOOLONG ... title= " Chinese 104-year-old twins , Cao Daqiao ( senior , R ) and Cao Xiaoqiao talk at home in Weifang , east China 's Shandong province on November 29 , 2009 . According to the Shanghai Guinness World Records , this twin sisters , who were born in 1905 , were the oldest living twins in the world as of 2009. " alt= " Chinese 104-year-old twins , Cao Daqiao ( senior , R ) and Cao Xiaoqiao talk at home in Weifang , east China 's Shandong province on November 29 , 2009 . According to the Shanghai Guinness World Records , this twin sisters , who were born in 1905 , were the oldest living twins in the world as of 2009. " data-portal-copyright= " STR " **28;12504;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1549939 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kaeberlein , a professor at the University of Washington who studies the biological mechanisms of aging , is asking for nothing less than a paradigm shift in the medical approach to the diseases associated with aging . He thinks it&amp;rsquo ; s reasonable to expect there will be products on the market within the next 20 years that could extend healthy life , although it will take a runway of several decades to prove it works . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This is a much more effective approach toward health than the traditional medical approach of waiting until people are sick and trying to cure their disease . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As it stands , physicians treat individual diseases . The risks for a number of diseases , including many types of cancer , heart disease , diabetes , Alzheimer&amp;rsquo ; s and neurological disorders like Parkinson&amp;rsquo ; s disease , increase with age . The biggest risk factor in heart disease , for example , isn&amp;rsquo ; t smoking , too many cheeseburgers or some other environmental cause . It&amp;rsquo ; s age . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One estimate suggests that curing cancer or heart disease would increase life expectancy for only three or four years and curing both cancer and heart disease would increase life expectancy by less than 10 years because the risk of other kinds of diseases continue to increase exponentially . If aging could be slowed down , then life expectancy could be increased by two or three decades . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Modern medicine has gotten pretty good at keeping sick people alive , but we are not very good at curing age-related disease , &amp;rdquo ; said Kaeberlein. &amp;ldquo ; By treating one disease at a time , we haven&amp;rsquo ; t done anything to prevent all of the other age-related functional declines from happening in that person . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;12534;TOOLONG ... title= " Christian Mortensen , 114-years-old , smokes a cigar at his home in the Aldersly Retirement Community in San Rafael , CA in 1997 . Mortensen was the oldest man alive and celebrated his 115th birthday 16 August , 1997 . AFP PHOTO/JOHN G. MABANGLO " alt= " Christian Mortensen , 114-years-old , smokes a cigar at his home in the Aldersly Retirement Community in San Rafael , CA in 1997 . Mortensen was the oldest man alive and celebrated his 115th birthday 16 August , 1997 . AFP PHOTO/JOHN G. MABANGLO " data-portal-copyright= " JOHN G. MABANGLO " **28;12570;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1549935 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; By holding back aging , it is possible to hold back not just one disease , but a whole bunch of them . Age-related chronic diseases are taking an increasing toll on the world economy . One 2017 analysis estimated that half of the global health burden among adults can be attributed to age-related diseases . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think you can make an argument that the extended period of time many older people live with multiple comorbidities currently is likely a direct result of the traditional medical approach of treating individual diseases , rather than treating biological aging . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Others are skeptical that an effective intervention to modify aging is just around the corner . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I know of no current intervention I would be willing to place a bet on , &amp;rdquo ; said Douglas Gray , a senior s &lt;span id= " mainContentlblContent " &gt;cientist at the Ottawa Hospital Research Institute and professor of biochemistry , microbiology and immunology at the University of Ottawa . " The motivations are genuine and admirable . I just do n't buy it . " &lt;br&gt;&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Researcher Jane Rylett , whose lab at Western University investigates the mechanisms regulating chemical communication in the nervous system , doesn&amp;rsquo ; t believe there is a single silver bullet for aging . What might work is a combination of approaches , she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Never say never . But I don&amp;rsquo ; t see anything on the horizon that would signal something imminent that would allow us to prolong life for , say , 20 years , &amp;rdquo ; said Rylett , who is the scientific director for the Institute of Aging at the Canadian Institutes of Health Research . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Even if there was a drug targeted at aging , it would affect different people in different ways . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are few hurdles to be overcome . One is research funding that recognizes biological aging as a risk factor in a disease . While biological aging accounts for more than 90 per cent of the risk of developing Alzheimer&amp;rsquo ; s , for example , only a fraction of one per cent of the Alzheimer&amp;rsquo ; s research dollars spent in the U.S. is allocated to research on the role of aging in developing Alzheimer&amp;rsquo ; s . To turn the tide on the disease , more resources need to be spent on understanding how aging works , Kaeberlein argues . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;12600;TOOLONG ... title= " In this Aug. 14 , 2018 file photo , Dr. William Burke goes over a PET brain scan at Banner Alzheimers Institute in Phoenix . " alt= " In this Aug. 14 , 2018 file photo , Dr. William Burke goes over a PET brain scan at Banner Alzheimers Institute in Phoenix . " data-portal-copyright= " Matt York " **28;12636;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1549933 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The other major hurdle is regulatory . Drugs approved by the FDA are approved for an &amp;ldquo ; indication&amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; that is , the drug treats a named disease . Most of the promising work on aging so far has been with animals , and not very complex animals at that . Translating these findings to people means clinical trials . Some contend that aging has to be recognized as a disease so it can be targeted by a drug aimed at treating it . It&amp;rsquo ; s worth noting , however , that some drugs have long been approved to prevent diseases . Vaccines are an example . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kaeberlein doesn&amp;rsquo ; t think we should be trying to gain acceptance for the idea that aging is a disease . That&amp;rsquo ; s a semantic argument that distracts from the more important conversation , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; What&amp;rsquo ; s more important is to get people to understand that biological aging is a modifiable process . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For some , the idea that aging is a disease is absurd and potentially dangerous . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The thing that excludes aging from being a disease is its universality , &amp;rdquo ; said Gray . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some animals may live extraordinarily long lives . The naked mole rat , a hairless burrowing rodent native to East Africa which can live more than 30 years &amp;mdash ; at least 10 times the lifespan for a mouse &amp;mdash ; is of great interest to scientists . The hydra , a tiny freshwater polyp , may live virtually forever . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is also great scientific interest in the outer limits of longevity for humans . There have been numerous documented cases of people living over the age of 110 . But all humans age and eventually die . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;12666;TOOLONG ... title= " In this Oct. 28 , 2014 photo , neurosurgeon Dr. John Sampson places a catheter into a glioblastoma patient at Duke in Durham , N.C. The risk of cancer increases with age . " alt= " In this Oct. 28 , 2014 photo , neurosurgeon Dr. John Sampson places a catheter into a glioblastoma patient at Duke in Durham , N.C. The risk of cancer increases with age . " data-portal-copyright= " Shawn Rocco " **28;12702;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1549931 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 1825 , the English actuary Benjamin Gompertz determined that the likelihood of dying doubled every eight years from the age of about 30 . Two hundred years later , even though people live longer , his mathematical model , known as the &amp;ldquo ; Gompertz curve , &amp;rdquo ; still holds . It just distributes death over a longer arc , said Gray . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There is a genuine problem with ageism in our society , and the last thing we need is for older people to be perceived as diseased , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; The very word invokes an entirely inappropriate response &amp;mdash ; at least that is my perception . Perhaps it is because to many people the word brings to mind the Spanish Flu or the Black Death , not a universal , passive , and gradual loss of function . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Geriatrician Dr. Peter Boling agrees . If aging is a disease , simply by staying alive , we are all sick , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While we can and should improve aging by tackling the impact of age-related health problems , aging is inevitable and should be considered a fact of life , not a disease . It&amp;rsquo ; s better to work on preventative measures that will result in healthier people , and better care for those who are sick , said Boling , who is also a professor of medicine at Virginia Commonwealth University . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The presumption is that the quality of life among older individuals is not good , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s an interesting mix of things when we get older . I&amp;rsquo ; m more and more aware of some of the things I can&amp;rsquo ; t do . I have arthritis . I know changes are taking place in my cardiovascular system . But these things are predestined . I want to say I&amp;rsquo ; m glad for the things I have seen and the experiences I have had . Accumulated experience is part of the aging process . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Advocating for more basic research funding to learn about the biological causes of aging is a legitimate scientific objective . It will help us understand what makes us tick and may then lead to better health . But it will likely not ultimately uncover a fountain of youth , Boling argues . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He is cautious about the idea of taking drugs with possible side effects , or editing genes to change the aging process . You might , for example , be able to boost the immune system and increase resistance to illnesses such as influenza and shingles , he said . But there might be downstream effects . Aging is a complex process and humans are complex organisms . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; When you change things in a large system , you don&amp;rsquo ; t know what else may occur , &amp;rdquo ; Boling warns . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;div class= " c-messagekitgutter " &gt; &lt;div class= " c-messagekitgutterright " &gt; &lt;div class= " c-messagekitblocks **28;12732;TOOLONG " &gt; &lt;div class= " c-messagemessageblocks **32;12762;TOOLONG " &gt; &lt;div class= " p-blockkitrenderer " &gt; &lt;div class= " **30;12796;TOOLONG **37;12828;TOOLONG " &g; ... &lt;div class= " p-richtextblock " dir= " auto " &gt; &lt;div class= " p-richtextsection " &gt;Both life expectancy ( the average number of years a person is expected to live ) and health-adjusted life expectancy ( the number of years spent in good health ) increased in Canada over a 20-year period , according to a study released by Statistics Canada in 2018 . Life expectancy at birth for Canadian men was 79.8 years in 2015 , but health-adjusted life expectancy at birth was 69.0 years , said the study , based on responses to health surveys . For Canadian women , life expectancy at birth was 83.9 years , but health-adjusted life expectancy was 70.5 years . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;p&gt; Increasing healthspans could save governments mountains of money . One 2013 study estimated that extending a healthy life by only 2.2 years would save the U.S. $7.1 trillion in health-care costs and increased productivity , for example . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But a world where 80 is the new 60 also brings up a lot of questions . Would treatments that extend life be available to everyone , or would this open a new rift between haves and have-nots ? Would two more decades of life mean postponing retirement and spending two more decades in the workforce ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Obviously , there are several policy and social implications associated with an increase in healthy lifespan of 20 to 30 years . Retirement age and social security would need to be redefined , for example , &amp;rdquo ; said Kaeberlein. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;12867;TOOLONG ... title= " A woman exercises with wooden dumbbells during an event marking Respect for the Aged Day at a temple in the Sugamo district of Tokyo , Japan , on Monday , Sept. 19 , 2016 . The proportion of Japan 's aged has been rising steadily for decades , with U.S. Census Bureau data showing that last year more than one in four Japanese were over 65. " alt= " A woman exercises with wooden dumbbells during an event marking Respect for the Aged Day at a temple in the Sugamo district of Tokyo , Japan , on Monday , Sept. 19 , 2016 . The proportion of Japan 's aged has been rising steadily for decades , with U.S. Census Bureau data showing that last year more than one in four Japanese were over 65. " data-portal-copyright= " Tomohiro Ohsumi " **28;12903;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1549932 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; He is doubtful it would happen all at once , and it will most likely be a gradual application of new discoveries . &amp;ldquo ; There are also questions about access and who will benefit first from such discoveries and how long it will take for them to become widely available &amp;mdash ; which can&amp;rsquo ; t be answered right now . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kaeberlein would like to see Food And Drug Administration approval of an intervention to target biological aging . &amp;ldquo ; This could take many forms . For example , approval of a drug that when given to healthy people acts as a preventative for several age-related diseases or indications . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Researcher Jane Rylett recently returned from a trip to Japan , the world&amp;rsquo ; s first &amp;ldquo ; super-aged&amp;rdquo ; country , defined as one in which more than one in five &lt;span class= " ILfuVd " &gt;&lt;span class= " e24Kjd " &gt;people are 65 or older . **26;12933;TOOLONG Canada will be a super-aged country in 10 to 15 years . The World Health Organization predicts that by 2050 , there will be several dozen countries in that category , she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Let&amp;rsquo ; s say there was something on the horizon . Let&amp;rsquo ; s say there&amp;rsquo ; s a great discovery , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; There are a lot of health policy questions that would have to be answered . What does it mean for society if people live longer ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; jlaucius@postmedia.com &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;12961;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;13003;TOOLONG ... this rare disease could help treat aspects of **26;13038;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;13066;TOOLONG ... can hold back the hands of time ? " data-portal-copyright= " STR " **28;13281;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1543240 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Public Health Agency of Canada has updated an advisory for travellers to Wuhan , China , where an outbreak of a mysterious virus has killed two people and sickened dozens . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Chinese New Year begins on Jan. 25 , and the number of travellers is expected to increase . The agency said risk to Canadians visiting Wuhan is low but that precautions should be taken . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At Canadian border points , planned additional measures to be implemented over the coming week include messaging on arrivals screens at the Toronto , Montreal and Vancouver international airports , reminding travellers from Wuhan to inform a border service officer if they are experiencing flu-like symptoms and an additional health screening question to be added to electronic kiosks , said the public health agency in a statement &lt;/p&gt; &lt;div class= " zn-bodyparagraph speakable " &gt;So far this month , two men in their 60s have died of respiratory failure in Wuhan in the outbreak of a pneumonia-like illness . The illness is a new strain of coronavirus , which is in the same family as severe acute respiratory syndrome ( SARS ) . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;Most cases have been linked to the Huanan Seafood Market ( also known as Wuhan South China Seafood City and South China Seafood Wholesale Market ) , which was closed as of Jan. 1 for cleaning and disinfection . However , some cases have not been linked to this market and the source of the virus remains unknown , according to the Public Health Agency of Canada . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt; &lt;p&gt;Screening measures have been implemented at ports of entry in some neighbouring countries for passengers arriving from Wuhan . There have also been reports of people infected with coronavirus outside of China who had recently travelled to Wuhan . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;p&gt; The health agency&amp;rsquo ; s notice to the Canadian public remains at a Level 1 , which advises practising usual travel health precautions , such as routine vaccinations and reminders about the importance of hand washing . Travellers to Wuhan are advised to take precautions against respiratory and other illnesses while travelling and to seek medical attention if they become sick . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;13311;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;13353;TOOLONG ... says 17 new cases in virus outbreak , Wuhan to restrict large **31;13388;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; Travellers are also cautioned to avoid high-risk areas such as farms , live animal markets , and areas where animals may be slaughtered ; avoid contact with animals , alive or dead , including pigs , chickens , ducks and wild birds ; to avoid surfaces with animal droppings or secretions on them and to avoid contact with sick people , especially if they have fever , cough , or difficulty breathing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Spending time in large crowds or crowded areas can increase your risk of getting sick , &amp;rdquo ; according to a travel health notice from the agency , the federal body responsible for public health , emergency preparedness and response , and infectious and chronic disease control and prevention . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a statement sent in response to questions from this newspaper , the agency said the risk to Canadians has been assessed as low because Canada has no direct flights from Wuhan and the volume of travellers arriving indirectly from Wuhan is low . Canada also has a number of standard border measures in place to prevent the introduction and spread of communicable diseases into Canada , said the statement . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When a traveller shows signs and symptoms of an infectious disease upon arrival , Canada Border Services Agency officers or airport and airline staff can contact a quarantine officer 24 hours a day , seven days a week . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Typically , a border services officer , designated as a screening officer under the Quarantine Act , is the first point of contact and will conduct a preliminary screening of the traveller based on criteria &amp;mdash ; such as fever or signs of fever , coughing , difficulty breathing , rash and other symptoms &amp;mdash ; developed by the Public Health Agency of Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The officer then performs a more detailed assessment by asking additional questions regarding the traveller&amp;rsquo ; s symptoms and confirming information about the traveller&amp;rsquo ; s country of origin and possible exposure to an infectious disease , said the Public Health Agency&amp;rsquo ; s statement . If there is a potential public health risk , the quarantine officer may order the traveller taken to hospital or to report to the local public health authority . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Entry screening alone is not a guarantee against the possible importation of this new virus but is an important public health tool during periods of uncertainty and part of a multilayered government response strategy , &amp;rdquo ; said the statement . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;13421;TOOLONG ... police charge man , 26 , with gun , drug crimes in ByWard Market patrol&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;13456;TOOLONG ... becoming part of a family : ' 74 become Canadian citizens at hockey rink ceremony&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;13491;TOOLONG ... brave winter 's wrath for skateway opening&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; Joanne LauciusSun , 19 Jan 2020 22:29:06 **45;13526;TOOLONG ... " data-portal-copyright= " Leah Millis " **28;13645;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1526909 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; No matter how much he denies the process and demeans his prosecutors , Donald J. Trump knows that things changed forever for him at 8:24 p.m. on Dec. 18 , 2019 , when the United States House of Representatives approved the first article of impeachment against him . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In that imperishable moment , legislators of the world&amp;rsquo ; s most powerful democracy invoked a constitutional instrument for the third time in the country&amp;rsquo ; s history . Of the Republic&amp;rsquo ; s 45 presidents &amp;ndash ; some of them truly incompetent or malevolent &amp;ndash ; only three have been impeached . Donald Trump now joins Andrew Johnson , a racist , and Bill Clinton , a perjurer. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;13675;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;13717;TOOLONG ... House impeaches Donald Trump for abuse of **26;13752;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;13780;TOOLONG ... president is impeached ' : U.S. House deals Donald Trump a historic **27;13815;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;13844;TOOLONG ... campaign rally , embattled Trump gives withering review of impeachment **25;13879;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;13906;TOOLONG ... House says it is confident U.S. Senate will exonerate Trump after **32;13941;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; What changes immediately for Trump today is more personal than political . It is not that he will be forced from office , which is the faint hope of his accusers , who find him guilty of &amp;ldquo ; high crimes and misdemeanors. &amp;rdquo ; He will be acquitted by Republicans in the Senate as surely he was indicted by Democrats in the House . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And if he is re-elected in 2020 , which is possible , the impact of impeachment will be deemed even less important . It will be cast as a purely partisan concoction , all theatre and no substance . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; With no prospect of being removed from office , then , why should Trump worry about impeachment ? With three such proceedings in the last four decades , impeachment has been diluted . It has become &amp;ldquo ; normalized , &amp;rdquo ; almost as routine as a president who swears in public and pressures foreign leaders for favours . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hasn&amp;rsquo ; t the country&amp;rsquo ; s new hyper-partisanship so drained impeachment of its power that we hardly recognize it anymore ? Like Christmas , we long for the impeachments that we used to know , in which presidents resigned ( Nixon ) or apologized ( Clinton ) . If it is really no more than an antiquated , illegitimate instrument of revenge today , impeachment becomes a slap on the wrist for Trump , a constitutional traffic ticket . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some even say that Trump hoped to be impeached so that he could use it to raise money for his re-election , rally his conservative base and crush his enemies , who seem to be everywhere today . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;13975;TOOLONG ... title= " U.S. Speaker of the House Nancy Pelosi ( D-CA ) wields the gavel as the U.S. House of Representatives votes in favour of two counts of impeachment against U.S. President Donald Trump on charges of abuse of power and obstruction of Congress . " data-portal-copyright= " JONATHAN ERNST " **28;14011;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1526907 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; That&amp;rsquo ; s ridiculous . Everything we know about Trump suggests that impeachment is devastating for him . It&amp;rsquo ; s a genuine humiliation to a man without shame who has not known humiliation or reversal in his privileged life . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For Trump , impeachment is worse than electoral defeat . It is not a footnote or an asterisk ; it&amp;rsquo ; s a scarlet letter . It is the first line in his obituary and a chapter in his biography . Impeachment becomes your new full name , as in &amp;ldquo ; the impeached Donald Trump . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For a clinical narcissist whose ego is as fragile as porcelain , this has to cut deeply . If it is true that impeachment doesn&amp;rsquo ; t really matter anymore because it&amp;rsquo ; s all politics , then Trump would not have been as aggrieved as he has been in recent weeks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While the White House insisted that he was &amp;ldquo ; working&amp;rdquo ; as the debate unfolded in the House on Wednesday , he tweeted more than 50 times . Unlike Clinton , who tried to act as president under pressure , Trump could not even pretend to ignore what was happening . Not when you are watching the hearings and railing about them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Or holding a political rally , as he did in Michigan on Wednesday evening while the House was debating his fate . He spoke so long at the event that many loyalists left , or worse , fell asleep . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The most damning evidence of how impeachment is eating at Trump is his letter to House Speaker Nancy Pelosi earlier this week . Even by the standards of Trump&amp;rsquo ; s usual protests , this was one long scream . Think Edvard Munch . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The tone was unhinged . The punctuation favoured exclamation marks and all-caps . The letter hurled insults , made outrageous historical claims , furnished its own facts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trump now uses the first-person plural when discussing impeachment , as in &amp;ldquo ; they impeached us. &amp;rdquo ; Ultimately , though , it is not about us but about him . Impeachment may not remove him from office , but it will threaten his vanity like nothing has before . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Blame injustice , burn down the house , scorn your enemies , gather your friends . " data-portal-copyright= " Thomas Peter " **28;14293;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1513425 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Five months into a sustained and unprecedented mass insurrection that Xi Jinping&amp;rsquo ; s puppet administration and its barbaric police force have failed to suppress , Hongkongers returned a sweep of democrats in last Sunday&amp;rsquo ; s local district elections . So much for Beijing&amp;rsquo ; s confident prediction that a &amp;ldquo ; silent majority&amp;rdquo ; would turn its back on Hong Kong&amp;rsquo ; s largely leaderless uprising . So much for the propaganda about the &amp;ldquo ; black hand&amp;rdquo ; of foreign troublemakers and plotters behind the unrest . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Roughly nine of every 10 seats in the race for 452 district council posts went to anti-regime candidates . The democrats now control 17 of the semi-autonomous city&amp;rsquo ; s 18 districts . Beijing is persisting in its claims that &amp;ldquo ; rioters&amp;rdquo ; conspired with foreign elements to bully the establishment slate , and whatever lies Xi Jinping&amp;rsquo ; s propagandists are telling now , they appear to have genuinely believed their own lies leading up to the vote . And that&amp;rsquo ; s something to worry about . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; &lt;a href= " https : **33;14323;TOOLONG ... is a heady drug , and Beijing got high on its own supply&lt;/a&gt; , &amp;rdquo ; writes James Palmer , senior editor for Foreign Policy magazine . And Beijing was supplied lavishly by its own influence-peddling and strong-arm channels in Hong Kong . This allows us all a modicum of amusing **34;14358;TOOLONG at the expense of the Chinese Communist Party , and we should enjoy it while we can . But Palmer , who has written extensively about Chinese history , and spent several years as a journalist in Beijing , counsels caution . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The problem is that under the increasingly paranoid regime of Xi Jinping , even these internal reports have become much more geared toward what the leadership wants to hear . Reporting on a failed program can be painted as a sign of disloyalty , &amp;rdquo ; Palmer writes . As a consequence , Beijing&amp;rsquo ; s propagandists &amp;ldquo ; appear to have sincerely believed that the establishment parties would win an overwhelming victory . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This degree of self-delusion is a frightening prospect for Hong Kong&amp;rsquo ; s future , and for governance in China generally , Palmer argues . He makes a persuasive case . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A similar dynamic appears to have become deeply entrenched within the rapidly degenerating Khomeinist regime in Tehran , where a similarly &amp;ldquo ; leaderless&amp;rdquo ; revolt is being met by state violence and extreme repression . At least 143 protesters have been killed by security forces in recent days . Internet connectivity has been almost completely shut down , and regime propaganda is at fever pitch : it&amp;rsquo ; s all the work of Zionist saboteurs and shadowy American spy networks . The usual culprits . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;14394;TOOLONG ... title= " Tehran residents pass by a burnt bank in the Iranian capital on Nov. 20 , following riots earlier this month . " alt= " Tehran residents pass by a burnt bank in the Iranian capital on Nov. 20 , following riots earlier this month . " data-portal-copyright= " Wana News Agency " **28;14430;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1513430 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Propaganda is a heady drug , all right , and you don&amp;rsquo ; t have to be a committed Chinese Communist functionary in Shenzhen or a conscript with one of the Islamic Revolutionary Guards Corps&amp;rsquo ; s baseeji squads in Tehran to succumb to a genuine belief in the most lurid fictions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Here in Canada , the proposition that the uprising that dislodged Bolivia&amp;rsquo ; s Evo Morales was a U.S.-backed military coup is commonplace among senior New Democrats . The idea that the agonies long endured by Venezuelans in the grip of Nicol&amp;aacute ; s Maduro&amp;rsquo ; s police state are attributable solely to American sanctions is pretty well a prerequisite for inclusion in British Labour leader Jeremy Corbyn&amp;rsquo ; s inner circle . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There&amp;rsquo ; s no great harm in finding amusement in the Boomer habit of retreating into the comfort of counterculture narratives to explain away the tumults that are underway in the world at the moment , but **34;14460;TOOLONG will only get you so far . Mass uprisings around the world have reached the point that Erica Chenoweth at Harvard&amp;rsquo ; s Kennedy school and a team of allied academics and researchers are making the case that we may be living through &amp;ldquo ; &lt;a href= " https : **33;14496;TOOLONG ... largest wave of non-violent mass movements in history&lt;/a&gt; . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For the first decade of this century , it was difficult enough to dislodge the fashionably highbrow notion that the Taliban , for instance , was just some sort of theocratic , latter-day Afghan Vietcong . It&amp;rsquo ; s still acceptable in certain academic circles to imagine Hezbollah , or Hamas , as just awkwardly nasty outliers of an otherwise progressive and virtuous circle of anti-imperialism. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But we&amp;rsquo ; re now closing in on the final weeks of the century&amp;rsquo ; s second decade , and as the University of Gothenburg&amp;rsquo ; s &lt;a href= " https : **33;14531;TOOLONG ... L ? rhmann and Staffan Lindberg&lt;/a&gt; point out , for the first time since the Second World War the number of United Nations&amp;rsquo ; member states turning away from democratic norms is outpacing the number of countries moving towards democracy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Arab Spring erupted eight years ago . Those uprisings were almost all brutally crushed , or hijacked by Islamists . In Syria , a non-violent democratic revolt has been met with a degree of state savagery that produced the largest global refugee exodus in decades , mass death by barrel bomb , the rise of the Islamic state and genocide . Tunisia remains a glimmer of hope . Elsewhere , the Arab Spring democrats were mostly forced underground . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For all the propaganda about U.S. imperialism , Zionist warmongers , shadowy agents of the philanthropist George Soros and so on , the mass uprisings in flower around the world are almost entirely leaderless , organic and a response to real-world conditions of poverty , dispossession and repression . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The old Cold War paradigms don&amp;rsquo ; t work anymore . From Ankara to Moscow and from Shanghai to Tehran , the &amp;eacute ; lites are not hunkered down behind walls . Their wealth and power is diffuse and globalized . Russian oligarchs are just as likely to be settled comfortably in London as in Saint Petersburg , just as the Chinese princeling caste is ensconced in Vancouver and Sydney , and the Iranian &amp;eacute ; lites have done quite well for themselves in Toronto real estate . The propaganda that keeps them in power follows them wherever they go . They enjoy impunity , wherever they go . And we are all implicated in their continuing hold on power . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are wins and losses . The Euromaidan uprising of the winter of 2013 ushered in an inspiring era of democracy in Ukraine &amp;ndash ; which Vladimir Putin has punished by his invasion of Donbass and annexation of Crimea . Taiwan&amp;rsquo ; s Sunflower rebellion has tilled the seedbed of a vital and flourishing democracy , but that gallant little country is increasingly threatened by Beijing . The Gezi Park protests in Istanbul were inspiring too , but that effort ended up crushed under the weight of Recep Erdogan&amp;rsquo ; s viciously repressive neo-caliphate. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The story is unfolding , and it doesn&amp;rsquo ; t fit any neat propaganda narrative , no matter how comforting , no matter how self-serving . But it is like nothing we&amp;rsquo ; ve seen in decades . Hong Kong may well be its front line . But it is unfolding all over the world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **29;14566;TOOLONG Glavin&lt;/strong&gt; is an author and journalist . &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN CITIZEN OPINIONS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;14597;TOOLONG ... Five good reasons to label this city ' Ottawa the Inept ' &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Terry GlavinWed , 27 Nov 2019 19:58:37 **45;14702;TOOLONG ... and Griffith : Human rights defenders should boycott immigration conference in Beijinghttp : **33;14749;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;14784;TOOLONG ... title= " In this file photo , a Uighur family prays at the grave of a loved one at a local cemetery in western Xinjiang province , China . " alt= " In this file photo , a Uighur family prays at the grave of a loved one at a local cemetery in western Xinjiang province , China . " data-portal-copyright= " Kevin Frayer " **28;14820;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1511777 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The number of international events being hosted by China is on the rise . At first glance , one &lt;a href= " https : **33;14850;TOOLONG ... argue&lt;/a&gt; that global exchange is a mechanism for the West to normalize democratic values and open science . However , the world is increasingly witnessing repressive regimes , such as that in China , rise in influence at the &lt;a href= " https : **33;14885;TOOLONG ... of human rights and democracy&lt;/a&gt; . This then raises the question : Does participating in events hosted by such regimes promote engagement or complicity ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Chinese regime has created a number of quasi-independent groups , such as the Center for China and Globalization ( CCG ) , which bills itself as &amp;ldquo ; China&amp;rsquo ; s leading global non-governmental think tank with more than 10 branches and overseas representatives . &amp;rdquo ; The CCG is in fact part of the &lt;a href= " https : **33;14920;TOOLONG ... Front Work Department&lt;/a&gt; , a branch of the Chinese Communist Party that aims to exert Chinese government influence around the world . Both organizations are key pillars in attracting conferences to China , which means that their events will most likely legitimize the regime on the international stage , rather than curb it through engagement . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One example of how this plays out can be seen through the International Metropolis Conference , which involves government policy makers , academics and non-governmental sector organizations and is set to be held in &lt;a href= " http : //metropolischina.org/ " &gt;Beijing this coming June&lt;/a&gt; . The CCG was a key stakeholder in wooing the conference&amp;rsquo ; s secretariat and bringing it to China . The conference focuses on immigration and refugee issues and is widely known for promoting multiculturalism , diversity and inclusion . It was founded in 1996 with Canadian government funding and with strong links with Immigration , Refugees and Citizenship Canada which hosted last year&amp;rsquo ; s conference . Its secretariat is currently &lt;a href= " https : **25;14955;TOOLONG " &gt;bas ... at Carleton University&lt;/a&gt; . The conference and the &amp;ldquo ; Metropolis&amp;rdquo ; brand are almost synonymous with Canadian immigration . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Holding such a conference in China under the current regime can only legitimize Beijing&amp;rsquo ; s human rights abuses . Both the United Nations and Amnesty International have issued reports warning that China is actively suppressing its ethnic minority populations . Up to one million&amp;nbsp ; Muslim Uighur are being held in &amp;ldquo ; re-education centres , &amp;rdquo ; which are essentially prison camps . It is ironic to hold a conference on refugees in a country that produced them , and doing so is an act of complicity . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Based on past practices of the regime , it is almost certain that Chinese authorities will not permit a free and open exchange of ideas on relevant Chinese policy and practice . Foreign speakers will likely be discouraged from talking about issues that might &amp;ldquo ; offend&amp;rdquo ; the government , or will censor themselves . Chinese participants will be prohibited from doing so . It is also very likely that minders will be present to monitor and intervene in the event of any real or perceived criticism . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some might argue that participating in the conference is a means to change the regime and that all countries have blemishes . Canada , for instance , still wrestles with &lt;a href= " http : **34;14982;TOOLONG ... colonialism&lt;/a&gt; . But , there is a major difference between countries that have entrenched human rights in their legislation and those , such as China , who do not . It is na&amp;iuml ; ve to think that hosting an event in China will change its practices . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For this reason , more than 150 academics and representatives of non-governmental groups from across Canada and 11 countries &lt;a href= " https : **33;15018;TOOLONG ... a petition&lt;/a&gt; against both holding and attending the International Metropolis Conference in Beijing . They recognize that it is not too late to do something about the message Canada and other democratic countries send when they fund and participate in events in China , and that it is not too late for individual Canadians to make a difference . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is time for Canada and other Western countries to recognize they can not assume that policy and academic exchange will change repressive regimes . Rather it could potentially legitimate them or send the signal that the international community is willing to turn a blind eye . For these reasons it is time to rethink when it is appropriate to participate in events held by repressive regimes . Failing to do so risks compromising Canadian and international human rights values . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **30;15053;TOOLONG Ramos&lt;/strong&gt; is Professor of Sociology at Dalhousie University . &lt;strong&gt;Andrew Griffith&lt;/strong&gt; is a fellow of the Canadian Global Affairs Institute . &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN OPINION&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;15085;TOOLONG ... " &lt;a href= " https : //twitter.com/IlwadElman ? refsrc= ... @IlwadElman&lt;/a&gt; ' s official statement on behalf of the Elman &amp; Hersi Families regarding the death of her sister &lt;a href= " https : **32;15433;TOOLONG ? ... who was killed in &lt;a href= " https : **31;15467;TOOLONG ? s ... on 20.11.2019 &lt;a href= " https : //t.co/yV5y2YxQFw " &gt;pic.twitter ... -- ELMAN PEACE &amp; HRC ( @ElmanPeaceHRC ) &lt;a href= " https : **33;15500;TOOLONG ... 23 , **33;15535;TOOLONG &lt;script async src= " https : **33;15570;TOOLONG " charset= " utf-8 " &gt;&lt;/script&gt; &lt;p&gt; Almas Elman is the second prominent Somali-Canadian to be killed this year . In July , Hodan Nalayeh , a journalist who immigrated to Canada as a child during the Somali civil war and grew up near Toronto , was among 27 people killed in a suicide bomb attack in the southern city of Kismayo. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Both had moved to Canada as children , part of a wave of more than 55,000 Somali refugees who arrived in the 1980s and 1990s , fleeing the civil war that engulfed the country at the time and its brutal aftermath . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Almas and Ilwad Elman are the daughters of Elman Ali Ahmed , a prominent human rights advocate who was assassinated in Mogadishu in 1996 . In 1999 , his widow , Fartuun Adan , came to Canada with her four daughters , raising them in Ottawa . Fartuun returned to Somalia in 2007 to continue her husband&amp;rsquo ; s work , founding the Elman Peace and Human Rights Centre in his memory . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;15605;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;15647;TOOLONG ... activist part of wave of returnees eager to help **28;15682;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; The daughters followed her back to Somalia , with Almas and Ilwad working with the organization . &lt;a href= " https : **33;15712;TOOLONG ... was nominated for the 2019 Nobel Peace Prize&lt;/a&gt; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In her message , Ilwad said the family had been moved &amp;ldquo ; by the incredible outpouring of support from all over the world , &amp;rdquo ; since the killing of her sister , noting that &amp;ldquo ; our family has been visited by this form of violent tragedy before . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;15747;TOOLONG ... title= " Ilwad Elman. " alt= " Ilwad Elman. " data-portal-copyright= " Earl Gibson III " **28;15783;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1511229 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This has compounded our grief and heartache , but it has not broken our resolve to build peace that saves other families from knowing the grief we are feeling right now . We remain unwavering in our commitment to working towards a Somalia where people can live without the fear of this happening to them or their loved ones . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Almas Elman is survived by her mother , Fartuun , sisters Ilwad , Iman and Gesia , and her husband , Zakaria Hersi. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; bcrawford@postmedia.com &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Twitter.com/getBAC &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;15813;TOOLONG ... family no stranger to art of playing politics as it tries to cope with Prince Andrew sex scandal&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;15848;TOOLONG ...
