NewsWorldA dog 's life : New study to investigate aging process in man 's best friendhttp : **34;15965;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;16001;TOOLONG ... title= " Good dogs . " alt= " Good dogs . " data-portal-copyright= " ipet photo " **28;16037;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1507525 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; AUSTIN , Texas &amp;mdash ; A team of researchers is hoping old dogs can teach aging science new tricks . The scientists , with the backing of the U.S National Institute on Aging , have launched an ambitious project in which they want dog owners to enrol canines in a study of aging in man&amp;rsquo ; s best friend , hoping it will help both dogs and humans live longer and better lives . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The &amp;ldquo ; citizen scientists&amp;rdquo ; will answer dozens of questions about their pooches over the lifetime of the animals , such as how much and how often they exercise , what they eat and how much , and their interactions with people or other pets in the household . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; These dogs will be doing what they do normally , &amp;rdquo ; said Daniel Promislow , co-director of the Dog Aging Project . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dog owners are very well-attuned to changes in their dog&amp;rsquo ; s behaviour , which makes their observations valuable to science . &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s the power of paying attention , &amp;rdquo ; Promislow said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As part of the study , a small number of the dogs will provide DNA , blood and urine samples . A select group of middle-aged canines will also be enrolled in a double-blind , placebo-controlled study of a drug that has already been shown to increase the lifespan of mice in the hope of determining whether it will have the same effect on dogs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;16067;TOOLONG ... title= " A 10-year-old chihuahua participates in the Pawlooza 's dog fashion show in London , Ont. , earlier this year . As small dogs , chihuahua would normally have longer live expectancy than larger breeds of dogs such as Great Danes . " alt= " A 10-year-old chihuahua participates in the Pawlooza 's dog fashion show in London , Ont. , earlier this year . As small dogs , chihuahua would normally have longer live expectancy than larger breeds of dogs such as Great Danes . " data-portal-copyright= " Max Martin " **28;16103;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1507194 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the long run , the study , which has $22.8 million U.S. in funding from the National Institute on Aging , will help the scientists understand the factors that affect &amp;ldquo ; healthspan&amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; the proportion of life that is spent in good health . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The project was launched last Thursday with a splashy media conference at the annual scientific meeting of the Gerontological Society of America in Austin . While the official goal is to enrol 10,000 canines , the researchers aspire to follow as many as 100,000 dogs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Within a few hours of announcing the project , 16,000 dog owners had &amp;ldquo ; nominated&amp;rdquo ; their pets online . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The hypothesis in gerontology circles is that , if aging can be delayed , it will also delay chronic diseases such as arthritis and Type 2 diabetes , leading to a longer and healthier life , said Dr. Marie Bernard , a geriatrician and deputy director of the National Institute on Aging . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Researchers have already shown they can increase the lifespans of yeast , fruit flies and mice through a number of measures , including caloric restriction , periodic fasting and administering a combination of metformin , which reduces blood sugar , and the immunosuppressant drug rapamycin. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jay Olshansky , a respected expert in pushing the boundaries of longevity , has estimated that curing cancer , heart disease or both in humans would only extend life expectancy by between three and eight years . However , slowing aging could extend life expectancy by over 30 years , and it would maximize the portion of life free of chronic disease and disability . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Those years would be spent in fairly good health , &amp;rdquo ; &amp;rsquo ; said Matt Kaeberlein , co-director of the Dog Aging Project . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It sounds like science fiction , but it&amp;rsquo ; s science fact . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;16133;TOOLONG ... title= " A woman walks her dog on Bank Street . " alt= " A woman walks her dog on Bank Street . " data-portal-copyright= " Tony Caldwell " **28;16169;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1507188 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nature has figured out how to modify the rates of aging in different species , Kaeberlein said . The naked mole rat , a burrowing rodent native to East Africa , can live to be more than 30 years old , for example , while most mice and rats live only a few years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Dog Aging Project&amp;rsquo ; s goal is to understand the genetic and environmental factors behind dog aging . Dogs age about seven times faster than humans , so data on thousands of dogs over their lifespan would yield important information about what correlates with a long and healthy life for a dog over an accelerated time frame . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In general , larger animals live longer than smaller animals , but the reverse is true in dogs . A chihuahua has a longer life expectancy than a Great Dane , for example , said Dr. Kate Creevy , a professor of veterinary medicine at Texas A&amp;amp ; M University and a member of the research team . Mixed-breed dogs also live , on average , about a year longer than purebred counterparts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;16199;TOOLONG ... title= " A black Great Dane . " alt= " A black Great Dane . " data-portal-copyright= " jsclark89 " **28;16235;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1507196 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The researchers will also be searching for dogs who have led exceptionally long lives : the canine equivalent of human centenarians. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The project is an open science initiative . Eventually the raw data , with confidential information scrubbed from it , will be available to any researcher who wants to use it to seek out patterns , including members of the public . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The study is open to dogs of all breeds and ages , but so far funding for the research is limited to dogs in the United States , Promislow said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rapamycin has been shown to restore heart function and boost immunity in middle-aged mice . It has already been used on a small number of dogs in a research study and their owners have noted no negative side effects and some positive side effects , including that the dogs were more energetic and affectionate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; People love their dogs . Our No. 1 priority is the safety of the dogs , &amp;rdquo ; Promislow said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rapamycin is approved for a limited number of uses in humans , including preventing rejection in organ transplantation . It appears to dampen the inflammation that comes with aging , but it&amp;rsquo ; s still a long way from being approved as an aging delay drug for humans , Kaeberlein said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This is a longitudinal study , which means it will follow the subjects over their entire lives . While researcher often have problems keeping in touch with their subjects in longitudinal studies , dog owners involved in research have been remarkably co-operative in previous long-term studies , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This is the most ambitious project on companion dogs and one of the most ambitious projects on aging , &amp;rdquo ; Promislow said . &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; ll have tons of data . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;i class= " " style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;&lt;span class= " " &gt;This article was written with the support of a journalism fellowship from The Gerontological Society of America , Journalists Network on Generations **26;16265;TOOLONG &lt;span class= " " style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt; the &lt;i class= " " &gt;Silver Century Foundation . &lt;/i&gt;&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;16293;TOOLONG ... title= " A man walks with his dogs . " alt= " A man walks with his dogs . " data-portal-copyright= " Sebastian Gollnow " **28;16329;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1257280 " /&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;16359;TOOLONG ... who live to at least 110 , boast stronger immune systems , helping them fight off diseases&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;16394;TOOLONG ... on fitness : Age is only a number , even if the number is over 50&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;16429;TOOLONG ... records mysterious , echoing howl in the Northern Ontario woods&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Joanne LauciusMon , 18 Nov 2019 11:40:45 **45;16464;TOOLONG ... **27;16511;TOOLONG says he suffers from Havana syndrome after incident during trade missionhttp : **33;16540;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;16575;TOOLONG ... title= " Canada 's embassy in Havana . " alt= " Canada 's embassy in Havana . " data-portal-copyright= " Desmond Boylan " **28;16611;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1434031 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; An Ontario businessman who travelled to Cuba in 2017 could be the first private Canadian citizen to have symptoms consistent with Havana syndrome , the mysterious illness that has affected diplomats and their families who were posted there . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The man , who has asked to remain anonymous because he is worried about his safety , says he experienced what he calls an &amp;ldquo ; attack&amp;rdquo ; while in his hotel room in downtown Havana in early 2017 . He was there as part of a privately sponsored trade mission . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He was sitting in his hotel room when he experienced a sensation inside his head that he has a hard time describing more than two years later . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I have never experienced anything like it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The man said he didn&amp;rsquo ; t hear any sounds , but felt overwhelming pressure and sensations in his head . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I felt pressure . It was like a gripping arrest of your consciousness , something being thrust on me , but I couldn&amp;rsquo ; t escape it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The man described the experience as sudden and terrifying . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I had no idea what the source was , but I knew it was abnormal . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When he could stand up from his chair , he was exhausted . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I thought , &amp;lsquo ; I have to lie down , I don&amp;rsquo ; t know what is going on&amp;rsquo ; . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He eventually left the hotel in an attempt to get some fresh air and clear his head , which was in a &amp;ldquo ; mental fog&amp;rdquo ; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While outside , he says he had a second experience with the sensations of pressure , but this time not as strong . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He returned to the hotel and slept for more than three hours . Because he was so terrified by the strange experience , he says , he was relieved when he woke up that he was still alive . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The man describes a severe &amp;ldquo ; brain fog&amp;rdquo ; that got worse in the days following the incident . He also began suffering from dizziness and tinnitus among many other symptoms . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It got worse to the point that I had dizzy spells in the weeks and months that followed . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It wasn&amp;rsquo ; t until he read an article in his local newspaper about the mysterious syndrome affecting Canadian and U.S. diplomats that the man began to wonder whether his experience was related . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His brief trip to Havana happened at the same time diplomats and their families experienced brain injuries from sources that have yet to be identified . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The man made an appointment at the University of Miami where some Canadian and American diplomats affected by Havana syndrome had been examined and tested . After a series of tests , the Ontario businessman was told his results were consistent with many other people who complained of similar attacks while in Cuba . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He also called the RCMP and was interviewed in Ottawa . This newspaper has seen a copy of the statement he sent RCMP in 2018. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Over the summer ( after returning from Cuba ) , I experienced almost every symptom that has been reported in the press and the symptoms were severe : dizziness , hearing loss , fatigue , weakness , difficulty concentrating , short-term memory loss , difficulty sleeping . &amp;rdquo ; He added that he was having difficulty managing his business responsibilities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; John Babcock , spokesperson for Global Affairs Canada , said an RCMP investigation is ongoing in collaboration with Cuban authorities . He added that there is &amp;ldquo ; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;no evidence that Canadian travellers to Cuba are at risk . Canadian travellers should continue to consult the Government of Canada 's travel advice and advisories for the latest updates . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Researchers in the U.S. and Canada have confirmed that diplomats and others posted to Havana suffered unusual brain injuries that are affecting their balance , hearing , vision , cognitive abilities and more . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Recently , research led by Dr. Alon Friedman of Dalhousie University&amp;rsquo ; s Brain Repair Centre , &lt;a href= " https : **33;16641;TOOLONG ... that pesticides used to combat mosquito-spread Zika virus could be responsible for the brain injuries&lt;/a&gt; suffered by Canadians and Americans posted to Havana . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That theory is controversial among some of the diplomats who are suing the Canadian government for $28 million , saying they failed to protect them or to take proper action to treat them . The diplomats , through their lawyer , say they are frustrated that there is no clear answer about what caused their illnesses , or any definitive answer about whether they were targeted with some kind of weapon . The insecticide theory , they say , is not conclusive. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Others have proposed that the diplomats could be victims of some kind of sonic weapon , although it is not clear why or by whom . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Ontario businessman said he was not aware of spraying inside or around the hotel room where he was staying . Nor did he have any explanation for why he would be the target of what he believes was an attack . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He has not heard anything back after speaking with RCMP officials . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And the man said the many stories about the issue and his own ongoing , but improving , symptoms have left him anxious and stressed . He hasn&amp;rsquo ; t even told his family about the experience . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It is the last thing you would want your family to be dragged into . Do you realize the controversy and how many people have dismissed it ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **27;16676;TOOLONG " **34;16705;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;16741;TOOLONG ... NewsMenWomenWorldDoctors Without Borders says not enough people are getting vaccinated in Ebola outbreakhttp : **32;16893;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;16927;TOOLONG ... title= " A child reacts as a health worker injects her with the Ebola vaccine , in Goma , Democratic Republic of Congo , August 5 , 2019. " alt= " A child reacts as a health worker injects her with the Ebola vaccine , in Goma , Democratic Republic of Congo , August 5 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Baz Ratner " **28;16963;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1480048 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; With Ebola deaths mounting in the Democratic Republic of Congo , the aid agency Doctors Without Borders is raising alarm bells about the way the vaccine to prevent the disease is being administered there . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jason Nickerson , humanitarian affairs adviser with Doctors Without Borders , said the organization is increasingly concerned that not enough people in DRC are being vaccinated to get the deadly outbreak under control . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Added to the frustration , he said , is lack of transparency around the vaccine , which was produced by Canadian scientists at the National Microbiology Laboratory in Winnipeg . The vaccine is now licensed to pharmaceutical company Merck , which is in the process of getting it approved and regulated . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meanwhile , it is being used during the Ebola outbreak in the DRC on a compassionate basis . So far , more than 2,000 people have died in what is the second-worst Ebola outbreak the world has seen , after the West African outbreak , which lasted from 2014 until 2016. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nickerson , who has recently returned to Ottawa from DRC , said Doctors Without Borders wants to see more transparency around how the vaccine is being rolled out by the World Health Organization there . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; For a long time , one of the big problems with this vaccine is that there has been very little transparency into many aspects of it . For a long time , we didn&amp;rsquo ; t know how many doses there were globally . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Given that , Nickerson said it concerns Doctors Without Borders that only 500 people are being vaccinated a day when the outbreak is not under control . So far , more than 200,000 people have been vaccinated. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One of the complicating factors is that vaccination is still being done using the so-called ring technique in which contacts of people with the virus are vaccinated in a ring around them to hinder the spread of the disease . The technique was used to eradicate smallpox . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nickerson said the strategy is rigid . As well , contact tracing is slow and often incomplete , especially when cases continue to turn up in the community . One third of all new Ebola cases occur in people who are not being tracked in the community . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Increasing the number of people being vaccinated could make a difference in the deadly outbreak . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We now have the vaccine and I think that is one tool in the tool box , but it needs to be implemented alongside other public health interventions . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This is the first major outbreak for which the Ebola vaccine &amp;mdash ; although still experimental &amp;mdash ; has been available from the beginning . It has had a success rate of around 97 per cent in DRC , said Nickerson. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Experimental antiviral treatments have also been successfully used in DRC. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;LISTEN TO THE 10/3 PODCAST&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe src= " **40;16993;TOOLONG ... " alt= " A screen grab from Al Masirah TV/Al Jazeera showing Saudi light armored vehicle captured by Houthi forces . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;17154;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1475159 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yemen&amp;rsquo ; s Houthi rebels have released video footage of the aftermath of a battle with Saudi forces which appears to show a captured Canadian-made light armoured vehicle . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The footage was released Sunday in what the rebels say started as an ambush inside Saudi Arabia but then turned into a major cross-border battle . The footage of the battle was shown on Houthi-run Al Masirah TV and Al Jazeera. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The rebels claimed that the attack killed or wounded 500 Saudi soldiers . Saudi Arabia has not acknowledged the fighting and the Houthi claims have not been independently verified . The footage shows the captured light armoured vehicle , another destroyed light armoured vehicle as well as armoured trucks provided to the Saudis by the U.S. company Oshkosh . The footage also showed Saudi troops who were taken prisoner . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Over the years , Saudi Arabia has purchased light armoured vehicles from Canada&amp;rsquo ; s General Dynamics Land Systems-Canada in London , Ont . In 2014 , the then Conservative government announced a deal worth an estimated $15 billion to sell Saudi Arabia more than 700 light armoured vehicles . That controversial deal was later approved by the Liberal government . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A Saudi-led coalition , which has been provided with arms and intelligence from the U.S. and other western nations , intervened in Yemen in 2015 after the Houthis overthrew the government . Saudi Arabia has faced severe criticism for its role in the ongoing war in Yemen , with allegations it has conducted unlawful airstrikes on civilians . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Besides the armoured vehicles , the video shows large amounts of captured small arms . It is not clear whether the Houthi forces took possession of the armoured vehicle or left it at the ambush site . Houthi spokesman Brig . Gen. Yahya Saree told Al Masirah TV that the captured soldiers were moved to &amp;ldquo ; secure places . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The fighting lasted for 72 hours , he added . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Doug Wilson-Hodge , spokesman for General Dynamics Land Systems-Canada , said the company is declining to comment on the footage. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Global Affairs Canada did not comment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Houthi rebels claimed more than 200 Saudi soldiers were killed in the battle . The fighting took place in the southern region of Najran. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Liberal government launched a review of the light armoured vehicle contract after the murder of journalist Jamal Khashoggi inside the Saudi consulate in Turkey . Earlier this year a United Nations report determined that Saudi Arabia was responsible for the killing of Khashoggi who was a critic of that country&amp;rsquo ; s regime . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A dozen organizations sent Prime Minister Justin Trudeau a letter earlier in August , questioning the status of the review and pointing out that no updates on progress have been provided . The lack of such information has brought &amp;ldquo ; the sincerity of the effort into question , &amp;rdquo ; according to the letter endorsed by organizations such as Oxfam Canada and Amnesty International . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Relations between Canada and Saudi Arabia soured last year after the Canadian government called for the release of two jailed Saudi human rights activists . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Saudis have also fallen behind in their payments for the light armoured vehicles received from General Dynamics . It was revealed in December that the Saudis owed Canada more than $1 billion for vehicles already delivered . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In August , the Liberal government announced it would provide General Dynamics with a $650 million repayable loan . It also signed a $2 billion sole-source contract to purchase 360 additional light armoured vehicles from the company . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Industry representatives say that deal was designed to help the firm handle the decision by the Saudis to withhold payment for vehicles already delivered as well shore up Liberal election fortunes in London area ridings . The armoured vehicle project for the Canadian Army was originally announced by the Liberal government with an estimated cost between $500 million and $1.5 billion . The plan was to award the contract in 2023 after a competition . But that competition was jettisoned in favour of the sole-source deal with General Dynamics . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The company&amp;rsquo ; s light armoured vehicle is the core of the Canadian Forces armoured vehicle fleet . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last month Houthi forces took credit for launching drone and missile attacks on Saudi Arabia&amp;rsquo ; s key oil processing facilities , causing significant damage . But the Saudis and the U.S. claimed that Iran and not the Houthis were behind the attack . Iran denied that claim . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;LISTEN TO THE DOWN TO BUSINESS PODCAST&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe src= " **40;17184;TOOLONG ... David Pugliese , Ottawa CitizenMon , 30 Sep 2019 20:11:38 **45;17226;TOOLONG ... **26;17273;TOOLONG research points to pesticides as possible cause of Havana Syndromehttp : **32;17301;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;17335;TOOLONG ... title= " Tourists ride in a vintage car past Canada 's Embassy in Havana . An early suggestion ascribing so-called ' Havana syndrome ' among diplomats to ' mass hysteria ' has now been generally dismissed . " alt= " Tourists ride in a vintage car past Canada 's Embassy in Havana . An early suggestion ascribing so-called ' Havana syndrome ' among diplomats to ' mass hysteria ' has now been generally dismissed . " data-portal-copyright= " ALEXANDRE MENEGHINI " **28;17371;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1437809 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; A new study of Canadian diplomats who were posted to Havana , Cuba points to insecticides used to fight the Zika virus as a possible cause of so-called Havana syndrome . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The research , led by Dr. Alon Friedman of Dalhousie University&amp;rsquo ; s Brain Repair Centre , runs counter to the prevailing theory that acoustic weapons attacks caused the brain damage seen in Canadian and U.S. diplomats who were posted in Havana between 2016 and 2018. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The team led by Friedman found the results of brain scans , blood tests and examinations , including trips to Cuba , point to environmental neurotoxins , the kind that might have been used during an aggressive campaign of spraying to mitigate the Zika virus , which is spread by mosquitos and can cause birth defects . Both the Canadian and Cuban governments were fumigating areas inside and outside where diplomats lived and worked around the time diplomats began having symptoms of Havana syndrome . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In an interview , Friedman said he sent the study to the federal government before it was published because of the urgency of the findings . The report was subsequently leaked and has since been released by Dalhousie University . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We sent it because we were thinking the government should do something . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Friedman is planning a joint study with the Cuban government to better understand what the neurotoxin or toxins might be and how they are being used . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meanwhile , the hypothesis at least provides a potential answer to what has been a long-standing mystery . It also offers a possible solution to the syndrome : Prevent exposure to toxic insecticides . Although there is currently no treatment , Friedman said the people will improve , some more slowly than others . Many have begun to improve . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We care a lot about these individuals . They were really sick and some of them are still sick . You care and you want to help . Personally , I just hoped that we could help . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As with earlier research on U.S. diplomats affected by the mysterious concussion-like syndrome , the new study confirms that the diplomats and their families suffered from brain injuries . But the researchers&amp;rsquo ; theory about what might have caused those injuries is new . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Friedman and his team studied 26 Canadian diplomats and their family members , most of whom had symptoms of so-called Havana syndrome after being posted to Havana . Their symptoms included cognitive impairment , visual and audio-vestibular impairment , including tinnitus , sensitivity to sound and feeling off-balance , as well as sleep disturbance , fatigue , headaches and irritability. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Friedman said he met in Ottawa recently with those diplomats to discuss the research findings . Not everyone reacted with relief . Some , he said , found it hard to believe . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think the response was very mixed between &amp;lsquo ; that might be interesting&amp;rsquo ; and &amp;lsquo ; no , it couldn&amp;rsquo ; t be&amp;rsquo ; . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Diplomats who earlier spoke with this newspaper described hearing sounds and developing headaches and cognitive problems as well as nausea , vision and balance issues . Several diplomats said their children suffered from severe nosebleeds and concussion-like symptoms . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Neuro imaging done as part of Friedman&amp;rsquo ; s study suggested that some kind of neurotoxin could be the culprit to the syndrome often described as concussion symptoms without a concussion . Subsequent tests revealed traces of an insecticide used in Cuba against mosquito larvae in some of the diplomats and families who had been exposed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Authors of the study said their results confirm brain injury in areas that suggest exposure to neurotoxins . They hypothesized organophosphorus insecticides as a &amp;ldquo ; likely source&amp;rdquo ; , noting that Cuba was aggressively fumigating to slow of the spread of Zika in 2016 and later . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Canadian embassy records furthermore confirmed a significant increase in the frequency of fumigations around and within staff houses beginning January 2017 , concurrent with reported symptoms . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Brain imaging of the former diplomats also showed differences in white matter from the brains of people who were not exposed . Some subjects who were tested before and after showed an increased brain volume after living in Havana and a leaky blood-brain barrier . The blood-brain barrier protects the brain from the entry of harmful substances. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Neuro imaging suggested that some kind of neurotoxin could be the culprit for the brain damage seen in diplomats and their families . Subsequent tests revealed traces of an insecticide used in Cuba against mosquito larvae in some of the diplomats and families who had been exposed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Pamela Isfeld , president of the Professional Association of Foreign Service Officers , said it would be a relief if this was the answer , but the mystery around its cause has been difficult for those affected . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This has got to be very stressful for the people involved . They are trying to get treatment for themselves and young children when theories are all over the place . Till we know what it is we don&amp;rsquo ; t know what to do about it . That has got everyone on edge until an answer is found . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She added that the federal government has put a more robust health and safety framework in place for foreign service officers that is addressing gaps . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dr. Douglas Smith , who directs the Center for Brain Injury and Repair at the University of Pennsylvania&amp;rsquo ; s Perelman School of Medicine , called the findings vindication for the patients who have been studied and treated there . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Many of those patients have felt &amp;ldquo ; under assault&amp;rdquo ; as a result of skeptical reporting about their symptoms and suggestions they are making them up . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; People outside keep making claims that this is psychological . This might give them some peace and some vindication that there is something real there and it is not just in their heads , &amp;rdquo ; said Smith . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Everyone who examined these patients feels this was real from a neurological point of view , that this was a true neurological disorder . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some former Canadian diplomats are suing the federal government for $28 million , claiming it failed to protect them and to initially take their symptoms seriously . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **27;17401;TOOLONG " **34;17430;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;17466;TOOLONG ... ca n't be sued for filing access to information requests , court rules&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;17501;TOOLONG ... internment camps to Hiroshima to Emperor 's enthronement , Dr. Henry Shibata a witness to history&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;17536;TOOLONG ... Party used Photoshop to add reusable cup and metal straw to photo of Elizabeth May&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Elizabeth PayneTue , 24 Sep 2019 17:13:40 **45;17571;TOOLONG ... **25;17618;TOOLONG of the world ' -- A celebration of Jessica Hyba 's too-short lifehttp : **36;17645;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;17683;TOOLONG ... title= " Close to 100 friends and family members gathered Sunday for a memorial service for Jessica Hyba , killed last March in an Ethiopian Airlines crash of a Boeing 737 MAX 8. " alt= " Close to 100 friends and family members gathered Sunday for a memorial service for Jessica Hyba , killed last March in an Ethiopian Airlines crash of a Boeing 737 MAX 8. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;17719;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1364914 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; When Ethiopian Airlines Flight 302 crashed minutes after takeoff from Addis Ababa last March 10 , it extinguished the lives of 157 people from more than 30 countries . One of the passengers was Jessica Hyba , a native of Ottawa and professional humanitarian . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That she was just 43 was a body blow to her extended family and tight , tight circle of friends , who gathered Sunday at the Cedarhill golf club for a celebration of her life . But Hyba&amp;rsquo ; s untimely death is notable for what she represents and what , therefore , has been lost . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Like so many others on that flight from Ethiopia , Hyba really was trying to help the dispossessed . Through a succession of jobs at Care Canada , Care International and the United Nations High Commissioner for Refugees , Hyba provided food , water , medicine and other assistance to refugees afflicted by natural and other disasters . For the better part of two decades , she took on tough , sometime dangerous assignments in Chad , Iraq , Indonesia and , shortly before her death , Somalia . &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;Hyba was , in a word , selfless -- in sharp contrast with the ethic of greed that has taken root in so many national governments in the time of U.S. President Donald Trump . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;17749;TOOLONG ... title= " Facebook photo of Jessica Hyba. " alt= " Facebook photo of Jessica Hyba. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;17785;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1444778 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; But Hyba understood the risks , too . &amp;nbsp ; She and her first husband , Arnaud Blazy , were both in the business of providing international aid . When the couple divorced some years ago , they came to an arrangement to ensure the safety of their two children , Ayla and Oth&amp;eacute ; a. &amp;nbsp ; From the moment of their divorce , they resolved that just one of them would work in dangerous territory . The other parent would help the refugee effort from the relative safety of administrative offices and look after the children . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For Hyba , that safe spot for most of the past five years was Geneva , the global headquarters for UNHCR . Earlier this year , with the children secure in the hands of Blazy in Europe , Hyba starting preparing for her new job as senior external relations officer with UNHCR in Mogadishu , Somalia . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; She was desperate to go to Somalia , &amp;rdquo ; said Jennifer Ghikas , a Geneva-based colleague . &amp;nbsp ; Part of it was to be with her new partner , Oliver Vick , who was also killed in the March 10 crash . But Hyba was also anxious to return to the field . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This is what makes her death so heart-rending . &amp;nbsp ; Front line humanitarian workers usually assume the biggest risks lie with kidnapping , war or disease . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Instead , Hyba would die because the globe&amp;rsquo ; s largest manufacturer of airliners , Boeing , failed to deal with a flaw in the&amp;nbsp ; anti-stall software required on the firm&amp;rsquo ; s most profitable aircraft , the Boeing 737 MAX 8 . That Boeing did not apply a software fix immediately following the crash under similar circumstances of a 737 MAX 8 last October in Indonesia is all the more egregious. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It is , and always will be unbelievable that someone like ( Jessica ) would leave life so violently , &amp;rdquo ; Blazy observed at Sunday&amp;rsquo ; s service . &amp;nbsp ; Nearly 100 people attended , including many within the group that attended Confederation High School in Barrhaven . Hyba left Ottawa for Banff shortly after graduating high school but was soon joined by half a dozen of her friends . Much of the clique hung out in the Alberta town for the next seven years , working in retail shops or restaurants and indulging their enthusiasm for hiking , skiing and snowboarding. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hyba&amp;rsquo ; s wanderlust took her to Australia , then England for university degrees &amp;mdash ; respectively , a BA in international relations and a master&amp;rsquo ; s degree in public policy . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; Jessica was a citizen of the world , &amp;rdquo ; noted Rev. Jim Sitler . The transformative event in her life was a successful personal interview with Care Canada &amp;mdash ; an international aid agency based in Ottawa . &amp;nbsp ; Hyba&amp;rsquo ; s first overseas assignment took her to Chad where she met a fellow humanitarian and her future husband , Blazy. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Jessica had incredible energy , &amp;rdquo ; said Jessica Mackie , one of her high-school friends . &amp;ldquo ; She actually felt compelled to make the world a better place . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Despite , or perhaps because of , the great physical distances , Hyba appears to have remained unusually close with her friends in Canada and elsewhere , through FaceTime and social media . It also says much that despite a rather complicated family , relations between its members do not appear strained . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jessica is survived by her mother , Karol Kovacs ( n&amp;eacute ; e Kalbfleisch ) , her stepfather , Allan Kovacs , and her brother Gregory . &amp;nbsp ; She is also survived by her father , Norbert Hyba , and Elizabeth Drew , Melanie and Naomi , as well as Susan Storey . Jessica&amp;rsquo ; s children Ayla and Oth&amp;eacute ; a are with their father , Blazy. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;17815;TOOLONG ... NewsWorldAs Ebola spreads in West Africa , Canada 's role in questionhttp : **32;17967;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;18001;TOOLONG ... title= " A Congolese health worker administers ebola vaccine to a child at the Himbi Health Centre in Goma , Democratic Republic of Congo , earlier in July . " alt= " A Congolese health worker administers ebola vaccine to a child at the Himbi Health Centre in Goma , Democratic Republic of Congo , earlier in July . " data-portal-copyright= " Olivia Acland " **28;18037;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1436755 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Four years ago in West Africa , during the biggest Ebola outbreak the world had seen , Canada was considered a leader in the global fight against the deadly virus , with a Canadian-developed vaccine considered a game-changer . Today Canada is largely on the sidelines &amp;mdash ; even being called out internationally for it &amp;mdash ; as a new deadly epidemic spreads across the Democratic Republic of Congo and beyond . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Where is Canada this time around ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The answer , like the epidemic that has infected more than 2,500 people in the conflict-ridden DRC , is complex . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The vaccine originally developed in Canada , now produced by Merck , is being used in the DRC , although it has not stopped the spread of the disease . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada has deployed emergency management specialists to work on the outbreak with the World Health Organization in neighbouring Congo . It has also provided $4.75 million in humanitarian aid to the UN , The Red Cross and Red Crescent and other NGOs working on the ground in the DRC. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada has contributed $4 million to the World Health Organization&amp;rsquo ; s contingency fund for emergencies since 2015. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It has also offered direct technical help to the DRC but has been turned down , according to a Canadian scientist . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still , observers say the overall global response to the outbreak that began on Aug. 1 , 2018 , has been disappointing and countries including Canada have not met financial commitments , which is slowing the response . Last month , former UK development minister Rory Stewart singled out Canada as a G7 country that needs to pull its weight in the fight against Ebola . He told BBC radio that he would be asking Canada to make more generous contributions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A spokesperson for Global Affairs Canada did not respond directly to questions about whether Canada is doing enough , but said the government is closely monitoring the situation and is committed to working with partners &amp;ldquo ; to support global public health . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;18067;TOOLONG ... title= " A Congolese health worker administers ebola vaccine to a child at the Himbi Health Centre in Goma , Democratic Republic of Congo , earlier in July . " alt= " A Congolese health worker administers ebola vaccine to a child at the Himbi Health Centre in Goma , Democratic Republic of Congo , earlier in July . " data-portal-copyright= " Olivia Acland " **28;18103;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1436755 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; With the Ebola outbreak now declared a public health emergency , observers say it is past time for Canada and other countries to do more . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The world was way too slow in responding to this outbreak , &amp;rdquo ; said Steven Hoffman , scientific director of the Canadian Institutes of Health Research&amp;rsquo ; s ( CIHR ) Institute of Population and Pubic Health . &amp;ldquo ; We can&amp;rsquo ; t respond to everything with lightning speed , but it has been many months since it has been known that this is a very serious public health emergency . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That could soon change , said Gary Kobinger , one of the Canadian scientists who helped design a vaccine against Ebola. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And Kobinger , who is based at the Centre for Research in Infectious Diseases at Laval University in Quebec City , said the recent resignation of the health minister in the DRC could set the stage for other changes in the outbreak response . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The declaration earlier this month that the outbreak is a public health emergency has brought more focus &amp;mdash ; and money &amp;mdash ; to the fight . The World Bank announced $300 million to fight the epidemic soon after it was declared a health emergency . There is no word on whether Canada will increase its contribution . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kobinger said he believes it is time to change the approach to fighting the outbreak , which has been hampered by ongoing conflict and mistrust . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He believes the Ebola vaccine should have been used to vaccinate everyone in a region when the outbreak began . Instead , health officials have employed the so-call ring-vaccine technique &amp;mdash ; tracing contacts and vaccinating everyone in a &amp;ldquo ; ring&amp;rdquo ; around the person infected with Ebola . The vaccine has saved lives &amp;mdash ; especially among health workers &amp;mdash ; but the technique is not working to stop the outbreak from spreading , he said . Kobinger said it is too late now to vaccinate everyone , given the amount of vaccine available and the number of people involved . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kobinger was part of a group of people who evaluated the response to the outbreak recently . Kobinger said there have been missed opportunities to shut down the epidemic in the past year , especially when it comes to engaging the public and coordination . He said he hopes a new focus on engaging the public will help turn things around in the DRC. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kobinger has been to the DRC multiple times each year for the past decade to help train workers there to manage an Ebola outbreak . Some of the labs and equipment used are from Canada . Kobinger offered his help during the current outbreak , but was turned down . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;18133;TOOLONG ... title= " Congolese health workers collect data before administering ebola vaccines to civilians at the Himbi Health Centre in Goma , Democratic Republic of Congo , in mid-July . " alt= " Congolese health workers collect data before administering ebola vaccines to civilians at the Himbi Health Centre in Goma , Democratic Republic of Congo , in mid-July . " data-portal-copyright= " Olivia Acland " **28;18169;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1436754 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The goal has been for the DRC to handle the outbreak themselves , but doing so right now has been a &amp;ldquo ; huge challenge , &amp;rdquo ; he said . Kobinger said he is hoping that will change , and that it will not be too late to get the outbreak under control . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Canada contributed a lot , now the goal is to let them do it . There are a lot of good people who are working really hard , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Canada is really keen on helping . They have never given up on supporting the DRC and the outbreak , but they can&amp;rsquo ; t impose that help . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hoffman , of the CIHR Institute of Population and Pubic Health , said he has been frustrated that the initial response to the outbreak didn&amp;rsquo ; t seem to fully factor in how crucial it was to have the trust of the community , given the complex social environment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But that could change , he said . The recent shift suggests the DRC may be more welcoming of outside help . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This might be the time Canada could be well-placed to step up to make the kind of contributions it historically has . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;18199;TOOLONG ... " alt= " Charlotte Girard and Romane Val ? e read aloud the names of Canada 's war dead at the Canada War Museum on Thursday . The two Normandy residents are members of the Westlake Brothers Souvenir Association , which visits Canada every four years to honour the Canadians who helped liberate France in 1944. " data-portal-copyright= " Bruce Deachman " **28;18426;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1435419 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; One by one , with just the right solemnity , the names of 7,000 of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s Second World War dead were read aloud on Thursday at the Canadian War Museum . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For the most part , the speakers were not themselves Canadian , nor were they even born when the last Canadian soldier fell to that war . But they remember nonetheless . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The 30 young people , ages 13 to 24 , were visiting Canada from France &amp;mdash ; specifically Normandy &amp;mdash ; and had come this far to honour the Canadians who 75 years ago liberated France from German occupation . They&amp;rsquo ; re in Canada for nearly a month , travelling from London to Quebec City to take part in 20 official functions . Five of those events will mirror Thursday&amp;rsquo ; s : In London , Toronto , Cornwall , Longueuil and Quebec City , the remaining names of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s 47,000 Second World War dead have been or will be read aloud . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some among the group were also planning to take part in a celebration of life on Thursday evening at Beechwood Cemetery for D-Day veteran Bud Hannam , who died on June 29 at the age of 94 . An honourary citizen of Basly , just eight kilometres from Juno Beach , Hannam spent 52 days in the area in June and July 1944 as a medic and stretcher&amp;ndash ; bearer . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s hard to put words on it , but we respect the fact that they came to Normandy 75 years ago , &amp;rdquo ; said Anna Mainhagu , 22 , from Coutances. &amp;ldquo ; They weren&amp;rsquo ; t French , but they came to serve our country . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; They came to give us freedom , &amp;rdquo ; added Philippe Bosquain , 17 , from Gonneville. &amp;ldquo ; We owe a lot to Canadians for our liberties . I&amp;rsquo ; ve always heard people talking about the Canadians . My grandfather wrote a book about ( D-Day ) , so being able to meet some of them has been something I&amp;rsquo ; ve wanted to do . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Over the past two weeks , the group has met numerous Second World War veterans , including on Wednesday at the National War Memorial . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think we&amp;rsquo ; ve touched them , &amp;rdquo ; said Mainhagu. &amp;ldquo ; You can see the emotion in their faces , to see they&amp;rsquo ; re not forgotten . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I feel that&amp;rsquo ; s how some of them feel , &amp;rdquo ; added Bosquain. &amp;ldquo ; They feel like they&amp;rsquo ; re being forgotten . But by seeing them now , we can put faces on the people that we&amp;rsquo ; re proud of . And we can tell them that they&amp;rsquo ; re our heroes , because that&amp;rsquo ; s what they are . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mainhagu and Bosquain are part of a 150-strong group called the Westlake Brothers Souvenir Association , which was founded in 2006 by Christophe Collet , a Caen high-school teacher who lives in Basly . A group of his students in 2004 asked him about how soldiers lived in 1944 &amp;mdash ; where they slept , what they ate , that sort of thing . They came up with 60 cards with questions on them for an imaginary soldier , which the M&amp;eacute ; morial de Caen military museum passed along to 35 Canadian vets visiting Normandy for the 60th anniversary of D-Day . The students were invited to meet the veterans . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You couldn&amp;rsquo ; t imagine the emotion , &amp;rdquo ; recalled Collet. &amp;ldquo ; It was incredible , this meeting of young people from today and young people from yesterday . It was like meeting old parents that you didn&amp;rsquo ; t see for a long time . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That led to a number of Collet&amp;rsquo ; s students dedicating a space in their school to the Canadian soldiers , and then , in 2006 , a trip to Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When they returned to France , they formed the Westlake group . The objective of the association , named for three Toronto brothers &amp;mdash ; Thomas , Albert and George Westlake &amp;mdash ; who all died in Normandy over five days in June 1944 , is &amp;ldquo ; the promotion and preservation of the Duty of Remembrance owed to those Canadians who dared to cross the ocean &amp;hellip ; to liberate first our region , then France , and finally the whole of Europe , from Nazi barbarity . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This year&amp;rsquo ; s trip to Canada is the third since the association&amp;rsquo ; s formation : they&amp;rsquo ; ve made similar journeys every four years since 2011. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Collet recognizes that remembrance , in France and elsewhere , is a fading art . In Canada , he says , war remembrances often tend to be downplayed or overlooked because the conflicts didn&amp;rsquo ; t take place on Canadian soil , and because Canada&amp;rsquo ; s war history is not particularly well taught in schools . But in France , he adds , relics of the war remain everywhere , not just in museums , and history of the wars is a frequent and mandatory school subject . In Basly , which boasts a population of only about 1,100 , two streets and three monuments are dedicated to Canadians . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In the next village , Anguerny ( same population ) , there are five monuments and three streets dedicated to Canadians . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Some of the people here in the association can&amp;rsquo ; t explain why they are here , &amp;rdquo ; he adds . &amp;ldquo ; Often they can&amp;rsquo ; t explain . They say &amp;lsquo ; We don&amp;rsquo ; t know why we are here , but we can&amp;rsquo ; t imagine being in another place when we do what we do . &amp;rsquo ; &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;18456;TOOLONG ... research needed on low-temperature liquid cremation , Public Health Ontario says&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;18491;TOOLONG ... flyover at RedBlacks ' Canadian Forces appreciation night&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;18526;TOOLONG ... know they are not alone ' : In Renfrew , community paramedics make house calls and change lives&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **30;18561;TOOLONG " **37;18593;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Bruce DeachmanThu , 25 Jul 2019 21:25:43 **45;18632;TOOLONG ... ( NASA ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; The company that manufactured the spacesuits for the Apollo program also designed and made Playtex bras. ( &lt;a href= " https : **33;18783;TOOLONG ... magazine&lt;/a&gt; ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; The moon&amp;rsquo ; s orbit around the Earth is getting larger , at the rate of about 3.8 centimetres a year . It is now , on average , about two metres farther away than it was in 1969 . ( Cornell University ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; Edwin &amp;ldquo ; Buzz&amp;rdquo ; Aldrin celebrated Communion on the moon with bread and wine . ( National Geographic ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;18818;TOOLONG ... title= " Edwin " Buzz " Aldrin is shown during the lunar landing mission on July 20 , 1969 .
