( Photo : Neil Armstrong/NASA ) " data-portal-copyright= " NASA " **28;18854;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1430382 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; The diameter of the moon&amp;rsquo ; s largest crater , the South Pole-Aitken basin , is approximately 2,500 km , almost the distance from Ottawa to Miami . ( Science magazine , google maps ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; From blastoff to splashdown , the Apollo 11 mission lasted eight days , three hours and 18 minutes . Of the 21 hours spent on the moon , 2&amp;frac12 ; were spent by Armstrong and/or Aldrin outside the lunar module . ( NASA ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; Buzz Aldrin&amp;rsquo ; s first words from the lunar surface , about 20 minutes after Neil Armstrong&amp;rsquo ; s famous &amp;ldquo ; One small step&amp;rdquo ; remark , were &amp;ldquo ; Beautiful view ! &amp;rdquo ; After Armstrong replied &amp;ldquo ; Isn&amp;rsquo ; t that something ! Magnificent sight out here , &amp;rdquo ; Aldrin answered , &amp;rdquo ; Magnificent desolation . &amp;rdquo ; ( NASA ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; Approximately 382 kilograms of moon rocks have been brought back to Earth . That&amp;rsquo ; s roughly equivalent to the weight of an adult female moose . ( NASA , Live Science ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;18884;TOOLONG ... title= " A large sample of moon rock is contained in airtight tanks at the Lyndon B. Johnson Space Center in Houston , Texas ( Photo:The Washington Post ) " alt= " A large sample of moon rock is contained in airtight tanks at the Lyndon B. Johnson Space Center in Houston , Texas ( Photo:The Washington Post ) " data-portal-copyright= " Spike Johnson " **28;18920;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1430459 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; Two hours before the Apollo 11 lunar module landed on the moon , Neil Armstrong&amp;rsquo ; s heart rate was 75 beats per minute . At the point of touchdown , it had doubled to 150 bpm . When he took his first step on the moon&amp;rsquo ; s surface , about six-and-a-half hours later , it was down to 125 bpm . ( The Atlantic magazine ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; When the lunar module landed on the moon , it had only about 25 seconds&amp;rsquo ; worth of fuel left . Had it used up that fuel , the landing would have been aborted . ( NASA ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; The volume of the command module that carried the three-man Apollo 11 crew was just over 200 cubic feet . By comparison , the volume of a 2019 Ford Explorer is about 100 cubic feet . ( NASA , Ford ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;18950;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;18992;TOOLONG ... years ago , men landed on the moon . Ottawans share their memories of that monumental day&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;19027;TOOLONG ... future in space starts with the moon , **26;19062;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; Of the 12 men who have walked on the moon , only four &amp;mdash ; Buzz Aldrin , David Scott , Charles Duke and Harrison Schmitt &amp;mdash ; are still alive . ( NASA ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; After splashdown in the Pacific Ocean near Honolulu , the Apollo 11 crew had to declare the space rocks and dust they returned with at U.S. Customs . The response to the Customs form&amp;rsquo ; s query regarding &amp;ldquo ; Any other condition on board which may lead to the spread of disease , &amp;rdquo ; was &amp;ldquo ; To be determined . &amp;rdquo ; ( space.com ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;19090;TOOLONG ... title= " The U.S. Customs declaration form that the crew of Apollo 11 had to fill out after returning from the moon . " alt= " The U.S. Customs declaration form that the crew of Apollo 11 had to fill out after returning from the moon . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;19126;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1430456 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; Upon their return to Earth , the crew spent 21 days in a Mobile Quarantine Facility , in case they had brought dangerous germs back from space . The &amp;ldquo ; facility&amp;rdquo ; was constructed using a modified 10.7-metre aluminum Airstream trailer . ( NASA ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; U.S. President Nixon had a contingency speech prepared in case Neil Armstrong and &amp;ldquo ; Buzz&amp;rdquo ; Aldrin failed to get off the moon alive . It opened with the line &amp;ldquo ; Fate has ordained that the men who went to the moon to explore in peace will stay on the moon to rest in peace . &amp;rdquo ; It ended with : &amp;ldquo ; For every human being who looks up at the moon in the nights to come will know that there is some corner of another world that is forever mankind . &amp;rdquo ; ( The U.S. National Archives and Records Administration ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt; -- Compiled by Bruce Deachman , Ottawa Citizen&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;h2 style= " text-align:center ; " &gt;WATCH : Moon Landing : Fifty Years On&lt;/h2&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;19156;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;19192;TOOLONG Bruce DeachmanFri , 19 Jul 2019 10:41:21 **45;19228;TOOLONG ... years ago , men landed on the moon . Ottawans share their memories of that monumental dayhttp : **37;19275;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;19314;TOOLONG ... title= " Astronomer Gary Boyle stands under the seven-metre wide moon model at the Canadian Museum of Nature . " alt= " Astronomer Gary Boyle stands under the seven-metre wide moon model at the Canadian Museum of Nature . " data-portal-copyright= " Bruce Deachman " **28;19350;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1429172 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;19380;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; At 10:56 p.m . EST on Sunday , July 20 , 1969 , Neil Armstrong set foot on the moon , the first human to walk on a world other than our own . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The event captured imaginations everywhere . An estimated record 600 million people watched it live on TV , one out of every six people on the planet . In Toronto , 35,000 people jammed into Nathan Phillips Square to watch on a 9&amp;times ; 12-metre screen , while 80,000 spectators crowded into Montreal&amp;rsquo ; s Man and His World to witness the event on a large screen at France&amp;rsquo ; s pavilion . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In New York , 10,000 gathered in Central Park for a &amp;ldquo ; moon-in , &amp;rdquo ; while a large television in the arrivals lounge of John F. Kennedy International Airport halted travellers in their tracks . Twelve hundred passersby stopped to watch on a five-metre screen set up at the corner of 6th Avenue and 54th Street . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In Paris , thousands assembled at Champs-Elys&amp;eacute ; es , while thousands more watched from London&amp;rsquo ; s Trafalgar Square . A crowded pub in Birmingham changed its name from Man in the Moon to Man on the Moon , a minor alteration that 50 years later still stands . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Elsewhere , smaller crowds gathered worldwide in cafes , on sidewalks outside window displays of televisions , in parks , boulevards , kitchens , living rooms and classrooms . In the high arctic of Norway , Laplanders reportedly sat around campfires listening to transistor radios and composing folk songs about the astronauts. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa astronomer and educator Brian McCullough , then 16 , watched on a black-and-white TV in his home in Cinnaminson , New Jersey , near Philadelphia , where his father , serving with the Royal Canadian Air Force , was posted . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Watching TV with these grainy images , and you hear all the static , and the beeps between communications . They&amp;rsquo ; re landing on the moon but you hear all the drama of it ahead of time &amp;mdash ; &amp;lsquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re down to 40,000 feet , &amp;rsquo ; &amp;lsquo ; 1202 alarm&amp;rsquo ; &amp;mdash ; and I&amp;rsquo ; m listening to all the drama of this and thinking , &amp;lsquo ; They&amp;rsquo ; re going to land on the moon ! &amp;rsquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I was really excited , &amp;rdquo ; he adds . &amp;ldquo ; When Eagle was going down to land , basically the Earth paused . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The Earth paused to watch . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;19416;TOOLONG ... title= " 12.29.1968 -- This view of the rising Earth greeted the Apollo 8 astronauts as they came from behind the Moon after the lunar orbit insertion burn . Earth is about five degrees above the horizon in the photo . The unnamed surface features in the foreground are near the eastern limb of the Moon as viewed from Earth . The lunar horizon is approximately 780 kilometers from the spacecraft . Width of the photographed area at the horizon is about 175 km ( 109 miles ) . On the Earth 386,000 km ( 240,000 miles ) away , the sunset terminator bisects Africa . " alt= " 12.29.1968 -- This view of the rising Earth greeted the Apollo 8 astronauts as they came from behind the Moon after the lunar orbit insertion burn . Earth is about five degrees above the horizon in the photo . The unnamed surface features in the foreground are near the eastern limb of the Moon as viewed from Earth . The lunar horizon is approximately 780 kilometers from the spacecraft . Width of the photographed area at the horizon is about 175 km ( 109 miles ) . On the Earth 386,000 km ( 240,000 miles ) away , the sunset terminator bisects Africa . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;19452;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1430351 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; McCullough&amp;rsquo ; s introduction to space occurred when he was seven or eight and living at CFB Rockcliffe . &amp;ldquo ; I remember my dad hauling us out into the grass of the backyard in our PJs to watch a satellite going over . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His interest , however , was torqued in 1968 with Apollo 8&amp;rsquo ; s Christmas Eve manned orbits of the moon that resulted in the first Earthrise photo . On that night , the mission&amp;rsquo ; s three-man crew of Frank Borman , James Lovell and William Anders took turns reading verses from the Book of Genesis , while McCullough looked on from his backyard , almost in disbelief . &amp;ldquo ; The farthest anyone had been from Earth at that point was about 1,200 nautical miles , and now they&amp;rsquo ; re a quarter of a million nautical miles downrange and have gone into orbit around another body . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It was pure theatre , &amp;rdquo ; he recalls . &amp;ldquo ; I was 16 years old and that was blowing my mind . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;19482;TOOLONG ... title= " Ottawa astronomer Brian McCullough in his backyard observatory . " alt= " Ottawa astronomer Brian McCullough in his backyard observatory . " data-portal-copyright= " Bruce Deachman " **28;19518;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1429164 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The next morning , he awoke to find a Sears Discoverer 60-millimetre refracting telescope under the Christmas tree . The combination of magnification and lens diameter made viewing too dark to see much , but McCullough was hooked . &amp;ldquo ; I remember looking at the moon through this shaky telescope and thinking , &amp;lsquo ; There are people there . &amp;rsquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It was amazing . It changed the whole way I looked at things . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As the world gathered in large public groups to watch the Apollo 11 moon landing , Ottawa was busy being Ottawa . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Ottawa was moonstruck Sunday night , &amp;rdquo ; the Citizen reported . &amp;ldquo ; The streets were nearly deserted , the all-night cafes were almost empty , theatre crowds were cut in half and taxi drivers weren&amp;rsquo ; t answering calls . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Almost everyone was inside watching the astronauts perform their curious moondance on television . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to Ottawa police Lt. Ken Spratt , &amp;ldquo ; The tempo of the city seems to have slowed down . There&amp;rsquo ; s hardly a soul on the street anywhere . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meanwhile , a better-than-average crowd had gathered at the St. Louis Hotel in Hull , where the bar&amp;rsquo ; s three colour TV sets drew in extra patrons , who cheered during the broadcast . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawans largely watched from their homes , perhaps inspired by acting prime minister Mitchell Sharp&amp;rsquo ; s sombre advice that &amp;ldquo ; Civil servants can rejoice at their desks just as well as anywhere else along with the rest of the human race in this magnificent achievement . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;19548;TOOLONG ... title= " Front page of The Citizen July 21 , 1969. " alt= " Front page of The Citizen July 21 , 1969. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;19584;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1430937 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; City controller Ellen Douglas Webber , however , called Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s lack of festivities &amp;ldquo ; a hell of a thing . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think we should have done something great and drastic , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; But good old Ottawa , as far as government organization goes , is just not part of the world when something tremendous happens . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; While people in other cities were celebrating , staid old Ottawa was sitting at home watching television . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And even at that , some of them weren&amp;rsquo ; t delighted . At least 15 viewers called CJOH-TV to complain that the moon landing coverage had pre-empted an episode of Star Trek . Over at CBOT-TV , two viewers called to ask why the CBC didn&amp;rsquo ; t have a cameraman on the moon to record the historic moment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The lunar landing did more than simply capture the world&amp;rsquo ; s attention as it paused to watch on TV or momentarily gaze up at the night sky . Neil Armstrong&amp;rsquo ; s small step , like the proverbial flapping of a butterfly&amp;rsquo ; s wings , led to a typhoon of interest , curiosity , hope and wonder , and , in ways big and small , changed the courses of countless lives . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;19614;TOOLONG ... title= " Front page , Ottawa Journal , July 21 , 1969. " alt= " Front page , Ottawa Journal , July 21 , 1969. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;19650;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1429171 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Phil Seto was one of those who watched on TV , in the living room of his parents&amp;rsquo ; Sandy Hill home . He was 13 years old and a Star Trek fan who had never flown in an airplane . The entire U.S. space program fascinated him . And almost half a century after Apollo 17 ended the first golden age of space travel , he can still recite the names and missions of the dozen astronauts who have walked on the moon . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I was a kid , &amp;rdquo ; he recalls , &amp;ldquo ; and these were the new heroes , the Mercury , Gemini and Apollo astronauts . They were the new heroes in their space suits , getting into the tight Mercury capsules and rocketing into space . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He no longer has the metre-high Saturn V rocket he built , but the kit contained a coupon with which model-builders could send away to NASA for an eight-millimetre film abut the Apollo 11 mission . Seto has hung onto that short movie , as well as space-related books , magazines and record albums , for 50 years . And in 1973 , Seto met Apollo 15 astronaut James Irwin , the eighth man to walk on the moon , at the Ottawa Ex , a meeting he calls the thrill of a lifetime . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But for a hopeful 13-year-old back in 1969 , the missions to space really marked the beginning of the future . &amp;ldquo ; I was up like everybody else around the world , glued to the television set watching every little bit of it , &amp;rdquo ; he recalls . &amp;ldquo ; They said they were going to do it and they did it . All the work and technology that went into it , the brilliant minds that designed the space suits , the capsules . Everything from the food the astronauts were going to eat . The computer they had on board &amp;mdash ; that was the baby-step slow start that led to Bill Gates . That was the start . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;19680;TOOLONG ... title= " Space enthusiast Phil Seto shows some of the paraphernalia he 's kept since the historic 1969 moon landing . " alt= " Space enthusiast Phil Seto shows some of the paraphernalia he 's kept since the historic 1969 moon landing . " data-portal-copyright= " Bruce Deachman " **28;19716;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1429161 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Past president of the Royal Astronomical Society of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s Ottawa chapter , Tim Cole describes himself as a lunatic with a romance for the sky . He paraphrases science fiction author Jerry Pournelle in describing his discouragement over the cancellation of Apollo missions 18 , 19 and 20 , and the end of the program in 1972 : &amp;ldquo ; I never thought I would live to see the first man walk on the moon , but I sure as hell didn&amp;rsquo ; t think I would live to see the last one . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; That was my attitude and disappointment , &amp;rdquo ; Cole recalls . &amp;ldquo ; How could we NOT keep doing this ? And then I realized &amp;mdash ; because we did it . The job is done . It&amp;rsquo ; s over . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Cole was born in Harbour Grace , N.L. in 1958 , the same year , he points out , that NASA was born . The first book he asked his parents to buy him was What Is a Rocket ? when he was four . The next was the How and Why Wonder Book of Stars . As a youngster , he built numerous aircraft and space models , including the Saturn V rocket , lunar and command modules , and one of Gemini 4 astronaut Ed White&amp;rsquo ; s famous 1965 tethered spacewalk , the first by an American . When he was eight , Cole sent away to NASA for a package of maps , photos and other space-related material . On one wall of his bedroom was a technical drawing of the Saturn V rocket that stretched from knee level to the ceiling . He joined a science book club . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; ve been a geek since forever , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &amp;ldquo ; Space was the thing at the time &amp;mdash ; space and computers &amp;mdash ; and that was me . Although I didn&amp;rsquo ; t have a clue what a computer was . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When he watched Neil Armstrong take his first moon steps , Cole was 10 , eating popcorn in his pajamas and trying desperately to stay awake . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I remember thinking , &amp;lsquo ; Get my Major Matt Mason stuff out . This is the future . This is where humanity is going . I could do this when I&amp;rsquo ; m older . I could be there . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; And I thought I might . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The cynic in Cole recognizes that the U.S. space program was essentially the political response to the Cold War threat posed by the Soviet Union . &amp;ldquo ; People were scared shitless of the Reds taking over . You can&amp;rsquo ; t overestimate how much Sputnik 1 ( the first artificial satellite in space , launched by the Soviets in 1957 ) scared the crap out of people on this side of the pond . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But what impresses Cole most about America&amp;rsquo ; s response was its largely non-aggressive nature . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; One other way to do that , to impress the Russians , might&amp;rsquo ; ve been to build an incredible set of ballistic missiles , or a better bomb . But instead , they chose to build a rocket to take people to the moon . That was their way to say , &amp;lsquo ; We are goddamn good . &amp;rsquo ; They decided to show their ability by doing something great , as opposed to doing something mighty . &amp;lsquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re going to do something brilliant , something more than just banging sticks together . &amp;rsquo ; &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;19746;TOOLONG ... title= " Ottawa astronomer and space educator Tim Cole in his backyard with one of six telescopes he owns . " alt= " Ottawa astronomer and space educator Tim Cole in his backyard with one of six telescopes he owns . " data-portal-copyright= " Bruce Deachman " **28;19782;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1429173 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Josh Moon wishes he still had the model lunar and command modules he built as a youngster , but they&amp;rsquo ; re long lost to the vagaries of time . He does , however , still have the scrapbook he kept throughout those early NASA missions . Its cover reads : &amp;ldquo ; Space , Cars ( Engines and Planes ) , and Facts , &amp;rdquo ; although he confesses it&amp;rsquo ; s woefully short of cars and engines . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Inside , though , is a clipping from the Citizen&amp;rsquo ; s July 21 , 1969 edition , with a photograph of Moon , along with his younger siblings , Beth and Jamie , and three adults who also bore the surname Moon &amp;mdash ; Gerry , Harold and Derek &amp;mdash ; posing beneath the model of the moon then on display at the Museum of Science and Technology . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Josh was 11 and living in Lucerne , between Hull and Aylmer . On the day of the moon landing , his mother , Anna , arranged a party with about two dozen friends . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Their birthdays are all in the winter , &amp;rdquo ; she recalls , &amp;ldquo ; and I&amp;rsquo ; d promised them a summertime party , so this seemed to be the perfect occasion . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She put a stack of paper grocery bags on the picnic table in the backyard , along with scissors , star stickers , crayons and such , which the children turned into space helmets and boots . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She fed them snacks with space-themed names &amp;mdash ; Galaxy punch , Apollo sweets , Luna snacks and Gemini sandwiches &amp;mdash ; and organized games such as the &amp;ldquo ; space race , &amp;rdquo ; in which the youngsters tried to jump the highest off the ground . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Josh remarked at the time how cool and calm Neil Armstrong and Buzz Aldrin seemed when they landed on the moon . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I was a geek , yes , &amp;rdquo ; he says today . &amp;ldquo ; I cut this stuff out . I remember my friends were into it as well . It &lt;em&gt;was&lt;/em&gt; something you talked about . I remember all of us talking about watching it on TV. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His recollection of the actual landing is now little more than &amp;ldquo ; a grainy memory . &amp;rdquo ; He better recalls the backyard party and the taxi ride to and from the museum . And for someone who&amp;rsquo ; d been repeatedly kidded about his last name , the late 1960s and early &amp;lsquo ; 70s was a time when he could brag. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It was pretty cool when your name is Moon and a newspaper headline says it&amp;rsquo ; s the dawn of the moon age . I remember it being cool to have your name relevant to the news of the day &amp;mdash ; but that was just a side story to the other story of man walking on the moon . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I was just fascinated by this notion of going to space and landing up there on that orb in the sky , &amp;rdquo ; he adds . &amp;ldquo ; We were entranced by that . It&amp;rsquo ; s a moon &amp;mdash ; we get it &amp;mdash ; but someone&amp;rsquo ; s going to go there . That was enchanting . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;19812;TOOLONG ... title= " Ottawa lawyer Josh Moon shows some of the clippings from a scrapbook he compiled in 1969 and kept for half a century . " alt= " Ottawa lawyer Josh Moon shows some of the clippings from a scrapbook he compiled in 1969 and kept for half a century . " data-portal-copyright= " Bruce Deachman " **28;19848;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1429168 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As he listened to CBS news anchor Walter Cronkite explain to viewers how to take a picture of their TV screen using a Brownie camera , Gary Boyle , 12 , was on the third-floor balcony of his family&amp;rsquo ; s Verdun walkup with his telescope , peering a quarter-million miles into the past , and the future . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I had the best of both worlds , &amp;rdquo ; he recalls . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His telescope didn&amp;rsquo ; t offer much detail , but he at least knew which part of the moon to aim it towards . &amp;ldquo ; I was thinking , &amp;lsquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s going to happen there . I&amp;rsquo ; m looking at the moon as this is going to happen . &amp;rsquo ; &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He&amp;rsquo ; d been a space enthusiast for four years by then , since wandering into his school&amp;rsquo ; s library when he was in Grade 3 and finding a book simply titled Stars . &amp;ldquo ; Just opening that book and seeing our sun &amp;mdash ; just a circle with a small blemish inside &amp;mdash ; and reading that 109 Earths &amp;mdash ; 109 ! &amp;mdash ; fit across the sun , and 1.3 million would fit inside . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; And from that day in September 1965 , just from the size of the stars , their distances and colours , I was interested . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The moon landings , he says , opened a new era , not just in space exploration , but for humans getting things done . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The end of the 1960s was five months away when we landed on the moon , &amp;rdquo ; he explains . A mainframe computer could take up an entire room , and they had to make a computer to fit on board . It was thinking outside the box , which humans can really do . We are a species that investigates . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Boyle points to studies done decades ago by an astrophysicist interested in water molecules in our galaxy&amp;rsquo ; s nebulae . That research ultimately led to magnetic resonance imaging used today in hospitals . &amp;ldquo ; He didn&amp;rsquo ; t make the MRI , &amp;rdquo ; Boyle notes , &amp;ldquo ; but his technology led to it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The night sky , &amp;rdquo ; he adds , &amp;ldquo ; is forever . It&amp;rsquo ; s the oldest of the allied sciences . You don&amp;rsquo ; t really need any instrument to look at the Milky Way . Binoculars are a great way to start , and telescopes are obviously better . But I&amp;rsquo ; ve never stopped . I write , I observe , I photograph , as much as I can . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Few people today would be unable to complete Armstrong 's celebrated " One small step for man ... " quote , as the reverberations of his quiet and distant footfall are still felt across the globe all these years later . The moon landing was a momentous achievement on its own , but it also opened worlds of possibility for the hundreds of millions who turned to the heavens to watch , a human triumph that forever changed the lives of so many . Indeed , you could argue that it could n't help but do so . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gary Boyle , a federal public servant in downtown Ottawa by day , lives in the country south of the city , where he finds a peaceful serenity looking up at the Milky Way and considering how small we really are . A frequent educator known on radio stations and in newspaper columns as The Backyard Astronomer , he advises people to at least once in their lifetime get out into the country on a clear moonless night and look at the 2,000 visible stars . &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s almost therapy , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &amp;ldquo ; If you had a bad day at the office , you just go outside and look up . Anyone who looks up for the first time opens their mouth and they have so many questions , and every time you get an answer it opens up more questions . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two years ago , as a result of all the outreach work he&amp;rsquo ; s done in astronomy , he had an asteroid named after him : 22406 Garyboyle . It&amp;rsquo ; s about 200 million kilometres from Earth , close to the orbit of Mars , and only about three kilometres in diameter . &amp;ldquo ; Still , &amp;rdquo ; he says , &amp;ldquo ; to have my name etched in the sky in catalogs , words can not describe . It&amp;rsquo ; s been two years and I still pinch myself . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;19878;TOOLONG ... title= " Astronomer Gary Boyle stands under the seven-metre wide moon model at the Canadian Museum of Nature . " alt= " Astronomer Gary Boyle stands under the seven-metre wide moon model at the Canadian Museum of Nature . " data-portal-copyright= " Bruce Deachman " **28;19914;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1429092 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tim Cole , an engineer , became an educator at the Canada Science and Technology Museum more than 25 years ago , where for much of his time he operated the 15-inch telescope that used to be in the Dominion Observatory on Carling Avenue . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Organizing astronomy programs for school children was &amp;ldquo ; a life changer , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &amp;ldquo ; I try to make science fun for kids , and that was a huge change for me and the way I looked at everything . I started looking at science as science instead of just stuff to know . And I still have the fascination for astronomy , for space . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He believes that an innate desire of human nature to expand feeds naturally into astronomy . &amp;ldquo ; That we can go more . Maybe not physically , but we can go more with a telescope . We can go more through an observatory . We can go more in our heads . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2012 , Cole traveled to Hawaii to witness the seven-hour transit of Venus across the face of the Sun , an event that won&amp;rsquo ; t happen again until 2117 . But his chief interest , and the celestial body most targeted by his handful of telescopes , remains the moon . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s different every hour , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &amp;ldquo ; For me , sunrise on the moon is a magical time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I am at peace under the sky . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Brian McCullough still has the Sears telescope he found under the tree 50 Christmases ago , but now prefers using the larger 11-inch one in the backyard observatory he built . Like Cole , he also typically directs his nighttime observations towards the moon , and he , too , often shares his knowledge with others . He counts among his possessions a small vial of lunar &amp;ldquo ; sawdust , &amp;rdquo ; a slice of lunar meteorite , and some pieces of Apollo 7&amp;rsquo ; s heat shield . His daughter , Emily , meanwhile , earned her PhD in astronomy from University of Western Ontario . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; People have to explore , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &amp;ldquo ; We want to know . What&amp;rsquo ; s on the other side of that hill ? Well , this just happened to be a 240,000 nautical mile distant hill that we wanted to see over . We could see it there and it was very tantalizing. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; But by going there , you&amp;rsquo ; re stretching people&amp;rsquo ; s minds . And even now , 50 years later , I can still stretch people&amp;rsquo ; s minds . Maybe some of the kids I&amp;rsquo ; m seeing now , the Guides and the Brownies , the Cubs and Scouts , they may be the first people to walk on the surface of Mars . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; To me , it&amp;rsquo ; s always about hope for the future . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; McCullough took a 20-year hiatus from astronomy following the first moon landing , as a career with the navy called . &amp;ldquo ; I got it out again in 1989 , and oh my God , I&amp;rsquo ; m setting up my telescope and it was just like I was setting it up on Christmas Day in 1968 , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; More than photographing it , he loves to sketch the moon , using whatever is at hand &amp;mdash ; a golf pencil , tempera paint powder , or even mini marshmallows. &amp;ldquo ; Sketching makes you look at the moon differently , &amp;rdquo ; he explains . &amp;ldquo ; And when you&amp;rsquo ; re working with computers , you have all the frustrations of your daily life . But when you&amp;rsquo ; re scribbling on paper , a lot of the stress gets shed . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;19944;TOOLONG ... title= " According to Ottawa astronomer Brian McCullough , you can use almost anything to create a likeness of the moon , including mini marshmallows. " alt= " According to Ottawa astronomer Brian McCullough , you can use almost anything to create a likeness of the moon , including mini marshmallows. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;19980;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1429167 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Most people who watched Armstrong make history didn&amp;rsquo ; t become astronomers , amateur or otherwise . Phil Seto had a long career in fundraising , while Josh Moon became a lawyer . Moon took his sons to visit the Kennedy Space Center at Cape Canaveral , where Apollo 11 and the other U.S. moon missions were launched , and confesses he got a little excited by Canadian astronaut David Saint-Jacques&amp;rsquo ; recent return from the International Space Station . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But both Seto and Moon keep abreast of space-related news stories in the mainstream media , and are fascinated by the possibility of the private sector , led by the likes of entrepreneurs Elon Musk and Richard Branson , making space travel a reality for ordinary ( wealthy ) citizens . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Maybe someday someone will set up a hotel on the moon , &amp;rdquo ; says Seto. &amp;ldquo ; To be able to say that you walked on the moon for a holiday ? Wow . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; My great-grandchildren may have the chance to go to an observation deck , &amp;rdquo ; says Moon , &amp;ldquo ; and from an environmental perspective , that&amp;rsquo ; s kind of cool to think that people will get the view of Earth that many astronauts come back with , saying it&amp;rsquo ; s beautiful but that we are alone . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Many astronauts have talked about how precious Earth is , &amp;rdquo ; he adds . &amp;ldquo ; Roberta Bondar . Chris Hadfield . American and Russian astronauts . So I think it&amp;rsquo ; s important we can get there to get that universal view of where we fit , which is that we&amp;rsquo ; ve pretty much got to live with this planet Earth . I think that&amp;rsquo ; s important . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **30;20010;TOOLONG " **37;20042;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;20081;TOOLONG ... is why we ca n't stop : ' Family of girl with rare blood disease driving bone marrow registry&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;20116;TOOLONG ... future in space starts with the moon , again&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;20151;TOOLONG ... prompts Pentagon to investigate claims that U.S. military has weaponized ticks&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Bruce DeachmanThu , 18 Jul 2019 14:43:15 **45;20186;TOOLONG ... **32;20233;TOOLONG future in space starts with the moon , againhttp : **35;20267;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;20304;TOOLONG ... title= " Edwin ' Buzz ' Aldrin on the surface of the moon , July 20 , 1969. " alt= " Edwin ' Buzz ' Aldrin on the surface of the moon , July 20 , 1969. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;20340;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1425641 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gilles Leclerc likes to joke that Canadian legs reached the lunar surface before U.S. astronaut Neil Armstrong&amp;rsquo ; s did . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The director general of the Canadian Space Agency is referring to the Canadian-designed and manufactured legs , or landing gear , on the lunar module Eagle that carried Armstrong and Edwin &amp;ldquo ; Buzz&amp;rdquo ; Aldrin to the moon&amp;rsquo ; s surface on July 20 , 1969 . The Eagle&amp;rsquo ; s legs touched down at 4:17 p.m . EST . Armstrong&amp;rsquo ; s historic footfall occurred at 10:56 p.m. , more than six-and-a-half hours later . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada , in fact , had a significant stake in NASA&amp;rsquo ; s efforts to land a man on the moon , a role that was inadvertently born a decade earlier , in February 1959 , when prime minister John Diefenbaker stood up in the House of Commons and announced the cancellation of the Avro Arrow program . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Arrow was an all-weather , supersonic jet aircraft , at the time the most advanced in the world , with computerized navigation and weapons systems and able to fly at twice the speed of sound . It was the Cold War response to the possibility of an attack by the Soviet Union on the U.S. that in all likelihood would come over the North Pole and through Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Arrow&amp;rsquo ; s demise threw many highly skilled Canadians out of work . The National Aeronautics and Space Administration ( NASA ) , meanwhile , had been hastily formed a year earlier , in 1958 , the U.S.&amp;rsquo ; s answer to the 1957 launch of the Sputnik satellite by the Soviets . The space race was on , but the Americans had a lot of catching up to do . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Enter about 30 newly unemployed Arrow engineers , who moved from Canada to the U.S. to work with NASA . Among them was Sarnia&amp;rsquo ; s Owen Maynard , who went on to work on Project Mercury and was later chief of systems engineering for the Apollo program and one of the architects of the lunar module . Joining him in the move south was Kamloops-born Jim Chamberlin , who was head of engineering for Project Mercury , project manager for Gemini , and a troubleshooter with Apollo . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another Canadian , physician William Carpentier , was the flight surgeon for the Apollo 11 and 13 missions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I was listening to Gene Kranz , &amp;rdquo ; recalls Leclerc , &amp;ldquo ; the legendary flight director during the Apollo 13 mission &amp;mdash ; &amp;lsquo ; Houston , we have a problem&amp;rsquo ; &amp;mdash ; and he said that the Canadians were one of the major contributors to the success of the Apollo mission . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Indeed , last month , with Apollo 11&amp;rsquo ; s 50th &lt;sup&gt; &lt;/sup&gt; anniversary on the horizon , Canada Post issued a stamp commemorating Canada&amp;rsquo ; s role in NASA&amp;rsquo ; s space program . The stamp was unveiled at aerospace developer and manufacturer H&amp;eacute ; roux-Devtek&amp;rsquo ; s plant in Longueuil , Que. , where the lunar module&amp;rsquo ; s legs were designed and built . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3 style= " text-align:left ; " &gt;&lt;strong&gt;FEA ... &lt;a href= " https : **33;20370;TOOLONG ... years ago , men landed on the moon . Ottawans share their memories of that monumental day&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; A half-century after the first man walked on the moon , Canada and the rest of the world have their sights set on returning , which would fulfill the final words of Apollo 17 commander Gene Cernan , the last man to walk on the moon , as he stepped off the lunar surface on Dec. 14 , 1972 : &amp;ldquo ; America&amp;rsquo ; s challenge of today has forged man&amp;rsquo ; s destiny of tomorrow . And , as we leave the Moon at Taurus&amp;ndash ; Littrow , we leave as we came and , God willing , as we shall return , with peace and hope for all mankind . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to the Canadian Space Agency&amp;rsquo ; s Leclerc , the 15 international space agencies , including Canada&amp;rsquo ; s , that form the International Space Exploration Coordination Group intend to return to the moon by 2024 . The first phase of that mission will be the Lunar Gateway , an orbiting science lab about one-fifth the size of the International Space Station , which will also serve as a rendezvous point for further lunar explorations and operations . New technologies will be tested at the Gateway , and the station , it is hoped , will facilitate missions to Mars . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada&amp;rsquo ; s involvement in the Gateway project will include its traditional role supplying robotics &amp;mdash ; in this case the development of the Canadarm 3 , which unlike previous iterations on the Shuttle and ISS , will be enabled by artificial intelligence . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The Gateway won&amp;rsquo ; t be crewed , &amp;rdquo ; explains Leclerc . &amp;ldquo ; There won&amp;rsquo ; t be astronauts on it 100 per cent of the time . It&amp;rsquo ; s simply an outpost for transfer to the lunar surface . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The moon is 1,000 times further than the ISS , so it&amp;rsquo ; s going to have to operate autonomously. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;20405;TOOLONG ... title= " This NASA image taken April 17 , 2015 , shows the Canadarm 2 reaching out to grapple the SpaceX Dragon cargo spacecraft and prepare it to be pulled into its port on the International Space Station ( ISS ) . " alt= " This NASA image taken April 17 , 2015 , shows the Canadarm 2 reaching out to grapple the SpaceX Dragon cargo spacecraft and prepare it to be pulled into its port on the International Space Station ( ISS ) . " data-portal-copyright= " HO " **28;20441;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1429025 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; As integrator for Gateway&amp;rsquo ; s robotics , he adds , Canada will help assemble and maintain the station , as well as conduct scientific experiments . He also expects to see Canada develop new niches for space , such as medical support . &amp;ldquo ; We see that there&amp;rsquo ; s a clear potential for a Canadian role , not only for maintaining the health of our astronauts , but also creating these technologies that will help with the health-care system . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He cites as an example the bio-analyzer that Canadian astronaut David Saint-Jacques carried with him on the ISS . The device can analyze blood , urine and saliva samples in real time ( think of the medical tricorder used in episodes of Star Trek , Leclerc says ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You can imagine the potential if one day you have this instrument available on your doctor&amp;rsquo ; s desk and he&amp;rsquo ; ll be able to perform live on you &amp;mdash ; you won&amp;rsquo ; t have to go get a blood sample and wait two weeks for results . This is the kind of technology that is needed in space and that we see having direct applications for all Canadians . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The ultimate goal , &amp;rdquo ; he adds , &amp;ldquo ; is to use the experience on ISS in lower earth orbit to go further , to go to the moon , around the moon , and then to Mars . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Mars is still the El Dorado , the ultimate destination for all of us partners in space exploration . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Going to the moon , he adds , echoing the words of NASA administrator Jim Bridenstine , is no longer a technical challenge ; it&amp;rsquo ; s a political one . But things look promising at the moment . At his Fourth of July speech in Washington , President Donald Trump indicated that the U.S. was willing to push the space envelope . &amp;ldquo ; I want you to know that we&amp;rsquo ; re going to be back on the moon very soon , &amp;rdquo ; he said to Apollo flight director Kranz , &amp;ldquo ; and , someday soon , we will plant the American flag on Mars . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Canadian Space Agency , meanwhile , has already secured $150 million over the next five years to support its Gateway commitments . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Additional funding for space exploration and travel , Leclerc says , will increasingly come from private companies like Elon Musk&amp;rsquo ; s SpaceX , Richard Branson&amp;rsquo ; s Virgin Galactic and Jeff Bezos&amp;rsquo ; s Blue Origin . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Between 1969 and 2019 , a lot has happened in terms of developing space for applications , for earth observations and telecommunications , &amp;rdquo ; Leclerc says , &amp;ldquo ; and now companies are making money from it , which was unbelievable just a few years ago . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;20471;TOOLONG ... title= " Gilles Leclerc , director general of the Canadian Space Agency . " alt= " Gilles Leclerc , director general of the Canadian Space Agency . " data-portal-copyright= " EricCarriere " **28;20507;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1429023 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although we&amp;rsquo ; re a far cry yet from space tourism , Leclerc is encouraged by the private sector&amp;rsquo ; s willingness to invest in space travel and exploration . It&amp;rsquo ; s just the latest step in a series of leaps for humankind. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The biggest success of the Apollo program was this view we got of our own planet &amp;mdash ; Earthrise on the moon , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &amp;ldquo ; The biggest benefit we got out of the Shuttle program was diversity , because for the first time there were women and people of colour , a lot of diversity . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; With the ISS , international cooperation , he says , was key , while the next step &amp;mdash ; returning to the moon and then getting to Mars , may see a push to a greater space economy and a permanent human presence outside our planet . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Everyone has their own reasons for space exploration . You can say that for humankind , it&amp;rsquo ; s because it&amp;rsquo ; s there , because we&amp;rsquo ; ve always explored the next hill , going to see what&amp;rsquo ; s over the horizon . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The Jeff Bezoses and Elon Musks of this world are saying that it&amp;rsquo ; s to make sure that humanity expands into the solar system in case something happens to earth . I&amp;rsquo ; m not of that religion , but it goes back to human nature . We explore because we have faith in the future . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **30;20537;TOOLONG " **37;20569;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;20608;TOOLONG ... is why we ca n't stop : ' Family of girl with rare blood disease driving bone marrow registry&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;20643;TOOLONG ... or starting exercise in middle age tied to longer life&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;20678;TOOLONG ... the Apollo 11 moon landing in NASA footage and photos&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Bruce DeachmanThu , 18 Jul 2019 11:43:59 **45;20713;TOOLONG ... NewsNationalWorldClark : Results of inquiry into Hassan Diab 's extradition must be made public nowhttp : **37;20760;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;20799;TOOLONG ... title= " Ottawa academic Hassan Diab spent 38 months imprisoned in France before finally being allowed to return to Canada , which had extradited him in as part of a deeply flawed case . " alt= " Ottawa academic Hassan Diab spent 38 months imprisoned in France before finally being allowed to return to Canada , which had extradited him in as part of a deeply flawed case . " data-portal-copyright= " Wayne Cuddington " **28;20835;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1430283 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; On the face of it , extradition may seem like a simple and rather convenient mechanism for a Canadian government to rid itself of unwanted and/or unsavoury individuals . What could possibly go wrong ? An old joke among extradition lawyers suggests that the only tricky question needing an answer is whether a window or an aisle seat will be required on the plane out of Canada . And yet , the recent entanglement with China following the detention in Vancouver of Huawei CFO Meng Wanzhou illustrates the pitfalls that wait in legal ambush for an unsuspecting government . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Extradition is a formal and legally binding process defined in parliamentary acts and treaties between partner nations . It is not a random happening determined by political whim , nor is it a casual matter to be kicked around like a diplomatic football . Yet , when things go wrong , they go horribly wrong and result in dreadful personal consequences for individual and their families . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;20865;TOOLONG ... title= " The extradition case against Huawei 's Financial Chief Meng Wanzhou shows the legal complexities of such proceedings . " alt= " The extradition case against Huawei 's Financial Chief Meng Wanzhou shows the legal complexities of such proceedings . " data-portal-copyright= " Lindsey Wasson " **28;20901;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1430285 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hassan Diab was arrested 11 years ago by the RCMP at the request of the French government , which believed it could build a case against him as author of a 1980 bomb attack outside a Paris synagogue . Diab lost his job , was held in detention for several months , then subjected to stringent bail conditions for six years . Following numerous court hearings and legal appeals , Hassan Diab was extradited to France in November 2014. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After more than three years , it became clear that none of the so-called evidence was reliable , that Diab was in fact studying in Beirut at the time of the 1980 bombing , and that there remained no feasible avenues of investigation . Diab was released , without charge , in January 2018 , after 38 months in solitary confinement . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The French prosecutors , desperate for a conviction , appealed that decision . A year-and-a-half later , there has been no final resolution and Hassan Diab continues to bear the weight of this miscarriage of justice . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Back in Canada , Diab and his many supporters began a sustained lobbying effort to determine two things : what went wrong in the whole , sad process ; and how could Canada&amp;rsquo ; s Extradition Act be changed to ensure that others would not fall foul of inadequate legal mechanisms . The prime minister , the foreign minister , and the justice minister all agreed that lessons had to be learned and in May 2018 an &amp;ldquo ; external review&amp;rdquo ; was established , to be conducted by former Ontario deputy attorney general Murray Segal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Segal was charged with determining whether Department of Justice officials followed the law and appropriate departmental procedures in carrying out Diab&amp;rsquo ; s extradition , and whether Canada should raise specific concerns with the French government about his treatment in that country . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The need for a complete public inquiry became even more urgent when a CBC investigation revealed that French fingerprint evidence exonerating Diab was not shared with Canadian officials . Subsequently , similar evidence was established by the RCMP and provided to Department of Justice lawyers who chose to conceal it from both Diab&amp;rsquo ; s lawyer and from the judge conducting the extradition hearing . It is reasonable to conclude that the Department of Justice effectively condemned Hassan Diab to be extradited to France . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What , then , is causing further delay ? Segal delivered his report to Justice Minister David Lametti in May 2019 . Allowing for translation , and even assuming a need for time to prepare a suitable government response , almost two months have gone by and the Segal Report has yet to made public . There are concerns that , when it does ultimately appear , it may well be incomplete or heavily redacted. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Segal Report could scarcely come at a more sensitive time . The increased attention to extradition resulting from the Meng Wanzhou case , and recent news of a U.S. request for the extradition of a retired McGill University professor , mean that the Canadian government will be more than reluctant to open an extensive public debate on the Canadian Extradition Act . With an election looming , there is every reason to believe that the Segal Report will be further delayed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When it is eventually made public , there is zero chance that it will lead to a full public inquiry into Hassan Diab&amp;rsquo ; s case . Justice will continue to be denied and some inconvenient truths will remain buried . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **29;20931;TOOLONG Clark&lt;/strong&gt; served as Secretary General of Amnesty International ( Canada ) in 1999 . He is an active member of the Hassan Diab Support Committee and continues to work as a human rights activist . &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN CITIZEN OPINIONS : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;20962;TOOLONG ... The EPIC hospital-records system is like a tour of the Irish countryside&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;20997;TOOLONG ... NASA/Bill Ingalls " data-portal-copyright= " HANDOUT " **28;21187;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1418321 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;21217;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; David Saint-Jacques returned to Earth on Monday after more than six months aboard the International Space Station . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The native of Saint-Lambert , Que. , has set a record for the longest single spaceflight by a Canadian at 204 days . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Canadian astronaut was joined by NASA astronaut Anne McClain and Russian cosmonaut Oleg Kononenko aboard a Soyuz capsule that landed in Kazakhstan . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; The Canadian PressTue , 25 Jun 2019 19:24:06 **45;21253;TOOLONG ... Joseph 's Training School abuse : Why papal apology matters to survivor , 60 years laterhttp : **35;21300;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;21337;TOOLONG ... title= " Pope Francis has said the church must take ' concrete ' action to address child sexual abuse . Critics say that has n't happened . " alt= " Pope Francis has said the church must take ' concrete ' action to address child sexual abuse . Critics say that has n't happened . " data-portal-copyright= " ALBERTO PIZZOLI " **28;21373;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1376957 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The dimly lit conference room looked like so many others &amp;mdash ; a long table with nameplates and microphones , surrounded by drab olive drapes and beige carpet . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On this particular Thursday , however , two things stood out : a painting of the Virgin Mary and Baby Jesus on the wall behind the head table , and the speaker whose back they appeared to be looking at : Pope Francis . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wearing his white cassock , the Pope faced the tiered rows of cardinals , archbishops , bishops and other clergy in their respective plumage , and in under two minutes read his opening address , delivering his words in a dry monotone and barely lifting his eyes to look at his audience . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The occasion was the Meeting on the Protection of Minors in the Church , a four-day summit held at the Vatican in February for Roman Catholic officials to address the issue of the abuse of minors by church clergy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In the face of the scourge of sexual abuse by churchmen to the detriment of minors , &amp;rdquo ; the Pope said , &amp;ldquo ; I have decided to meet you , patriarchs , cardinals , archbishops , bishops , religious superiors and leaders , so that together we might listen to the Holy Spirit and , with docility , with its guidance , hear the cry of the little ones who plead for justice . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He demanded that action be taken : &amp;ldquo ; The holy people of God look to us and expect from us not simple and predictable condemnations , but concrete and effective measures to be put into place . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We need to be concrete . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;21403;TOOLONG ... title= " Pope Francis . " alt= " Pope Francis . " data-portal-copyright= " Andrew Medichini " **28;21439;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1376974 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the end , however , concrete measures were not taken . Delivering a Sunday mass to mark the end of the conference , the Pope laid much of the blame for the abuse on the devil&amp;rsquo ; s doorstep : &amp;ldquo ; The consecrated person , chosen by God to guide souls to salvation , lets himself be subjugated by his own frailty , or by his own illness , thus becoming a tool of Satan , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;He also challenged the world at large to solve the issue . " There are n't enough explanations about child abuse , " he said . " Humbly and bravely we must recognize that we stand before the mystery of evil , which rages against the weakest because they are an image of Jesus . This is why in the church there is now a growing awareness of having not only to try and stem the most serious abuses with disciplinary measures and civil and canonic trials , but also to face the phenomenon decisively both inside and outside the church . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It feels it is called to fight this evil that touches the centre of its mission to announce the Gospel to the little ones and to protect them from the ravenous wolves . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;The National Catholic Reporter , an American newspaper that reports on matters involving the Roman Catholic Church , described the Pontiff 's concluding remarks as " a melodramatic avoidance of the truth . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; David McCann wasn&amp;rsquo ; t particularly surprised by the church&amp;rsquo ; s response . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For decades , he&amp;rsquo ; s been trying to get an official apology for the abuse that he and hundreds of other boys suffered at a Catholic training school in Alfred , about 70 kilometres east of Ottawa . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s still the same &amp;lsquo ; **34;21469;TOOLONG ... sentiment , but no commitment on their part to apologize , &amp;rdquo ; McCann said recently from his home in Vancouver . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You can have all the pretty words you want , all the promises , all the commissions , and bring people in from around the world , but if you can&amp;rsquo ; t spend 10 minutes saying &amp;lsquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m sorry , &amp;rsquo ; I don&amp;rsquo ; t think you&amp;rsquo ; re that serious . I don&amp;rsquo ; t think they know how to deal with it . It&amp;rsquo ; s a bunch of old men in dresses who don&amp;rsquo ; t have a clue . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It has been six decades since McCann was abused , and 30 years since he went public with his story . His decision to do so opened a floodgate of similar allegations from more than 1,600 former wards of either St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s Training School for Boys , in Alfred , or St. John&amp;rsquo ; s in Uxbridge , about 70 kilometres northeast of Toronto . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; More than 120 perpetrators were identified by former students , but by the time McCann came forward , many of the accused had died , while others were deemed too infirm to stand trial . Still , almost 200 charges were laid against nearly 30 De La Salle Brothers of the Christian Schools and Christian Brothers of Ontario , the Catholic religious lay orders that ran the schools , and other staff . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;21505;TOOLONG ... title= " David McCann as a boy . " alt= " David McCann as a boy . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;21541;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1366061 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; At the time , it was the largest such scandal to come out of Canada , although similar stories have since surfaced all over the world , most notably , perhaps , the hundreds of cases of abuse in Massachusetts uncovered by the Boston Globe in 2002 and depicted in the 2015 film Spotlight . The Globe&amp;rsquo ; s investigation led to even more cases coming to light . Only last month , former Australian Archbishop and Cardinal George Pell was sentenced to six years in jail for molesting two choir boys in the 1990s . Pell is the highest-ranking Catholic official to be convicted of child sexual abuse . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a 2014 interview with La Repubblica newspaper in Italy , meanwhile , Pope Francis said that about two per cent of Catholic clergy members &amp;mdash ; or roughly 8,000 &amp;mdash ; including priests , bishops and cardinals , were pedophiles . The Pope referred to them as a &amp;ldquo ; leprosy&amp;rdquo ; within the Church . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among the 16 convicted of abuse at St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s and St. John&amp;rsquo ; s was McCann&amp;rsquo ; s chief tormentor , Brother Joseph , a.k.a . Lucien Dagenais , who was given a five-year sentence after being found guilty of seven counts of indecent assault , six counts of assault causing bodily harm and two counts of buggery . Throughout the court proceedings , Dagenais showed no remorse and maintained his innocence . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In passing sentence , Justice Hector Soubliere noted : &amp;ldquo ; We built walls around that school : not of brick and mortar , but of much stronger material &amp;mdash ; walls of silence and indifference . I&amp;rsquo ; m even tempted to say walls of ignorance . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And while the memory of the crimes committed by Dagenais and others has , in many cases , been blurred by the passage of time , their rough edges worn smooth , the physical and psychological damage inflicted on the boys placed in their care often remains . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;21571;TOOLONG ... title= " The former St. Joseph 's Training School for Boys , in Alfred , Ont. " alt= " The former St. Joseph 's Training School for Boys , in Alfred , Ont. " data-portal-copyright= " Bruce Deachman " **28;21607;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1366060 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Now 72 , McCann says he&amp;rsquo ; s never really healed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I still pay the price , &amp;rdquo ; he says , noting that he lives alone and hasn&amp;rsquo ; t been in a serious relationship in years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He well recalls lying frightened in the dark , one of about 50 youngsters in a room of steel-framed beds laid end-to-end , row upon row , and hearing footsteps approach . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was December 1958 , and McCann , then a 12-year-old delinquent from Kingston , had , along with two friends , been arrested the previous month after the trio went on a one-night crime spree , breaking into and robbing eight Kingston businesses of , among other things , $300 in cash , a $50 watch and some sticks of dynamite . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His accomplices got probation , but McCann , already no stranger to the courts , was sent to St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I believe that at St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s Training School there will be discipline and the instruction given to him that will try and correct his ways , &amp;rdquo ; said Judge James Garvin. &amp;ldquo ; We hope so anyway . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A psychologist , Dr. Oscar Karabenow , thought otherwise , but his report to McCann&amp;rsquo ; s probation officer arrived too late : McCann had already been taken from school by a police officer and driven to St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A scrawny , freckle-faced boy with big ears , McCann wasn&amp;rsquo ; t tough . True , he frequently skipped school , but often to go read at the Kingston Public Library . He had never been away from home before . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s , a provincially funded reformatory , he found an imposing and menacing institution &amp;mdash ; a stone building he recalls today as cold , smelly and dank . The charges there were overseen by men in black robes , members of the De La Salle Brothers of the Christian Schools , an order with roots in early-1700s France . The Brothers had petitioned the Ontario government to create a school for delinquent Catholic boys &amp;mdash ; St. John&amp;rsquo ; s Industrial School , which opened in 1895. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s opened in 1933 , with three dozen boys shipped there from St. John&amp;rsquo ; s . At the school&amp;rsquo ; s opening ceremony , Ontario premier George Henry remarked that &amp;ldquo ; No child should emerge from industrial schools without a better outlook on life . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Speaking on behalf of the Christian Brothers , Brother Martial , the superior , pointed to the inscription on the lintel stone above the main entrance , which read , in French and English , &amp;ldquo ; Young man , I say , Arise . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The command is divine , &amp;rdquo ; he noted . &amp;ldquo ; We are but the humble instruments , but if in the passage of years , we can but point to one man whom we were permitted to help on to the right road , then would this investment of labour and of love not be in vain . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; William Martin , the province&amp;rsquo ; s minister of public welfare added : &amp;ldquo ; The passion , zest and enthusiasm of youth must be safeguarded and guided into the proper channels if the future of civilization is to be made safe and secure . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While many of the boys in their charge had been sent to St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s for criminal misdemeanors , not all had run afoul of the law . Upwards of 30 per cent of the boys were runaways from residential schools , while others were &amp;ldquo ; unmanageables&amp;rdquo ; who simply proved too much for the parents or guardians who sent them there . At any one time , there were about 160 boys at the school , some sent there from as far away as Kenora. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The purpose of the schools , as outlined in the Training Schools Act of 1937 , was &amp;ldquo ; to provide the boys or girls admitted therein with a mental , moral and vocational education and training and with profitable employment . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But former Renfrew North Liberal MPP Sean Conway recalled in 1996 in the Ontario legislature the school&amp;rsquo ; s reputation as &amp;ldquo ; synonymous with some kind of Alcatraz&amp;rdquo ; when he was a youngster . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; On more days than I can remember , &amp;rdquo ; he said , &amp;ldquo ; in the elementary school , the very Catholic elementary school to which I was sent &amp;hellip ; we were told , &amp;lsquo ; Be bad and you&amp;rsquo ; ll go to Alfred . ' &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3 style= " text-align:left ; " &gt; **33;21637;TOOLONG : &lt;a href= " https : **33;21672;TOOLONG ... apologies matter : ' Words can move mountains ' &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; On this particular night , McCann listened and the footsteps stopped at his bed , and a tall figure leaned over and whispered in the young boy&amp;rsquo ; s ear , &amp;ldquo ; Viens avec moi . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; McCann didn&amp;rsquo ; t understand French , so Brother Joseph mumbled the phrase again , this time in English . &amp;ldquo ; Come with me. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Known to the boys as The Hook for a table saw accident he&amp;rsquo ; d suffered years earlier , leaving him with just his middle finger and stump of his thumb on his left hand , Brother Joseph took McCann to a room off the dormitory , and raped him . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I don&amp;rsquo ; t remember all the details , &amp;rdquo ; McCann admits . &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s something I don&amp;rsquo ; t try to think about very often . But it was repeated several times . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was repeated not just with McCann , but hundreds and hundreds of boys , both at St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s and St. John&amp;rsquo ; s . The abuse came in all forms : sexual , physical and psychological . &amp;ldquo ; I saw grown men &amp;mdash ; beefy guys &amp;mdash ; beat little frail 11- and 12-year-old kids into a pulp and unconsciousness , &amp;rdquo ; recalls McCann. &amp;ldquo ; They beat them black and blue . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; They ripped the kid out of me , &amp;rdquo ; said former St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s ward Gary Sullivan in 1990 , according to journalist Darcy Henton&amp;rsquo ; s 1995 book , Boys Don&amp;rsquo ; t Cry . &amp;ldquo ; It was nothing but absolute terror . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the Recorder&amp;rsquo ; s Report Concerning Physical and Sexual Abuse At St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s and St. John&amp;rsquo ; s Training Schools for Boys , prepared by Benjamin C. Hoffman and presented to the Reconciliation Process Implementation Committee in 1995 , a number of former students spoke anonymously of their experiences at the schools . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I have hated , hated all my life , &amp;rdquo ; said one who was sent to St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s in 1938 , five years after it opened , when he was nine . &amp;ldquo ; I can not express love , I have never known love . My son loves me and I don&amp;rsquo ; t show him love . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I saw many young children beaten up and strapped , &amp;rdquo ; recalled another . &amp;ldquo ; I saw Brother &amp;mdash ; &amp;mdash ; wake up young children and take them to a room to sexually assault them . I saw children handcuffed to a pillar in the basement . They would be pushed and kicked . I saw Brother &amp;mdash ; &amp;mdash ; use a pool table stick to hit children if they would not have anal sex with him . Children were given cold showers then strapped . If I told any Brothers that another Brother tried to have sex with me , I would be strapped . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Former student Gerry Sirois described St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s to Henton as &amp;ldquo ; like Dachau with games . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;21707;TOOLONG ... title= " David McCann at a 1991 news conference . " alt= " David McCann at a 1991 news conference . " data-portal-copyright= " Wayne Hiebert " **28;21743;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1366056 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sixty years ago , David McCann felt alone and abandoned , bewildered and confused by the fact that his family didn&amp;rsquo ; t protect him . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I understand now that they had no way to protect me , but it took me a couple of years to sort that one out , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &amp;ldquo ; But kids don&amp;rsquo ; t have the skills set to deal with these things &amp;mdash ; a lot of adults don&amp;rsquo ; t , either . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His mother tried to help . While visiting her son once at St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s , McCann broke down and told her about the abuse he&amp;rsquo ; d suffered . &amp;ldquo ; My mom could wheedle anything out of me , &amp;rdquo ; he says . But when she returned home to Kingston and raised the subject with her parish priest , she was threatened with excommunication if she continued to spread such horrible lies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So the abuse continued , and McCann tamped his emotions down . &amp;ldquo ; I didn&amp;rsquo ; t dream about it , &amp;rdquo ; he recalls . &amp;ldquo ; I basically buried it , closed the door , nailed it shut , plastered it over and let the memories &amp;hellip ; they were just there . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I didn&amp;rsquo ; t deal with it at all . I didn&amp;rsquo ; t talk to people about it . I think what it did was it made me not trust people . Here I&amp;rsquo ; d been raised in the church where these priests and bishops and Christian Brothers and nuns were all these really righteous people , and then you see them doing something like that . I just learned really to not trust anybody . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;21773;TOOLONG ... title= " Brother Leo ( Leo Monette ) on a field trip with the St. Joseph 's Training School for Boys ' gym team , in 1959 or 1960. " alt= " Brother Leo ( Leo Monette ) on a field trip with the St. Joseph 's Training School for Boys ' gym team , in 1959 or 1960. " data-portal-copyright= " Courtesy of Gerry Belecque " **28;21809;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1366048 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; McCann&amp;rsquo ; s reactions to his abuse &amp;mdash ; his feelings of betrayal that his family didn&amp;rsquo ; t help , his lifelong lack of trust in others and his attempts to bury his experiences rather than confront them &amp;mdash ; are not uncommon , says University of Ottawa psychology professor and clinical psychologist Elisa Romano , who specializes in childhood maltreatment and its impact on development . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Studies , Romano said , suggest that men who have suffered sexual abuse don&amp;rsquo ; t tell anyone about it for an average of 20 years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Imagine living with something that is such an invasion of you as a person for so long . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Apart from the inherent power imbalance that gave the Brothers an upper hand over their wards , Romano adds that many of the boys at training schools such as St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s and St. John&amp;rsquo ; s would have faced the additional burden of not being listened to if they spoke out against their keepers. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; All children are vulnerable , but these particular children may have been even more vulnerable because perhaps they wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t have been believed , perhaps they would have been perceived as lying because these are boys who had significant behavioural challenges . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; If you&amp;rsquo ; ve been hurt by people that you trusted , &amp;rdquo ; she adds , &amp;ldquo ; whether it was people in this institution that were supposed to look out for you , or whether it was people in your neighbourhood or in your family , you start to develop a sense that it isn&amp;rsquo ; t safe to be around people because they do hurt you , and that could definitely affect your ability to trust , your ability to make yourself vulnerable . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;21839;TOOLONG ... title= " Gerry Belecque , far right , with some other students at St. Joseph 's Training School for Buys in Alfred , Ont. in 1959 or 1960. " alt= " Gerry Belecque , far right , with some other students at St. Joseph 's Training School for Buys in Alfred , Ont. in 1959 or 1960. " data-portal-copyright= " Courtesy of Gerry Belecque " **28;21875;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1366053 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gerry Belecque is among the St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s alumnus who for decades buried his experiences there . The North Bay resident didn&amp;rsquo ; t tell even his wife about it until McCann went public with his story in 1989 , exactly 30 years after Belecque arrived at St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s , and 26 years after he and his wife were married . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; She grew up across the street and we&amp;rsquo ; ve known each other since we were four , &amp;rdquo ; he says , &amp;ldquo ; so I expect she had been told by her parents that I ended up there . So I think she knew I was a bad boy and went somewhere , but I never told her where . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; And I never told my parents what happened there , ever . I didn&amp;rsquo ; t want anyone thinking I was a &amp;lsquo ; faggot . &amp;rsquo ; Those were the words that were used : &amp;lsquo ; faggot&amp;rsquo ; or an &amp;lsquo ; abuser , &amp;rsquo ; and if you were abused , you were going to abuse . And as you get older , the more you learn about life , the less you want to say about it . That&amp;rsquo ; s the way I felt &amp;mdash ; it was in the past , it was gone . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;21905;TOOLONG ... title= " Gerry Belecque , far left in front row , played hockey on the St. Joseph 's Training School for Boys team , in Alfred , Ont. , durting the winter of 1959-60. " alt= " Gerry Belecque , far left in front row , played hockey on the St. Joseph 's Training School for Boys team , in Alfred , Ont. , durting the winter of 1959-60. " data-portal-copyright= " Courtesy of Gerry Belecque " **28;21941;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1366054 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Belecque was just 14 when he was sent to St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s in May 1959 . An occasional truant from school who ran with a couple of older boys , he first got into trouble for stealing chocolate bars from a delivery truck when he was 13 . He violated his parole by hitchhiking to Toronto one night with the same boys and acting as a lookout while they attempted to break into a store there . A judge decided that a stay at St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s was in order . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The abuse , Belecque recalls , began before he even arrived at St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wearing a leather jacket and Wellington boots , his hair Brylcreemed like Elvis , Belecque was handcuffed to the passenger door of his probation officer&amp;rsquo ; s car for the drive to Alfred . Belecque says that as they drove along Hwy. 17 , the officer began to fondle him . When Belecque tried to push the advance away , &amp;ldquo ; then he grabbed my hand , wanting me to fondle him . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At a rest stop halfway to Ottawa , the abuse continued in a washroom , where Belecque , now handcuffed by the wrist to his probation officer , was forced to grab the PO&amp;rsquo ; s penis while he relieved himself . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Belecque thought that things had improved when he finally arrived at the school and was greeted by one of the Brothers , wearing a black frock and white collar . &amp;ldquo ; I thought , &amp;lsquo ; This is going to be easy , ' &amp;rdquo ; Belecque recalls . &amp;ldquo ; &amp;lsquo ; This is going to be a cakewalk . I&amp;rsquo ; m not in jail here , and there are no fences around it . It&amp;rsquo ; s just a school . &amp;rsquo ; There&amp;rsquo ; s a statue of St. Joseph out front , holding a child , and above the door are the words &amp;lsquo ; Young men arise . &amp;rsquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; So OK , I&amp;rsquo ; m a bad kid , and I&amp;rsquo ; m going to go there and they&amp;rsquo ; re going to straighten me out . I&amp;rsquo ; m not going to give them any trouble . I&amp;rsquo ; m here now and I&amp;rsquo ; m going to do good .
