I always respected these people , so I thought &amp;lsquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; ll get what I give . &amp;rsquo ; So that&amp;rsquo ; s my first impression . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Early on in his 16-month stay at St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s , he hung back and watched others , but soon became involved in activities there , particularly athletics , competing in gymnastics and hockey . Boys who played on the school&amp;rsquo ; s sports teams faced less abuse than others , but no one was immune . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Belecque describes Brother Leo ( Leopold Monette , who was eventually sentenced to five years after being charged with 21 counts of assault causing bodily harm , eight counts of indecent assault , one count of buggery and one count of gross indecency ) as tough and unpredictable . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; He was ex-army , and wanted the boys to excel at sports . But he was the kind of guy who was your friend one minute , and the next you didn&amp;rsquo ; t know . He&amp;rsquo ; d punch you in the face and you&amp;rsquo ; d think , &amp;lsquo ; Oh , shit , what did I do ? ' &amp;rdquo ; While working in the kitchen once , handing out meals , Belecque was beaten for sneaking an extra slice of toast to one of the older boys . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;21971;TOOLONG ... title= " Gerry Belecque , second from left in the top row , on the baseball team at St. Joseph 's Training School for Boys , in Alfred , Ont. , in 1959 or 1960. " alt= " Gerry Belecque , second from left in the top row , on the baseball team at St. Joseph 's Training School for Boys , in Alfred , Ont. , in 1959 or 1960. " data-portal-copyright= " Courtesy of Gerry Belecque " **28;22007;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1366047 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Belecque recalls another Brother beating boys with canes , hockey sticks and pool cues. &amp;ldquo ; He&amp;rsquo ; d let you have it , across the head or face , every time . He was a little wee runt of a guy , and he&amp;rsquo ; d kick the shit out of you at the drop of a hat . He beat me up a few times , for like a ball going off the pool table . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That same Brother , Belecque says , ran the maintenance shop behind the school . The curtains were always closed and Belecque walked in one day to find one of the boys performing oral sex on the Brother . &amp;ldquo ; That was my first encounter with that , and ( the Brother ) kicked the living shit out of me &amp;mdash ; and him , the boy , for not locking the door . He kicked us and beat us up . And you&amp;rsquo ; d be walking around with a black eye and nobody would ask &amp;lsquo ; Where&amp;rsquo ; d you get that ? &amp;rsquo ; Everybody minded their own business . No one wanted to get into anyone else&amp;rsquo ; s stuff . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A week after that beating , Belecque was working in the shop when the same Brother approached him from behind and rubbed his penis against Belecque&amp;rsquo ; s back for a few minutes . &amp;ldquo ; I think I got off lucky , &amp;rdquo ; Belecque says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was Brother Gabriel ( Aim&amp;eacute ; Bergeron , two years less a day for one count of indecent assault and one count of buggery ) who was charged with sexually abusing Belecque. &amp;ldquo ; And he was in charge of the altar boys , &amp;rdquo ; Belecque says . &amp;ldquo ; Give me a break . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; What goes through these people&amp;rsquo ; s minds ? &amp;rsquo ; he wonders . &amp;ldquo ; It happened , and you go &amp;lsquo ; OK , it happened . That&amp;rsquo ; s the way that person is. &amp;rsquo ; But after a while when I got out of there , I thought , &amp;lsquo ; Why ? Why do they do this ? What&amp;rsquo ; s the matter with these guys ? And I can never get an answer in my mind . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; d like to call people up who are abusers and ask them that question . What are you thinking ? What goes through your mind when you&amp;rsquo ; re doing that ? Are you thinking that you&amp;rsquo ; re helping these kids by showing them what sex is about ? You&amp;rsquo ; re religious , you&amp;rsquo ; re Catholic , and you believe these people represent Christ or God on Earth or whatever , and then they do this , and you think &amp;lsquo ; There is no God . ' &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Belecque returned to North Bay from Alfred in September 1960 , 16 years old and a loner . Around the same time , reports of abuse at St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s began to circulate in official circles . In an August 1960 letter to Ottawa archbishop M.J. Lemieux , Archie Graham , deputy minister of Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s Department of Reform Institutions , wrote , &amp;ldquo ; We know that you will appreciate our desire to take the necessary action before the growing number of complaints received by us from parents , welfare workers and others reaches such a magnitude that the matter becomes public knowledge , in which event the school , this department , and the Church can only suffer . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And still the abuse continued . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Back in North Bay , Belecque married and had two children , but his anger remained . He drank , did drugs and gambled. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This was not an uncommon outcome for former students . According to The Recorder&amp;rsquo ; s Report concerning abuse at the schools , &amp;ldquo ; The men as a group are bitter , poor , marginalized and lack self esteem . &amp;rdquo ; Upwards of 80 per cent of them maintained that some or all of the problems they faced after leaving training school were related to the abuse they suffered there . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It wasn&amp;rsquo ; t until the mid 1970s , when he decided to finish high school and then earn his BA in sociology and psychology , that Belecque recognized the source of many of his troubles . &amp;ldquo ; I took those subjects because I wanted to know what was going on in my mind , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &amp;ldquo ; It was personal . And a lot of those things in the books applied to me . I was neurotic , I was psychotic. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; So that&amp;rsquo ; s when I started to see a psychiatrist. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But he told no one else , not even his wife , out of embarrassment and a fear that others might think him a &amp;ldquo ; weirdo. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; David McCann similarly did not deal with the abuse he suffered . Released from St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s after two years , he moved into a summer cottage on Lake Ontario , just west of Kingston , where he lived alone for two years before returning home to his parents&amp;rsquo ; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I didn&amp;rsquo ; t deal with it . I just buried it , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &amp;ldquo ; As a 12-year-old when this happens to you , you just go , &amp;lsquo ; What did I do to deserve this ? &amp;rsquo ; But it took years before I figured out that I was in no way to blame , that the system was to blame . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Part of that realization came in 1989 , when he saw a news report on TV about the then-emerging story of child abuse by the Christian Brothers of Ireland in Canada , at Mount Cashel Boys School , an orphanage in St. John&amp;rsquo ; s , Newfoundland . The news floored him . He wasn&amp;rsquo ; t alone , he realized . And in the case of Mount Cashel , it looked as though something might be done about it : Newfoundland interim premier Tom Rideout had appointed a Royal Commission to investigate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;22037;TOOLONG ... title= " A carpentry shop on the property of what once was St. Joseph 's Training School for Boys , in Alfred , Ont. " alt= " A carpentry shop on the property of what once was St. Joseph 's Training School for Boys , in Alfred , Ont. " data-portal-copyright= " Bruce Deachman " **28;22073;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1366059 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; And so McCann , who by coincidence had been speaking with Toronto Star reporter Darcy Henton about an unrelated story , came forward with his allegations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Other victims surfaced , and McCann and a few others , including Belecque , helped found Helpline , and advocacy group for former students of St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s and St. John&amp;rsquo ; s . Rather than pursue a class-action lawsuit , the group elected to work with the religious groups that ran the schools , the archdioceses of Ottawa and Toronto and the Ontario government to reach a reconciliation agreement with the hundreds of victims who came forward . Ultimately , although the Christian Brothers of Ontario , who ran St. John&amp;rsquo ; s , refused to participate , the other sides agreed to a $16-million settlement . Reports varied , but most recipients received in the neighbourhood of $20,000. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A 1996 story in the Citizen looked at what some of the former students did with their settlement . Some opened businesses and at least one had dental work done , to replace the teeth that the Brothers had knocked out . Still others spent theirs on drugs or alcohol . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For many , though , the settlement arrived too late : 32 of the victims had committed suicide . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; McCann , meanwhile , continued to seek apologies . As part of the reconciliation agreement , the Ottawa and Toronto archdioceses apologized , with Ottawa archbishop Marcel Gervais doing so at a noon mass at Notre Dame Cathedral in April 1996 . For some , the act helped . McCann at the time said , &amp;ldquo ; It puts a lot of devils to rest . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; McCann also found some unexpected encouragement during the mass . Seated in the pew in front of him were a woman , a man and a young girl , perhaps five or six years old . The girl , McCann recalls , repeatedly turned to her mother to ask why the man behind her was crying . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Now there&amp;rsquo ; s a part of the Catholic mass where you turn to your neighbour and greet them , &amp;rdquo ; says McCann. &amp;ldquo ; And the mother turned around and looked directly at me and said , &amp;lsquo ; Mr. McCann , I know who you are , and I want to thank you and all the men with you . You&amp;rsquo ; ve made the world a safer place for my child . &amp;rsquo ; And if you want validation for whether it was worth doing , in that one moment that woman told me that everything we did was worthwhile . There&amp;rsquo ; s another child who won&amp;rsquo ; t go through what we did . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But others were not as comforted . One former student , Paul Gagnon , stood and shouted at Gervais : &amp;ldquo ; I don&amp;rsquo ; t want an apology from you . I want an apology from the brothers that ruined my life . I want an apology from those two dogs &amp;mdash ; those two pedophiles. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another former student , Jimmy Toal , confessed his circumspect resignation . &amp;ldquo ; All of this brings back memories , &amp;rdquo ; he told the Citizen at the time . &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s never really done . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;22103;TOOLONG ... title= " Brother Etienne -- Etienne Fortin -- with a dog and one of his students , at St. Joseph 's Training School for Boys in Alfred , Ont. in 1959 or 1960. " alt= " Brother Etienne -- Etienne Fortin -- with a dog and one of his students , at St. Joseph 's Training School for Boys in Alfred , Ont. in 1959 or 1960. " data-portal-copyright= " Courtesy of Gerry Belecque " **28;22139;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1366055 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; As part of the agreement , Ontario premier Mike Harris was to &amp;ldquo ; propose an all-party resolution in the legislature , apologizing for and condemning the abuse . &amp;rdquo ; He refused , however , instead leaving the apology to attorney general Charles Harnick to deliver while Harris was out of town . McCann responded by taking Harris to court . Ultimately , it was premier Dalton McGuinty who , in 2004 , offered an apology on behalf of the province . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I don&amp;rsquo ; t like all of Dalton McGuinty&amp;rsquo ; s policies , &amp;rdquo ; says McCann , &amp;ldquo ; but on 10:30 on Election night , after he&amp;rsquo ; d been elected , his senior aide called me on the phone and said &amp;lsquo ; I just spoke to Dalton , and he asked me to ask you to give him 60 to 90 days to assume the reins of power , and he will rise in the legislature . &amp;rsquo ; And he did rise in the legislature and spoke , not only as the premier , but he begged the indulgence of the House after everybody else had spoken , and he rose and spoke as a Catholic and as a father . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I give the guy a lot of credit . The entire time he spoke , he looked at us , and the second time he spoke , he talked about sitting at his breakfast table and looking at his 12-year-old son , and he said , &amp;lsquo ; I can&amp;rsquo ; t imagine that happening to my son , and as a father and a Catholic , I apologize . I&amp;rsquo ; m appalled . ' &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;22169;TOOLONG ... title= " David McCann in Vancouver in 2017. " alt= " David McCann in Vancouver in 2017. " data-portal-copyright= " Jason Payne " **28;22205;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1366107 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 1990 , as his public allegations began to snowball , McCann and 15 other survivors hand-delivered a letter to Angelo Palmas , Apostolic Pro-Nuncio to Canada , at his Rockcliffe residence , asking the then-Pope , John Paul II , for an apology . McCann never received a reply . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two Popes later , in 2017 , McCann travelled to the Vatican where , at the behest of the Pope , he was seen by Father Hans Zollner , professor of psychology and current president of the Centre for Child Protection at the Pontifical Gregorian University in Rome . The pair spoke for a couple of hours . &amp;ldquo ; ( Zollner ) was hopeful that Francis would apologize , &amp;rdquo ; recalls McCann , &amp;ldquo ; but he was very clear that he had no authority to speak for the Pope . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Over the years , popes have spoken on the issue of abuse of minors by clergy . In a 2002 address to U.S. Cardinals , Pope John Paul II said , &amp;ldquo ; People need to know that there is no place in the priesthood and religious life for those who would harm the young . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2008 , Pope Benedict apologized for the sexual abuse of children by priests and clergymen in Australia , noting , &amp;ldquo ; I am deeply sorry for the pain and suffering the victims have endured and I assure them that , as their pastor , I too share in their suffering . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Benedict was criticized , however , for delivering the apology to a group of Catholic bishops , seminarians and novices , rather than victims . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two years later , at a mass at Westminster Cathedral in London , Benedict again acknowledged and somewhat apologized for the suffering caused by the church , saying , &amp;ldquo ; I think of the immense suffering caused by the abuse of children , especially within the church and by her ministers . Above all , I express my deep sorrow to the innocent victims of these unspeakable crimes &amp;hellip ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I also acknowledge with you the shame and humiliation that all of us have suffered because of these sins . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And last August , in a 2,000-word letter addressed to the &amp;ldquo ; People of God , &amp;rdquo ; Pope Francis admitted that the Church &amp;ldquo ; showed no care for the little ones ; we abandoned them . &amp;rdquo ; This came after a U.S. grand jury confirmed that more than 1,000 children had been sexually abused by &amp;ldquo ; predatory&amp;rdquo ; priests in Pennsylvania while church officials failed to discipline offenders and often covered up incidents . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Let us beg forgiveness for our own sins and the sins of others , &amp;rdquo ; the Pope added . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For some , though , any apology now is simply too little too , late . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gerry Belecque long ago lost his faith in God , and has avoided church since leaving St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s. &amp;ldquo ; When there are weddings , I sit outside , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &amp;ldquo ; When there were baptisms , I always stayed outside . &amp;rdquo ; On occasions when felt he had no choice but enter , such as at his parents funerals , he stayed at the back . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; If the apology comes from the people who did it , that&amp;rsquo ; s a different thing . But if it comes from those who knew that they did it but didn&amp;rsquo ; t do anything , big deal . It doesn&amp;rsquo ; t make a damn difference to me if the Pope apologizes . It&amp;rsquo ; s just words . They don&amp;rsquo ; t give a goddamn , and they&amp;rsquo ; re still doing it . So apologies don&amp;rsquo ; t mean anything . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You never get closure , &amp;rdquo ; he adds . &amp;ldquo ; It took me a long time to get over it . Right now I&amp;rsquo ; m not seeing anybody , but I&amp;rsquo ; ve got one foot in the grave , maybe . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yet McCann persists . &amp;ldquo ; There aren&amp;rsquo ; t many Don Quixotes in the world , and I don&amp;rsquo ; t mind tilting at windmills . It&amp;rsquo ; s a chance to continue to make a difference in the world . It gives meaning to going out there and saying it doesn&amp;rsquo ; t hurt to apologize . It wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t hurt Francis to stand on the balcony in St. Peter&amp;rsquo ; s Square and say he&amp;rsquo ; s sorry . He doesn&amp;rsquo ; t have to come to Canada to do it . It would carry just as much weight . For a lot of people , just the words are enough . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s Training School for Boys was officially opened on Aug. 6 , 1933 , by Les Fr&amp;egrave ; res des Ecoles Chr&amp;eacute ; tiennes d&amp;rsquo ; Ottawa . In 1973 , the Ontario Ministry of Correctional Services assumed operation of the school , which was renamed Champlain Training School . It closed in 1984 . St. John&amp;rsquo ; s closed in 2003. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **30;22235;TOOLONG " **37;22267;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Bruce DeachmanSat , 06 Apr 2019 12:46:11 **45;22306;TOOLONG StoryLocal NewsNationalWorldThe Havana Syndrome : Why Canadian diplomats have accused their government of abandoning themhttp : **36;22353;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;22391;TOOLONG ... title= " The ' Havana Syndrome ' has plagued diplomats serving in Cuba . " alt= " The ' Havana Syndrome ' has plagued diplomats serving in Cuba . " data-portal-copyright= " Illustration by Andrew King " **28;22427;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1356766 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; **28;22457;TOOLONG is Part 1 of a special **32;22487;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For many Canadian diplomats with young families , it was a dream posting . Life in Havana was busy , pleasant and generally sunny . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Diplomats and their families enjoyed year-round sports &amp;mdash ; tennis , golf , swimming , snorkelling , sailing and horseback riding . Diplomatic staff played ultimate frisbee in neighbourhood parks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There were minor complications . They had to spend part of their annual leave back home doing rounds of medical appointments and stocking up on supplies , for example . But life in the upscale Havana neighbourhood of Miramar , where many international diplomats lived &amp;mdash ; a community dotted with colonial homes , embassies and palm trees &amp;mdash ; was family friendly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Their children attended the International School of Havana , founded by a British ex-pat in 1965 . It was a progressive school that emphasized academics and sports and catered to children of the diplomatic community . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Such was the life Diplomat Allen , his wife and their two teenage sons were living in 2017 , on their second posting to Cuba . They frequently socialized . Their boys went to a good school , they were involved in sports and their buddies were in and out of the house . &amp;ldquo ; We had a nice house , we had a good life down there , &amp;rdquo ; Allen said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Then everything changed , in ways that , at first , the family didn&amp;rsquo ; t understand . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Allen , as he is identified in legal documents , is one of 15 people , including five diplomats and their families from the Ottawa area , who are suing the federal government for $28 million in connection with mysterious health issues they suffered when they were posted to Cuba . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The suit alleges the Canadian government failed to properly inform , protect , treat and support the Canadians . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Throughout the crisis , Canada downplayed the seriousness of the situation , hoarded and concealed critical health and safety information , and gave false , misleading and incomplete information to diplomatic staff , &amp;rdquo ; the suit alleges . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; During the past few years , the lawsuit contends , Canadian diplomatic families in Havana &amp;ldquo ; have been targeted and injured , suffering severe and traumatic harm by means that are not clear but may be some type of sonic or microwave weapon . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The brain injuries they&amp;rsquo ; ve experienced , similar to injuries suffered by American diplomats posted to Cuba , are now known as Havana Syndrome . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The complainants&amp;rsquo ; allegations have not been tested in court . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the wake of the lawsuit , this newspaper spoke to a number of the diplomats now home in the Ottawa area . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The spoke on the condition of anonymity , saying they still fear for their security and that of their families . Many of the Canadian diplomats are struggling not only with health issues , but with anxiety and fear . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Diplomat Allen , which is the pseudonym he uses in the lawsuit , admits he is shaken by some of the negative comments people have posted on the bottom of stories about the case suggesting the symptoms are invented . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At the start of a lengthy interview , he asked this newspaper : &amp;ldquo ; What did you think the first time you heard about this . Did you believe it ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;22521;TOOLONG ... title= " Illustration by Andrew King " alt= " Illustration by Andrew King " data-portal-copyright= " Illustration by Andrew King " **28;22557;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1356767 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the late winter of 2017 the previously healthy members of the Allen family began experiencing symptoms , from nosebleeds to headaches , that they couldn&amp;rsquo ; t explain . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; They would wake up with excruciating headaches . They experienced nausea and vision problems . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Their youngest son , 12 , was getting as many as four nosebleeds a day , even passing out . Mrs. Allen began hearing high-pitched noises . The couple became uncharacteristically irritable . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We knew there was something , but we just couldn&amp;rsquo ; t figure out what was going on . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And then , one evening in early April , there was a knock on their door . An American diplomat who lived across the street asked if Allen would go for a walk . &amp;ldquo ; Living in Cuba , we understood that our houses were bugged . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What Allen learned shook him . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His neighbour told him a dozen Americans had already been evacuated from Havana after suffering from symptoms including nausea , headaches , nosebleeds , hearing and eye problems . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He told Allen the symptoms were believed to be the result of attacks with some kind of a weapon , maybe sonic . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It just hit me , &amp;lsquo ; Holy crap , this is going on in my house . ' &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The American diplomat was upset that the U.S. government had not told the Canadians about the situation . His colleagues had been sworn to secrecy , but he hadn&amp;rsquo ; t yet been &amp;ldquo ; gagged , &amp;rdquo ; so he took the opportunity to warn Allen , &amp;ldquo ; because we lived so close and he thought we were in danger . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The next day , Allen went to the ambassador who began high-level talks but warned Allen not to tell his Canadian colleagues , allegedly saying : &amp;ldquo ; We don&amp;rsquo ; t want to start mass hysteria . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On June 1 , 2017 , Allen woke up around 3 a.m. to a &amp;ldquo ; grinding , screeching metallic noise&amp;rdquo ; that filled the bedroom of their home and lasted about 30 seconds . As it faded , the sound slowed down and became lower . He was paralyzed with nausea. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; At the low point , I thought I was going to throw up . I was nauseous like you wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t believe . I don&amp;rsquo ; t know whether I went back to sleep or passed out . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His son came into the bedroom upset and covered in blood from a severe nosebleed . His wife took him back to his room where she changed his bloody sheets and clothes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Allen woke up in the morning feeling awful . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The next day , Allen went to tell the ambassador what had happened . On the way up the stairs at the embassy , a colleague jokingly asked if he was drunk because he was stumbling . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When he got to a high-security zone inside the embassy , Allen swiped his card and looked at the keypad but couldn&amp;rsquo ; t remember his code . He began randomly punching numbers . &amp;ldquo ; It wasn&amp;rsquo ; t like me . I had never done that . &amp;rdquo ; He told officials he could no longer wait for the Canadian embassy to take action . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two days later , the family was sent to the University of Miami to undergo testing with a doctor who had examined at least 20 Americans who had been based in Havana . All four members of the Allen family were diagnosed with traumatic brain injuries akin to concussions. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The physician recommended the children not return to Cuba , saying : &amp;ldquo ; They need to be away from Cuba for awhile . &amp;rdquo ; Allen asked the Canadian embassy if he could send his sons directly to Ottawa from Miami , but that request was denied , according to the statement of claim . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The family returned to Cuba but quickly sent the boys ahead to Ottawa to stay with relatives . Allen and his wife followed soon after for a three-week holiday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That break left them feeling refreshed and optimistic . Their health , which had been a worry for months , seemed better after the break in Canada . Since the Canadian diplomatic staff had not been allowed to talk to each other about their experiences and they weren&amp;rsquo ; t aware of any other affected Canadians , the Allens hoped maybe it was a &amp;ldquo ; one-off thing&amp;rdquo ; and agreed to return to Cuba . They were hopeful their lives could return to normal , but cautious . &amp;ldquo ; If we heard another family was pulling out , we were gone . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; They did not return to Havana for long . By October they were back in Ottawa for good , coping with worrisome symptoms and waiting for medical assistance and information . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meanwhile , other Canadians in Havana were suffering mysterious and debilitating symptoms . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;22587;TOOLONG ... title= " Illustration by Andrew King " alt= " Illustration by Andrew King " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;22623;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1356768 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Diplomat Davies , his wife and their two young children were having strange experiences in their nearby Havana house during the spring and summer of 2017. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Early in the year , their young daughter began having difficulty concentrating at school . She suffered from nausea , tinnitus , sensitivity to light , visual impairment , and , like the Allen children , sudden nosebleeds , sometimes in the middle of the night . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mrs. Davies had complained about hearing high-pitched sounds . Then , during a game of ultimate Frisbee with American and Canadian diplomats in a local park , Mrs. Davies suddenly fell to the ground . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I no longer knew where was up and where was down , &amp;rdquo ; she told her concerned husband of the fall . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Davies family was sent back to the Ottawa area in August 2017. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mrs. Davies was diagnosed as having damage to her vestibular system &amp;mdash ; which includes parts of the inner ear and brain that processes sensory information . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She was hypersensitive to light and noise , suffered from headaches , dizziness and muscle twitching that would last for days . She often spent long parts of the day sleeping . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She now works part time , has to wear special glasses because of sensitivity to light and vestibular damage and continues to suffer from headaches and to be easily overwhelmed by light and sounds . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Their daughter had difficulty concentrating at school and had sudden nosebleeds , nausea and light sensitivity , among other symptoms . She had tinnitus and heard three distinct sounds . Those sounds were so persistent that the little girl gave each one a name . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Davies has since had testing that has shown he has visual impairments and &amp;ldquo ; fusional facility problems , &amp;rdquo ; which are associated with brain injuries and can result in blurred vision , headaches , eye fatigue , motion sickness and loss of concentration . He has had several strange episodes involving visual impairment and confusion , including one in which he could no longer see what he was writing in a notebook and another in which he experienced confusion , flashbacks and a sense of deja vu. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Davies are among diplomats undergoing testing at Dalhousie University&amp;rsquo ; s Brain Repair Centre . Some of that research , he said , has identified leakages in the groups&amp;rsquo ; blood brain barriers consistent with concussions. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The statement of claim contends that Global Affairs &amp;ldquo ; actively interfered with the plaintiffs&amp;rsquo ; attempts to receive proper health care , including going so far as instructing hospitals to stop testing and treating them . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That happened , according to the suit filed by the former diplomats , when two members of the group , frustrated with lack of information and delays , travelled on their own to the University of Pennsylvania , which had been treating affected U.S. diplomats and had developed an expertise on Havana Syndrome . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Diplomat Baker , who was posted to Havana with her two young children , began experiencing symptoms of Havana syndrome in early 2017 , including tinnitus , headaches and vertigo . Her elementary school aged daughter was nauseous , had headaches and heavy nosebleeds. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Baker underwent medical testing that confirmed balance and vestibular issues and suggested the need for further investigation early in 2017 , but was told follow up testing would not take place for months . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Distressed about the symptoms and lack of medical attention , Baker and Mrs. Davies went to the University of Pennsylvania at their own expense after officials there offered to assess and treat Canadians but were turned down by the federal government . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While there , the medical team confirmed Baker had a brain injury that was visible on MRI and similar to those suffered by affected American diplomats . Testing on her daughter disclosed post-concussion symptoms . Her son was cleared . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And then testing was interrupted according to the statement of claim , when Canada used diplomatic channels in the United States to instruct the University of Pennsylvania Centre for Brain Injury and Repair to &amp;ldquo ; stop testing Canadians . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But the assessments on the Canadians sparked action from the federal government , says Baker , who pushed for all the Canadian cohort of diplomats to get tested . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Shortly after the women and children returned from Pennsylvania , the Canadian government pulled diplomatic families out of Cuba and told those back in Canada suffering symptoms that they would all get assessed and treated . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Today , Baker does not work , undergoes cognitive therapy and still must live &amp;ldquo ; a very cocooned life&amp;rdquo ; because of ongoing symptoms and sensitivities . Her daughter , who was the first child tested at the University of Pennsylvania to show clear evidence of concussion , according to her mother , has visual impairment and other ongoing symptoms . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While the Ottawa five wait for some resolution , many of them remain anxious , even angry . Before taking legal action , they wrote to the prime minister , among others , looking for better care and support . Some&amp;nbsp ; have speculated that the federal government didn&amp;rsquo ; t want to jeopardize its long-standing diplomatic relationship with Cuba . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; My belief , 21 months later , is that decisions were made that simply did not prioritize us , not our health , not our safety and not that of our families . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I am angry , yes , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &amp;ldquo ; I am disappointed and I am sad . I am sad because there is no justification for not having considered the personal and lifelong impact this was going to have on us. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Members of all five families , including young children , suffer from neurological symptoms . Some are still unable to work full time , many have long-term effects including cognitive vestibular and ocular motor dysfunction requiring therapy , special glasses and special accommodation . One young child had such severe tinnitus that she was hearing three distinct sounds . She gave each of them a name . One was Emile . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Allen recounts receiving a tearful phone call from his wife who had gone to the grocery store and couldn&amp;rsquo ; t find her way home . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The lawsuit claims that Canada &amp;ldquo ; badly mishandled&amp;rdquo ; the crisis as it grew and failed to ensure the safety of the diplomats and their families . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Despite knowing of the risks of Havana Syndrome early on , Canada continued to put its diplomats and their families in harm&amp;rsquo ; s way by sending them to Havana and requiring them to stay there despite becoming aware of the high and growing risk that they would sustain the brain injuries associated with Havana Syndrome . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The government has confirmed 14 cases of diplomats and their families who have been affected while in Havana . With the latest confirmed case in January of this year , staff at the Canadian mission in Havana has been reduce from 16 to eight , and families are no longer permitted as part of the posting . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Allen , meanwhile , is back to work for Global Affairs Canada , but his wife is unable to return to the kind of life she led before the mysterious attacks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; She is not the same person who went to Cuba , &amp;rdquo ; says Allen . &amp;ldquo ; She used to love to read . She can&amp;rsquo ; t stand reading now . She used to be a whiz with numbers , not anymore . She can&amp;rsquo ; t work . She is suffering from PTSD. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The government has confirmed 14 affected Canadian employees and relatives . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada&amp;rsquo ; s Foreign Affairs Minister Chrystia Freeland has said the affected diplomats have Canada&amp;rsquo ; s &amp;ldquo ; utmost sympathy and support . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last month , the government said in a statement : &amp;ldquo ; The health , safety and security of our diplomatic staff and their families remain our priority . The Canadian government continues to investigate the potential causes of the unusual health symptoms experienced by some Canadian diplomatic staff and their family members posted in Havana , Cuba . To date , no cause has been identified . &amp;hellip ; There is no evidence that Canadian travellers to Cuba are at risk . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Canada has a positive and constructive relationship with Cuba . We have had close co-operation with the Cuban authorities since the health concerns of our employees posted in Havana first surfaced in the spring of 2017. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;b&gt;&lt;i&gt;Havana Syndrome , Part 2 : &lt;a href= " https : **33;22653;TOOLONG ... a dog 's brain may help solve the mystery of Canadian diplomats ' Cuban **39;22688;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;b&gt;&lt;i&gt;Havana Syndrome , Part 3 : &lt;a href= " https : **33;22729;TOOLONG ... say ordeal has ' struck a nerve ' in Canada 's diplomatic **39;22764;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;b&gt;&lt;i&gt;Havana Syndrome , Part 4 : &lt;a href= " https : **33;22805;TOOLONG ... it could be and how experts will try to crack the **34;22840;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " epayne@postmedia.com " **34;22876;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;22912;TOOLONG ... suggest Ontario autism wait list frozen last fall , parents not told&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;22947;TOOLONG ... dies after eating poisonous mushrooms at Spanish Michelin-starred restaurant&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;22982;TOOLONG ... instances when SNC-case was discussed with Wilson-Raybould , clerk says&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Elizabeth PayneMon , 25 Feb 2019 12:13:47 **45;23017;TOOLONG ... **32;23064;TOOLONG : Here 's what the numbers say about ' screwed ' millennialshttp : **29;23098;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;23129;TOOLONG ... title= " Millennials have been criticized by some , but the numbers illustrate the real and unique economic challenges they 've been saddled with . " alt= " Millennials have been criticized by some , but the numbers illustrate the real and unique economic challenges they 've been saddled with . " data-portal-copyright= " FarknotArchitect " **28;23165;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1343485 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Has the millennial generation really been &amp;ldquo ; screwed&amp;rdquo ; ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Take a quick survey of friends and you&amp;rsquo ; ll get answers from &amp;ldquo ; yes , &amp;rdquo ; to &amp;ldquo ; no , &amp;rdquo ; to eye rolls and groans at the mere mention of &amp;ldquo ; millennials , &amp;rdquo ; likely divided along lines of age . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Even the millennials themselves aren&amp;rsquo ; t completely sold on the oft-floated notion that life is harder for them than it has been for previous generations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s hard to judge . You can&amp;rsquo ; t compare your experiences with someone else&amp;rsquo ; s , &amp;rdquo ; said Matilda ( better known by her friends as Mati ) Barrett , a 24-year-old Ottawa resident . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; ve seen a fair amount of challenges . Our generation may seem to think that we go through more . But , I think that every generation goes through some kind of hardships when it comes to finance or jobs . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;23195;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;23231;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Barrett is struggling through many of the same issues facing her peers . The former Dalhousie University varsity hockey goalkeeper completed her degree , a Bachelor of Science , with a major in kinesiology , in 2016 , and is now struggling to find a career path . She lives at home , and holds down three jobs , which dominate her time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She is keenly aware of the price of housing , which has been steadily marching upwards , and the increasing demands that employers are putting on candidates for higher education . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canadians between the ages of 18 and 35 , such as Barrett , have been caught up in an economic maelstrom &amp;mdash ; a pushing and pulling of forces &amp;mdash ; that is unique compared to anything faced by the Gen Xers or Baby Boomers who came before them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Abacus Data regularly puts out the Canadian Millennials Report , a publication that&amp;rsquo ; s dedicated to understanding the views of Canadians born between 1980 and 2000 . More than 4,000 millennials are interviewed annually and results are released on a monthly basis . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m seeing , in the data , a growing sense of a shared experience that is getting people quite anxious about their future , &amp;rdquo ; says David Coletto , the company&amp;rsquo ; s chief executive , and himself a millennial. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There is something to be said for a growing collective understanding among this age group , and this generation , that somehow we&amp;rsquo ; ve been screwed . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &amp;bull ; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 1980 , Queen&amp;rsquo ; s Another One Bites the Dust was on the radio , The Empire Strikes Back was in theatres , and the average Canadian between the age of 18 and 35 was making $34,200 a year . ( That&amp;rsquo ; s in today&amp;rsquo ; s dollars , adjusted , not what was on their paycheque , according Statistics Canada. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2016 , as we were downloading Drake&amp;rsquo ; s One Dance , and streaming Star Wars : Rogue One , the average Canadian between 18 and 35 was making $34,300 &amp;mdash ; a difference of &amp;hellip ; $100. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Average wages for people between the ages of 18 and 35 have remained stagnant for nearly four decades . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;23261;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;23297;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; By comparison , people in the 35-through-50-year-old age group were making , on average , $49,500 in 1980 . By 2016 , their average income rose to $57,200 , a $7,700 jump , according to Statistics Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Those between the ages of 51 and 65 saw even bigger gains . Average salaries for that demographic were at $46,700 in 1980 and had jumped to $54,700 by 2016 , an $8,000 increase . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The millennial salary stagnation is also happening at a time when benefits , such as dental care and Defined Benefit Pension Plans , are being pared back or even eliminated by employers , giving those older generations an even bigger financial leg up . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Laziness isn&amp;rsquo ; t the culprit here . Millennials are working and working hard . In fact , they&amp;rsquo ; re actually participating in the labour force more and , by some measures , working harder than previous generations did . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 1980 , 78.9 per cent of all Canadians between the ages of 18 and 35 were employed in some sort of job , according to Statistics Canada . In 2016 , it was 81.2 per cent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;23327;TOOLONG ... title= " A ( human ) welder. " alt= " A ( human ) welder. " data-portal-copyright= " PUNIT PARANJPE " **28;23363;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1344059 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; That increase is even more impressive considering the size of the demographic today . There are as many as 10 million people now in the millennial generation , accounting for 27.5 per cent of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s population . That sheer size makes them the largest generational demographic in the country . It also suggests there are more people between the ages of 18 and 35 working in the labour force today than ever before . Still , there seem to be major hurdles in their path . &lt;/p&gt; **28;23393;TOOLONG FOR THE HOME **30;23423;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Housing was the top issue cited by millennials in six surveys done by Abacus last year , Coletto says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Of those who don&amp;rsquo ; t own a home currently , 86 per cent want to , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 1980 , the average price of a house in Canada was $203,972.86 &amp;mdash ; or about six times average annual salaries , once adjusted to account for today&amp;rsquo ; s dollar values , the Canadian Real Estate Association says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; By 2016 , the average home price hit $506,760.01 &amp;mdash ; or 14.8 times average annual wages . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have a situation where this generation is really , really stuck in many ways , &amp;rdquo ; said Benjamin Tal , managing director and deputy chief economist at CIBC . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This is really the story of this cohort : We have a situation where housing is much more expensive , so they have to compromise on either renting or they have to switch to condominiums or smart houses . Or , they might buy a car and stay with their parents . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;23455;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;23491;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the 1960s , Census data suggest as few as eight per cent of Canadians ages 18 to 35 were living with parents . Today , as has been well documented , those numbers have skyrocketed. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Come 2016 , a total of 34.7 per cent of Canadians between the ages of 20 and 34 were living at home with their parents . That&amp;rsquo ; s as many as 3.5 million Canadians , the highest number recorded to that point . And Ontario had the highest percentage of millennials still living at home : 42.1 per cent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The situation hasn&amp;rsquo ; t necessarily changed aspirations , though . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last year , 94 per cent of the millennials living at home or with family planned to buy a home , a CIBC survey found . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; How will they do it ? A national survey by HSBC Bank of Canada , released in 2017 , found 37 per cent of millennials who have purchased a home received some of the money for the purchase from their parents . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;23521;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;23557;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " " /&gt; **28;23587;TOOLONG EDUCATION SYSTEM BASICALLY FAILED THEM ' **26;23617;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The only thing that is helping them is their parents , &amp;rdquo ; said Tal from the CIBC . &amp;ldquo ; The education system basically failed them . They are highly educated but they are not educated in what&amp;rsquo ; s relevant . There is a mismatch between the skills that they have and what the economy needs and therefore you have many people underemployed and , or , trying to compromise . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is no denying that millennials are highly educated . A study released by the PEW Research Center in the U.S. suggests that they make up the most highly educated generation ever produced . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; More than 3.5 million Canadians between the ages of 20 and 34 reported having some sort of post-secondary education , including bachelor degrees and post-graduate certificates and diplomas , in the 2016 Census . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But , the degree by itself isn&amp;rsquo ; t proving to be enough . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s not an option about whether or not they&amp;rsquo ; re going to have an education . That&amp;rsquo ; s like an invitation card to the party , &amp;rdquo ; said Carleton University&amp;rsquo ; s Linda Duxbury , a professor in management sciences at the Sprott School of Business who specializes in demographic trends . She&amp;rsquo ; s also a member of the Baby Boomer Generation ( people who are now 54 to 73 years of age ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You don&amp;rsquo ; t have the skills , you don&amp;rsquo ; t have post-graduate education today , be it college or university , you aren&amp;rsquo ; t even going to be looked at . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;23645;TOOLONG ... title= " Linda Duxbury " alt= " Linda Duxbury " data-portal-copyright= " Darren Brown " **28;23681;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1342308 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Duxbury described the situation like an arms-race for qualifications to set a candidate apart from their competitors so they can land a good job and start their lives . The problem is , the process is forcing them to put their actual life on hold . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The thing about this generation is that everything is delayed , &amp;rdquo ; said Duxbury. &amp;ldquo ; They stay in school longer , they get married later . If you start comparing the average age of marriage , the average age of their first kid , the average age of their first permanent full-time job . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s also forcing those within the generation to compete with one another in ways that are specifically benefitting employers . With so much talent available , competition for available entry levels jobs is fierce . This allows employers to pay less for the workers they need . Some are even getting away with not paying their employees at all . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to the Canadian Federation of Students , as many as 300,000 millennials were working in unpaid internships in a bid to further their careers by collecting relevant on-the-job experience , in 2016. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The issue with unpaid internships is that most millennials in those jobs are also working in another position , likely one that pays minimum wage , in order to make ends meet , according to the CFS. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The organization , which lobbies on behalf of students , also pointed to Statistics Canada data that shows , in 1997 ( the first year that wage data was collected ) , only 640,000 Canadians were making the minimum wage or less . In 2017 , that number sat at 1.02 million , almost double the number previously recorded . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Further compounding the issues facing the millennials is the steady drop in government support offered to post-secondary institutions . According to the CFS , government provided more than 80 per cent of university operating revenues in 1982 . That figure fell below 60 per cent in 2012 , the most recent year for which statistics are available . The CFS says government support has continued to fall in more recent years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As government support has decreased , students have been forced to shoulder more of the financial burden when obtaining their education . That has led to students exiting post-secondary institutions with record amounts of debt . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Using data from Statistics Canada , the CFS said that average student debt levels have increased more than 40 per cent between 1999 and 2012 . Those levels have continued to climb in more recent years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The increased cost of education , at a time when obtaining a degree has never been more important , is another hot-button issue for millennials. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;23711;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;23747;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " " /&gt; **27;23777;TOOLONG UNDEREMPLOYED **36;23806;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Underemployment , &amp;rdquo ; a term used to describe people who are overqualified for the positions they find themselves in , is also rising rapidly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s Barrett , it means her Bachelor of Science degree isn&amp;rsquo ; t enough for her to start a career . She is working at a family health clinic on a part time basis and as a goaltending instructor at two different businesses in the nation&amp;rsquo ; s capital . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She is hoping the work will help to save up enough to help pay for a graduate degree . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There is no getting anywhere with just a bachelor of science anymore , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; I graduated . I played varsity hockey at Dalhousie University . Academic All-Canadian all five years I was there . I had a good GPA . That doesn&amp;rsquo ; t matter in terms of getting a job . I need to get another degree in order to get a job in a desired field . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She has applied to get into a program to become a physician assistant , but hasn&amp;rsquo ; t been successful in getting into the program so far . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;23844;TOOLONG ... title= " Mati Barrett , 24 , is a millennial facing many of the issues those her age deal with . " alt= " Mati Barrett , 24 , is a millennial facing many of the issues those her age deal with . " data-portal-copyright= " Julie Oliver " **28;23880;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1342300 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s tough and competitive , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; Even for a lot of the programs you are looking at applying to , to get an actual career . The amount of hours of experience you need , for example the physician assistant program that I applied to . It&amp;rsquo ; s only offered by three universities in Canada and I couldn&amp;rsquo ; t apply to the third , which is the University of Toronto , because they require almost 1,000 hours of health care experience . So , where am I supposed to get that ? Hypothetically , what I&amp;rsquo ; m doing now could count . But , most people applying to that , it&amp;rsquo ; s their second career . They&amp;rsquo ; re going to be older . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In America , where numbers are more readily available , underemployment held steady at around 33 per cent of all post-secondary graduates between the 1980s and 2010. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , that figure jumped to more than 44 per cent of all graduates in 2012 , the most recent year for which statistics were available . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This week , Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s Financial Accountability Office flagged underemployment as an issue that is affecting certain demographics and their ability to keep up financially . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe src= " **40;23910;TOOLONG ... &lt;div&gt; " Income inequality has become more entrenched , as lower-income Ontarians are increasingly staying in low-income , and higher-income Ontarians are increasingly staying in high-income , " said Peter Weltman , the province 's financial accountability officer , in a report released Thursday . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;He highlighted significant structural changes in Ontario 's economy and labour market , including a rise in part-time and temporary work . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; These trends in Ontario incomes raise important questions about the role of government in ensuring that all individuals have access to similar economic opportunities , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Abacus&amp;rsquo ; s Coletto is seeing signs that the trend exists here in Canada , too . One of the key indicators of underemployment is wages , which would be reflected in the rise of minimum wage workers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But , the other big indicator is the benefits offered by employers . Self-employed people , largely , don&amp;rsquo ; t have access to benefits . People making minimum wage get a minimum amount of benefits from their employers . Only 55 per cent of millennials have access to a prescription drug plan , 53 per cent to dental insurance , 36 per cent to an RRSP , and only 29 per cent to an employer provided pension plan , according to a recent study released by Abacus Data . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;23952;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;23988;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " " /&gt; **27;24018;TOOLONG WORKFORCE : EVOLVE OR **29;24047;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Careers are also changing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Older millennials in the demographic who graduated 10 or 15 years ago are finding the education that they worked so hard to receive , and paid good money for , is no longer relevant to the work they are expected to be doing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 1980 , there were a total of 2,080,000 people employed in Canada&amp;rsquo ; s manufacturing industry , according to Statistics Canada . By 2016 , that number dropped by more than half to 1,032,900 people . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The professional , scientific and technical industry ( which includes high technology workers ) employed 393,400 people in 1980 . That swelled to more than 1,393,700 by 2016. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Today&amp;rsquo ; s employers are less likely to train existing employees to meet modern demands , choosing instead to hire younger , cheaper talent that is now exiting school with the skills they need . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;24078;TOOLONG ... title= " Shimizu Corp . ' s Robo-Welder is demonstrated during a press tour . " alt= " Shimizu Corp . ' s Robo-Welder is demonstrated during a press tour . " data-portal-copyright= " Koji Sasahara " **28;24114;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1344060 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Jobs are becoming obsolete on a monthly basis . New jobs are taking over . As we move to AI ( artificial intelligence ) , robotics , all these kinds of things , certainly jobs are going to be replaced . New jobs are going to be created , mostly at the high scale end , &amp;rdquo ; said Carleton&amp;rsquo ; s Duxbury. &amp;ldquo ; ( Employers ) don&amp;rsquo ; t spend the money on training and development that they should and they&amp;rsquo ; d rather have someone coming in with the credentials , then they would training somebody who is currently working for them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; My father , when he came back from the war , had a Grade 12 education . When he retired he was vice-president at 3M . Why ? Because there was a different attitude then . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Is it harder to buy a home ? Yes . Are we taking on higher student debt ? Yes . Have we been trained and educated in the wrong things ? Many of us , yes , &amp;rdquo ; said Abacus&amp;rsquo ; s Coletto. &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; ve been raised around these expectations . &amp;lsquo ; Go do what you want to do . The world is your oyster . Dream big . &amp;rsquo ; Those expectations were set quite high . When you enter a world where it&amp;rsquo ; s not possible to achieve the things that your parents and your teachers and society has been saying is going to be there , it creates further tension . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;24144;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;24180;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " " /&gt; **28;24210;TOOLONG BOOMERS WO N'T GO , CA N'T **28;24240;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; The tension is being compounded by the Baby Boomer generation&amp;rsquo ; s refusal to leave the workforce . If the millennials are stuck in a bottleneck fighting for career advancement , right now the Baby Boomers are acting like a cork preventing any movement . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Whereas the age 65 was once marked by a person&amp;rsquo ; s announcing their retirement , today&amp;rsquo ; s senior citizens are opting to soldier on . The idea of freedom at 55 seems long forgotten . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Extended life spans , better health and , in some cases , continued financial responsibilities are seeing many people in the Baby Boomer demographic work well into their 70s before opting for retirement . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;24270;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;24306;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Boomers have significant expenses that perhaps they didn&amp;rsquo ; t anticipate , plus they love their jobs and they&amp;rsquo ; re healthy . They think , &amp;lsquo ; What am I going to do for the next 25 years ? &amp;rdquo ; said Duxbury. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Duxbury also said that Baby Boomers are now finding themselves in a situation where they are caring for , or paying for the care , of their aging parents . Many also find themselves sandwiched in between paying for their parents , while also supporting their millennial children who have returned to the family home . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The millennials can&amp;rsquo ; t afford to leave their parents homes because they don&amp;rsquo ; t have the opportunity or means to do so . But , their parents can&amp;rsquo ; t afford to retire , which would open spaces in the workforce for millennials , because they are still supporting those kids at home . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It is a problem that the Boomers aren&amp;rsquo ; t leaving . That&amp;rsquo ; s absolutely true , &amp;rdquo ; said Duxbury. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So , what comes next ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;24336;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;24372;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " " /&gt; **27;24402;TOOLONG AWAKENING OF THE **36;24431;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Tectonic plates are beginning to shift . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That&amp;rsquo ; s the opinion , at least , of Karen Foster , assistant professor at Dalhousie University and Canada Research Chair in Sustainable Rural Futures for Atlantic Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One of Foster&amp;rsquo ; s areas of expertise is in researching generational divisions at work . She said there is no doubt millennials are upset and that their complaints have been falling on deaf ears . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , one of the larger issues that millennials are facing is a growing wealth gap between themselves and older generations . Due to the fact that major life milestones have been delayed , investments in real estate or other financial transactions aren&amp;rsquo ; t yielding the same return for the younger generation , Foster said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sure property values have skyrocketed for older generations who bought when prices were affordable and have been hanging onto the property . But the millennial who just purchased is seeing bigger mortgages than ever before and interest rates that are steadily rising . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Critics of millennial concerns have often pointed out that are next in line to inherit massive amounts of wealth that has been accumulated by their aging parents . In fact , between 2016 and 2026 , the largest wealth transfer in history is expected to take place as older generations pass on as much as $1 trillion to younger generations in Canada , according to Toronto-based research firm Strategic Insight . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But Foster pointed out one critical problem with the upcoming windfall : The expected transfer of wealth will only benefit those people who come from families that are already wealthy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It will also affect people from major Canadian cities , where property prices have been steadily rising , in a much different way than those people from less populated towns . If your family is from somewhere such as Moncton , N.B. , Moose Jaw , Sask. or Sudbury , Ont. , you won&amp;rsquo ; t see the same windfall when it comes time to collect an inheritance . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;24469;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;24505;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; People who are set to inherit money from their parents , or , in a sense , have been inheriting it their whole lives through various kinds of support , that&amp;rsquo ; s where the real inequality happens , &amp;rdquo ; she said , adding that millennials are looking to completely upend the status quo. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; That really comes down to a restructuring of the tax system . We need to be taxing wealth , and high incomes and corporate profits more in order to redistribute wealth across the demographics. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Foster predicts that millennials are likely to push for things such as increased estate taxes in order to ensure that some of the wealth that is transferred from older generations is redistributed throughout all of society . The idea is being spearheaded in the United States by recently elected Democrat Congresswoman Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez . Ocasio-Cortez , who at 29 is also a millennial , is lobbying for the introduction of a tax on the wealthiest Americans in the country . She believes that every dollar of income over $10 million should be taxed at 70 per cent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There will , of course , be pushback to that idea . It&amp;rsquo ; s been panned by the audience at the World Economic Forum in Davos , Switzerland , as recently as last week . With attendees including Michael Dell , founder of Dell Technologies and Microsoft Corp . founder Bill Gates expressing objections . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The call for higher estate taxes in Canada was recently reinforced in a study released by the Canadian Centre for Policy Alternatives ( CCPA ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You&amp;rsquo ; d expect Canada&amp;rsquo ; s tax regime would try to counteract this concentration of wealth at the very top , where it&amp;rsquo ; s needed the least , but in fact , federal policies encourage it , &amp;rdquo ; said the centre&amp;rsquo ; s senior economist David Macdonald in a release . &amp;ldquo ; Canada is the only country in the G7 without an inheritance , estate or gift tax on tremendous family wealth . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The CCPA&amp;rsquo ; s report said Canada&amp;rsquo ; s wealthiest families saw their net worth grow , on average , by 37 per cent between 2012 and 2016 . Middle class families saw their net worth grow by only 16 per cent during the same time frame . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The study calls for high estate taxes , eliminating tax preferences for capital gains and dividend income in a bid to better distribute wealth across demographics. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That may sound like a scary scenario to older generations who have amassed wealth throughout their lifetimes , and typically been more conservative when it comes to political fiscal policies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But , for the millennial generation it&amp;rsquo ; s only the tip of the iceberg . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;24535;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;24571;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " " /&gt; **29;24601;TOOLONG THE BIG GOVERNMENT ? **26;24632;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; According to Abacus Data , millennials are looking to government to take big steps to fix their problems . This generation is more comfortable with big , interventionist government than allowing market forces to play their part , surveys suggest . Many millennials truly believe that the economic system that has been in place for hundreds of years fails to work for them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The company&amp;rsquo ; s research suggests that millennials clearly prioritize increased government spending over balanced budgets in order to tackle issues such as alleviating income inequality . They believe corporations don&amp;rsquo ; t pay their fair share of taxes and that government has a big role to play in redistributing that money . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Having lived through the 2008 financial crisis and a rise in housing costs , many are outright skeptical of the free market . When asked to indicate if they lean more towards one perspective or the other , only 46 per cent suggested that capitalism is the best economic model we have , while 54 per cent believe Canada would be better off with a more socialist system , &amp;rdquo ; reads an Abacus Data survey released last year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Perhaps the most pertinent fact : Millennials are now the largest block of voters in the country and have the ability to swing an election toward the party or platform of their choice , provided the generation gets out the vote . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So far , they have remained relatively quiet . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In most elections politicians aren&amp;rsquo ; t talking about issues that they care about , they aren&amp;rsquo ; t very compelling , they aren&amp;rsquo ; t giving them a reason to get engaged , &amp;rdquo ; said Abacus&amp;rsquo ; s Coletto. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Millennials are unique from previous generations , particularly the Boomers and those who came before the Boomers , in that voting is not ( considered ) a duty . We don&amp;rsquo ; t do it because we have to . We do it because we have a reason to . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Reasons seem to be emerging . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;span class= " embed-youtube " style= " text-align:center ; display : block ; " &gt;&lt;iframe class='youtube-player ' type='text/html ' width= ' 640 ' height= ' 360 ' src='https : **34;24660;TOOLONG ... allowfullscreen='true ' style='border:0 ; ' **28;24696;TOOLONG ... &lt;p&gt; According to Elections Canada , 38.8 per cent of eligible voters between the ages of 18 and 24 years of age voted in the 2011 federal election . Of those , between 25 and 34 years of age , 45.1 per cent cast their ballot . By comparison , a total of 64.5 per cent of Canadians between the ages of 45 and 54 voted in that election . When it came to Canadians between the ages of 65 and 74 , a total of 75.1 per cent of eligible voters participated . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The federal election in 2015 saw better turnout by millennials . More than 57.1 per cent of eligible voters between 18 and 24 years of age cast a ballot . The number was similar in the 25 through 34-year-old age group , where 57.4 per cent of eligible voters participated . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One of the big issues during the 2015 election was the Liberal party&amp;rsquo ; s promise to legalize cannabis sales in Canada , which is something that appeals specifically to the millennial generation&amp;rsquo ; s interests . More than 60 per cent of Canadians between the ages of 18 and 34 supported legalizing cannabis , according to a study released by NRG Research Group and Peak Communicators in March 2017. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;24726;TOOLONG ... title= " A woman smokes cannabis in a Toronto park on first day of legalization of cannabis across Canada on October 17 , 2018. " alt= " A woman smokes cannabis in a Toronto park on first day of legalization of cannabis across Canada on October 17 , 2018. " data-portal-copyright= " Chris Young " **28;24762;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1341866 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s expected that more politicians will be looking to woo the millennial vote heading into October&amp;rsquo ; s federal election . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last week , Finance Minister Bill Morneau said the federal government is looking at ways to make housing purchases more affordable for millennials in the months ahead . He called the issue a &amp;ldquo ; priority&amp;rdquo ; but didn&amp;rsquo ; t elaborate . NDP Leader Jagmeet Singh has said his party would propose measures to help build as many as 500,000 affordable housing units across the country , if elected . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; With more politicians tuning into the fact that the millennial generation will vote for political platforms that speak to their agenda , the 18 through 35 demographic looks as though it will soon find itself in an unfamiliar position . Instead of taking a back seat while older generations plot the course , millennials are poised to grab the wheel and take a drastic detour . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It hasn&amp;rsquo ; t boiled over yet . But , the pot is starting to get warm , &amp;rdquo ; said Coletto. &amp;ldquo ; There are more millennials than there are boomers . More millennials are eligible to vote than any other generation . More politicians are mindful of the shift that millennials could bring . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meanwhile , Barrett , is taking things in stride . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;24792;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;24828;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; ve seen a fair amount of challenges . Our generation may seem to think that we go through more . But , I think that every generation goes through some kind of hardships when it comes to finance or jobs . It doesn&amp;rsquo ; t make things worthwhile if they&amp;rsquo ; re just handed to you . It&amp;rsquo ; s tough , but once you get what you&amp;rsquo ; re going for , you appreciate it more . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;24858;TOOLONG " **36;24889;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;24927;TOOLONG ... will overtake baby boomers as the largest U.S. population group in 2019&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;24962;TOOLONG ... is losing ice 6 times faster today than in 1980s&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;24997;TOOLONG ... are no different from their baby boomer parents -- they 're just poorer&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; Vito PilieciFri , 01 Feb 2019 15:31:27 **45;25032;TOOLONG StoryLocal NewsNationalWorldCanada sending emergency management expert to help fight Ebola in West Africahttp : **34;25079;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;25115;TOOLONG ... title= " In this Sunday , Sept 9 , 2018 file photo , a health worker sprays disinfectant on his colleague after working at an Ebola treatment centre in Beni , Eastern Congo . " alt= " In this Sunday , Sept 9 , 2018 file photo , a health worker sprays disinfectant on his colleague after working at an Ebola treatment centre in Beni , Eastern Congo . " data-portal-copyright= " Al-hadji Kudra Maliro " **28;25151;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1332288 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Canadian government has sent an emergency management expert to the Republic of the Congo to help the World Health Organization battle the worsening Ebola outbreak in nearby Democratic Republic of Congo . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The outbreak is now the second largest in history after the West Africa Ebola one that killed 11,000 people between 2014 and 2016 and put the world on edge . So far , 385 people have died in DRC and the outbreak , amid ongoing conflict and contentious elections , continues to grow . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Public Health Agency of Canada is &amp;ldquo ; closely monitoring the situation , &amp;rdquo ; said spokeswoman Anna Maddison . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although there are new tools to fight Ebola since the 2014 crisis &amp;mdash ; notably the Canadian-developed experimental vaccine &amp;mdash ; the backdrop of political instability and violence in DRC is making it difficult to contain the outbreak . It is also causing growing alarm . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ongoing and routine attacks on health workers and violence are limiting the kind of help countries are sending . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;25181;TOOLONG ... title= " A member of the medical staff of the Ebola Treatment Unit ( ETU ) gets ready as she checks her Personal Protective Equipment ( PPE ) in front of the mirror . " alt= " A member of the medical staff of the Ebola Treatment Unit ( ETU ) gets ready as she checks her Personal Protective Equipment ( PPE ) in front of the mirror . " data-portal-copyright= " ISAAC KASAMANI " **28;25217;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1332527 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last year , the U.S. Centres for Disease Control and Prevention pulled Ebola experts back from conflict zones in DRC over fears about their safety . There have been reports that health teams responding to the outbreak have been attacked three or four times a week . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada , which is a member of WHO&amp;rsquo ; s Global Outbreak Alert and Response Network , a group that deals with threats of epidemic-prone diseases , responded to a request to send an emergency management specialist . That specialist will work at WHO&amp;rsquo ; s emergency operations centre in the neighbouring Republic of the Congo . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Unlike the previous West African crisis , the Public Health Agency of Canada has not , so far , sent epidemiologists , lab support or other experts directly to the outbreak region . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A PHAC spokesperson acknowledged Canada has a history of providing on-site support to WHO for Ebola . In addition to work developing the vaccine that has been deployed in DRC , the public health agency sent out epidemiologists , laboratory support and emergency management resources in 2014 and 2015 to support global efforts to respond to the West African Ebola epidemic . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There have been no similar announcements this time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; When making decisions about how to support requests for assistance and subsequent deployments , &amp;rdquo ; said spokeswoman Maddison , &amp;ldquo ; PHAC considers where the greatest need exists and carefully considers the security of personnel . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;25247;TOOLONG ... title= " A health worker carries a four-day-old baby suspected of having Ebola , into a MSF ( Doctors Without Borders ) supported Ebola Treatment Centre ( ETC ) on November 4 , 2018 in Butembo , Democratic Republic of the Congo . " alt= " A health worker carries a four-day-old baby suspected of having Ebola , into a MSF ( Doctors Without Borders ) supported Ebola Treatment Centre ( ETC ) on November 4 , 2018 in Butembo , Democratic Republic of the Congo . " data-portal-copyright= " JOHN WESSELS " **28;25283;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1332289 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The decision to send an emergency management expert is one part of the Canadian response to the Ebola outbreak . The federal government has invested $1.5 million in rapid research aimed at improving the way the world responds to public health emergencies . It has also provided more than $3.3 million in emergency humanitarian assistance to WHO , the Red Cross , M&amp;eacute ; decins&amp;nbsp ; sans Fronti&amp;egrave ; res and other agencies that have health and support workers on the ground in DRC. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Steven Hoffman , scientific director of the Canadian Institutes of Health Research&amp;rsquo ; s ( CIHR ) Institute of Population and Public Health , noted that responding to the current outbreak has been challenging . &amp;ldquo ;
