It is happening in a part of a country which is experiencing significant conflict , which makes it much more difficult for organizations and governments to help out . &amp;rdquo ; It is also happening close to a large urban area , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But Hoffman said the launch of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s rapid research fund for Ebola which supports four projects with African and Canadian researchers is an important accomplishment. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; From a Canadian perspective , this is a really good news story . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;25313;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;25355;TOOLONG ... story of ' the Canadian vaccine ' that beat back **26;25390;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; The fund was created for the West African Ebola outbreak and was mobilized soon after the DRC outbreak began last spring , in partnership with the Social Sciences and Humanities Research Council and Canada&amp;rsquo ; s International Development Research Centre . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Four Canadian and African research teams were chosen to work on projects during the current Ebola surge &amp;mdash ; including studying how better access to routine health information could help control outbreaks and the role social , cultural and environmental factors play in containing them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; No other national government has supported similar research that can be done during the Ebola crisis and help with future ones , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The big value is that this is happening as an outbreak is occurring , which allows them to study the mechanics of the response in a way they would not be able to do when looking at it historically . &amp;rdquo ; ' : Canada and the world have work to do before next pandemic , say expertshttp : **33;26221;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;26256;TOOLONG ... title= " Flu vaccinations. " alt= " Flu vaccinations. " data-portal-copyright= " Ed Kaiser " **28;26292;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1316125 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; One hundred years after the deadly Spanish flu killed tens of millions of people around the world , the conditions are ripe for another severe flu pandemic , say experts , and Canada is not as ready as it needs to be . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada is not alone . At the biannual Canadian Immunization Conference , being held in Ottawa this week , delegates heard from a leading international expert who warned that more work needs to be done globally before the next severe pandemic. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A focus of the conference was on the Spanish influenza pandemic that killed between 50 million and 100 million people around the world , as many as 50,000 in Canada alone . Canada and other countries are preparing for the next one . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Are we ready in 2018 ? &amp;rdquo ; asked Nancy Cox after giving a brief history of the 1918 pandemic. &amp;ldquo ; The answer is we are better off than we were before 2009 but not where we need to be and vaccine readiness has a long way to go . &amp;rdquo ; Cox headed the U.S. Centres for Disease Control influenza branch and led a World Health Organization centre on influenza control and surveillance . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;26322;TOOLONG ... title= " Influenza victims crowd into an emergency hospital near Fort Riley , Kansas during the 1918 Spanish flu pandemic killed at least 20 million people worldwide . " alt= " Influenza victims crowd into an emergency hospital near Fort Riley , Kansas during the 1918 Spanish flu pandemic killed at least 20 million people worldwide . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;26358;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1316120 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Despite medical advances since 1918 , neither vaccines nor anti-viral treatments are as effective as they should be and there are new challenges , including disseminating accurate information in the age of social media , conference delegates were told . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meanwhile , the goal of developing a universal flu vaccine , to replace seasonal vaccines created annually , remains elusive , and seasonal vaccines are often not very effective . And , while there has been progress , gaps remain in global surveillance and in availability of treatments . During a severe pandemic , said Cox , there would not be enough ventilators or reusable respiratory protective devices to support and protect people around the world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;26388;TOOLONG ... title= " Dr. Bonnie Henry is British Columbia 's provincial health officer and chairs the Canadian pandemic influenza task group " alt= " Dr. Bonnie Henry is British Columbia 's provincial health officer and chairs the Canadian pandemic influenza task group " data-portal-copyright= " JONATHAN HAYWARD " **28;26424;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1316117 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The 1918 pandemic was exceptionally severe . A similar pandemic today would cause tremendous illness , death and economic cost . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;26454;TOOLONG ... title= " Three Alberta farmers stand in a field wearing masks during the influenza epidemic that swept the world in the fall of 1918. " alt= " Three Alberta farmers stand in a field wearing masks during the influenza epidemic that swept the world in the fall of 1918. " data-portal-copyright= " Staff " **28;26490;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1308739 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Cox said estimates range from 105 million to 110 million potential deaths . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; That is a very sobering number . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Pandemic influenza is a global outbreak that occurs when a new flu virus for which humans have little or no immunity emerges and spreads easily . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canadian officials have been working to prepare the country for the next pandemic , building on lessons learned during the 2009 H1N1 pandemic , said Bonnie Henry , British Columbia&amp;rsquo ; s provincial health officer who chairs the Canadian pandemic influenza task group . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While progress has been made , gaps remain , she said . And Henry warned that even a pandemic with relatively mild to moderate impact can overwhelm services . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **33;26520;TOOLONG : &amp;nbsp ; &lt;a href= " https : **33;26555;TOOLONG ... to live longer ? There 's a gene for it&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The 2009 pandemic , for example , was considered to be relatively mild compared with other pandemics . But it severely stressed British Columbia&amp;rsquo ; s health system just months before the Winter Olympics were to be held in Vancouver . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; People think of 2009 as being very mild , but if the Olympics had been planned for the fall of 2009 , we would have had to cancel because we did not have ICU ( intensive care ) beds. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada&amp;rsquo ; s pandemic influenza task group aims to minimize serious illness and societal disruptions , to provide a safe and effective vaccine for all Canadians and to monitor the safety and effectiveness of the vaccine , said Henry . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;26590;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;26632;TOOLONG ... down the rhetoric , advises former anti-vax **38;26667;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; During the 2009 , there were logistic problems getting vaccines to all parts of the country quickly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Many of us may remember the challenges of not knowing when vaccines would come , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; A lot of things happened that we were not prepared for that led to inconsistencies . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada now has a contract for two doses of vaccine for every Canadian in the event of a pandemic , along with a backup contract , she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Planning is underway to do a better job of getting vaccine to remote communities and prioritize who gets vaccinated first . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among areas where more work is needed are effective electronic systems to manage vaccine inventory and track vaccine uptake , among other thing , said Henry . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We are still not where we need to be . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While Henry says there is work to be done , Canada is likely in better shape than many countries . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Most countries lack robust pandemic plans , said Cox , and very few countries hold emergency response exercises to ensure they are ready . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS : &lt;/strong&gt; After growing increasingly concerned about vaccines , the family had stopped immunizing their children . But in 2015 , Hills began to re-examine her views and was contacted by an Ottawa woman who runs a blog called The Scientific Parent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;27064;TOOLONG ... title= " In this file photo , a health worker prepares a syringe with a vaccine against measles . But before she could do so , all seven children were diagnosed with whooping cough &amp;mdash ; a vaccine preventable disease . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The children all recovered , but Hills said she didn&amp;rsquo ; t need to be told &amp;ldquo ; how much worse it could have been . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Tuesday , Hills brought her youngest infant to the conference at the Shaw Centre . The baby girl , like all eight of her siblings now , is up to date with her vaccines . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The family of 11 has stayed out of the limelight since the burst of media attention in 2015. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hills spoke to a standing-room-only crowd made up mainly of public health workers and officials who are increasingly trying to understand how better to communicate with parents about vaccines . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Vaccine-hesitant parents make up as much as 20 per cent of the population . And with Canada&amp;rsquo ; s rates of childhood immunization some of the lowest among wealthy countries , health officials say it is important to better understand why parents sometimes hesitate to vaccinate their children . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;27130;TOOLONG ... title= " In this Jan. 16 , 1957 file photo , Jon Douglas , 6 , right , visits his friend , Greg Cox , standing behind a sign warning he has mumps , on the door of his home in Altamont , Ill . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;27166;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1315828 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dr. Noni MacDonald , a Dalhousie University professor of pediatrics and vaccine expert , said Canada&amp;rsquo ; s childhood immunization rates make the country &amp;ldquo ; a sitting duck&amp;rdquo ; to see the return of deadly diseases such as the measles . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Germany recently had its first endemic cases of measles since the childhood disease was eradicated there . MacDonald warned that could happen in Canada if the country is complacent about childhood immunization rates . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We know there have been studies done in different provinces about why we are not getting where we would like to be , and over and over ( vaccine ) hesitancy is part of it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada&amp;rsquo ; s overall rate of immunization against measles is in the upper 80s , she said . To prevent measles , it needs to be 95 per cent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We just aren&amp;rsquo ; t getting where we need to go . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; MacDonald , who works with the World Health Organization on immunization , said it is embarrassing that Canada doesn&amp;rsquo ; t have higher immunization rates . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; All the public health units are really trying to work on improving immunization uptake , but this is not easy . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;27196;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;27238;TOOLONG ... mother of 7 abandons anti-vaxxer views as kids hit with whooping **37;27273;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; On Monday , the Canadian Paediatric Society sponsored a day-long workshop on the issue , offering tools to help health-care workers talk to vaccine-hesitant parents . Among key advice : Don&amp;rsquo ; t close the door on them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some physicians in the U.S. and elsewhere refuse to see patients who don&amp;rsquo ; t vaccinate their children . Canadian health officials say it is important to continue to treat them and raise the issue when appropriate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Canadian Immunization Conference , which is held every two years , is also focusing on the pandemic flu , and marking a century since the deadly Spanish flu . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nancy Cox , former director of the U.S. Centres for Disease Control&amp;rsquo ; s influenza division , said the conditions are right for a new flu pandemic. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If another one as severe as the Spanish flu occurred , it could kill as many as 110 million people , despite medical advances since 1918. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It is a very sobering number . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **27;27312;TOOLONG " **34;27341;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;27377;TOOLONG ... artist suffered mysterious symptoms for years . Clifford " **28;27665;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1272643 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;27695;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; PANAMA CITY , Fla. &amp;mdash ; The third-most powerful hurricane to hit the U.S. mainland in recorded history left a wide path of destruction across Florida and Georgia , destroying homes and shopping centres and felling trees that killed at least two people . And it&amp;rsquo ; s not done yet . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hurricane Michael finally weakened to a tropical storm on Thursday , no longer a Category 4 monster packing 155 mph ( 250 kph ) winds . But it was still menacing the Southeast with heavy rains , blustery winds and possible spinoff tornadoes , soaking areas swamped by epic flooding last month from Hurricane Florence . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; By 5 a.m. , Michael&amp;rsquo ; s eye was about 45 miles ( 72 kilometres ) west of Augusta , Georgia , packing top winds of 50 mph ( 80 kph ) and moving at 21 mph ( 33 kph ) into South Carolina , the National Hurricane Center in Miami said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;27731;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thousands of law enforcement officers and search and rescue teams rolled out in its wake to find survivors amid the wreckage of homes where people defied evacuation orders . Michael washed away white sand beaches , hammered military bases and destroyed coastal communities , stripping trees to stalks , shredding roofs , toppling trucks and pushing boats into buildings . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It will take some time for residents of north Florida to take stock of the enormity of the disaster . Reaching the worst-hit areas wasn&amp;rsquo ; t easy . The Florida Highway Patrol closed 80 miles of Interstate 10 , the main east-west route along Florida&amp;rsquo ; s Panhandle , to clear debris . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;27767;TOOLONG ... title= " MEXICO BEACH , FL -- OCTOBER 09 : Carol Cathey spray paints the words " Calm down Michael " on the plywood over her daughter 's business in preparation for the arrival of Hurricane Michael on October 9 , 2018 in Mexico Beach , Florida . " data-portal-copyright= " Joe Raedle " **28;27803;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1272642 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Many homes were ripped apart or washed away altogether in Mexico Beach , a town of 1,000 where the hurricane made landfall and the storm surge pushed lead-grey water up to the rooftops . Authorities said falling trees killed a man outside Tallahassee , Florida , and an 11-year-old girl in southwest Georgia . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; An Associated Press team drove for miles and encountered extensive destruction around Panama City , just west of where the centre of Michael&amp;rsquo ; s eye hit the shore . Although most homes were still standing , no property was left undamaged . Downed power lines lay nearly everywhere . Roofs were peeled away and sent airborne . Aluminum siding was shredded to ribbons . Homes were split open by fallen trees . Hundreds of cars had broken windows , many turned askew by the wind . Twisted street signs lay on the ground . Pine trees were stripped into stalks and snapped off about 20 feet high . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; More than 780,000 homes and businesses were without power Thursday in the wake of the storm . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;27833;TOOLONG ... title= " PANAMA CITY , FL -- OCTOBER 10 : Damaged buildings and a flooded street are seen after hurricane Michael passed through the downtown area on October 10 , 2018 in Panama City , Florida . " data-portal-copyright= " Joe Raedle " **28;27869;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1272644 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Families tentatively emerged from darkened shelters and hotels to this unfamiliar and perilous landscape , under a perfectly clear blue sky , to the sounds of a Coast Guard helicopter hovering over damaged neighbourhoods , beeping security alarms and the occasional sirens of police and fire department vehicles . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Vance Beu , 29 , was staying with his mother at her home , Spring Gate Apartments , a complex of single-story wood frame buildings where they piled up mattresses around themselves for protection . A pine tree punched a hole in their roof and his ears even popped when the barometric pressure went lower . The roar of the winds , he said , sounded like a jet engine . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It was terrifying , honestly . There was a lot of noise . We thought the windows were going to break at any time , &amp;rdquo ; Beu said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sally Crown rode out Michael on the Florida Panhandle thinking at first that the worst damage was the many trees downed in her yard . But after the storm passed , she emerged to check on the cafe she manages and discovered a scene of breathtaking destruction . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s absolutely horrendous . Catastrophic , &amp;rdquo ; Crown said . &amp;ldquo ; There&amp;rsquo ; s flooding . Boats on the highway . A house on the highway . Houses that have been there forever are just shattered . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A Panhandle man was killed by a tree that toppled on a home , Gadsden County Sheriff&amp;rsquo ; s Office spokeswoman Anglie Hightower said . But she added emergency crews trying to reach the home were hampered by downed trees and debris blocking roadways . The debris was a problem in many coastal communities and still hundreds of thousands of people were also left without power . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gov . Rick Scott said search and rescue efforts would be &amp;ldquo ; aggressive . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Hurricane Michael can not break Florida , &amp;rdquo ; Scott vowed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Michael sprang quickly from a weekend tropical depression , going from a Category 2 on Tuesday to a Category 4 by the time it came ashore . More than 375,000 people up and down the Gulf Coast were ordered or urged to evacuate , but it moved so fast that people didn&amp;rsquo ; t have much time to prepare , and emergency authorities lamented that many ignored the warnings , thinking they could ride it out . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In Panama City , plywood and metal flew off the front of a Holiday Inn Express . Part of the awning fell and shattered the glass front door of the hotel , and the rest of the awning wound up on vehicles parked below it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;27899;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Oh my God , what are we seeing ? &amp;rdquo ; said evacuee Rachel Franklin , her mouth hanging open . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Based on its internal barometric pressure , Michael was the third most powerful hurricane to hit the U.S. mainland , behind the unnamed Labor Day storm of 1935 and Camille in 1969 . Based on wind speed , it was the fourth-strongest , behind the Labor Day storm ( 184 mph , or 296 kph ) , Camille and Andrew in 1992. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The storm is likely to fire up the debate over global warming . Scientists say global warming is responsible for more intense and more frequent extreme weather , such as storms , droughts , floods and fires , and Michael was fueled by abnormal water temperatures in the Gulf &amp;mdash ; 4-to-5 degrees above the historic norm for this time of year . But without extensive study , they can not directly link a single weather event to the changing climate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;27935;TOOLONG ... title= " SAINT MARKS , FL -- OCTOBER 10 : Bo Lynn 's Market starts taking water in the town of Saint Marks as Hurricane Michael pushes the storm surge up the Wakulla and Saint Marks Rivers which come together here on October 10 , 2018 in Saint Marks , Florida . " data-portal-copyright= " Julie Oliver " **28;28628;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1253911 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; In his 23-page affidavit , Boyle alleges his wife assaulted him and neglected their children because of untreated mental health issues . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He also alleges Coleman tried to push him in front of a Toronto Transit Commission subway car years before they were married . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Justice Engelking , however , said she received no evidence to support the contention that Coleman suffers from a mental health issue that could affect her suitability as a parent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The court does have evidence , on the other hand , that C.C. is healthily and protectively parenting the children , &amp;rdquo ; Engelking noted before concluding : &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; Based on all of the evidence before me , I can find little to suggest C.C. would not be a suitable temporary custodian of the children . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Obtained by this newspaper after a court application , the documents in the case offer a window into the couple&amp;rsquo ; s deeply troubled relationship and provide new details about their hellish , five-year hostage ordeal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Boyle , an aspiring journalist , met Coleman , a Quiznos restaurant manager , online in 2002 . They became romantically involved four years later , but theirs was a turbulent , on-again , off-again relationship . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Boyle says their shared interests sustained them . &amp;ldquo ; We both enjoyed BDSM ( bondage ) , &amp;rdquo ; Boyle says in his affidavit . &amp;ldquo ; We both wanted to travel by way of backpacking , and we both wanted to see the world . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; They married during a trip through Central America in July 2011 , but soon separated . Coleman began divorce proceedings in March 2012. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One month later , Boyle visited Coleman at her home in Pennsylvania where they reconciled . &amp;nbsp ; They agreed to go backpacking through Central Asia , he says , and decided to leave on the trip even after finding out that Coleman was pregnant with their first child . They departed in July 2012. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In her affidavit , Coleman insists that she reluctantly agreed to the trip only after Boyle promised not to go to Afghanistan . Boyle disclosed his true intentions after they landed in Central Asia , she says , &amp;ldquo ; so that I would not back out . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;28658;TOOLONG ... title= " Caitlan Coleman . " data-portal-copyright= " Michelle Shephard " **28;28694;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1253912 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Boyle insists Coleman knew he intended to go to Afghanistan &amp;mdash ; a trip designed to further his journalistic ambitions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We crossed into Afghanistan for a short while in hopes that I could meet people who could give me a story I could write about , &amp;rdquo ; he says in his affidavit . &amp;ldquo ; I had hoped that personal experience in Afghanistan might help me to land more permanent journalism work . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Boyle has previously suggested that they travelled to Afghanistan to do humanitarian work . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; They were taken hostage on Oct. 10 , 2012 , after leaving a Kabul guest house in a taxi . Coleman was five months pregnant at the time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Boyle says their captors inflicted severe physical and psychological damage on them for the next five years , during which they were moved to 19 hideouts in both Afghanistan and Pakistan . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In their affidavits , Boyle and Coleman offer completely different accounts of their relationship during captivity . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Coleman says Boyle became increasingly erratic and irrational during their ordeal , and fixated on &amp;ldquo ; depicting me as an enemy in his life . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The guards would separate us for a few days , weeks or months at a time , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &amp;ldquo ; When we were returned together , J.B. would accuse me of betraying him by accepting niceties from the guards and not asking for him more often . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After three years in captivity , he became increasingly abusive , she says : &amp;ldquo ; J.B. had uncontrolled rage , instituted corporal punishment of me , and struck me in a fit of rage . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He repeatedly told her that she was &amp;ldquo ; one of the worst people in the world , &amp;rdquo ; Coleman says , and suggested that &amp;ldquo ; a husband who kills his wife is justified . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After a disagreement in February 2017 , she alleges , Boyle &amp;ldquo ; hit me in the face hard enough to break my cheekbone. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Coleman alleges Boyle later confined her to a small shower stall for weeks at a time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **33;28724;TOOLONG : &amp;nbsp ; &lt;a href= " https : **33;28759;TOOLONG ... vs . Against Trump , Canada never had a chancehttp : **34;30390;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;30426;TOOLONG ... title= " Lester Pearson and Lyndon Johnson did n't exactly build a warm relationship either . ( Gazette file photo ) " alt= " Lester Pearson and Lyndon Johnson did n't exactly build a warm relationship either . ( Gazette file photo ) " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;30462;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1215057 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **33;30492;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; WASHINGTON &amp;ndash ; It had to come to this . Given the man and his methods , is it any surprise ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We hoped otherwise , that with goodwill , generosity and forbearance &amp;ndash ; as well as commerce , history and geography &amp;ndash ; we could come to an understanding with Donald J. Trump . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We tolerated his fears and his facts . We ignored his assault on the liberal international order : his resentment of NATO , his withdrawal from the Paris Agreement , the Trans-Pacific Partnership and the Iranian nuclear deal . We abstained at the United Nations when he moved the U.S. Embassy to Jerusalem , much as we opposed it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We winced when he lied about his trade surplus with us . We stayed at the table when he made demands on NAFTA that no self-respecting nation could accept . Indeed , we vowed to stay at the table until he took away the table &amp;ndash ; which he may yet do . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We recruited Brian Mulroney to seduce him . We established a war room in Ottawa to map strategy . We created a women&amp;rsquo ; s business forum to flatter Ivanka Trump and invited her to attend &amp;ldquo ; Come From Away&amp;rdquo ; on Broadway . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We sent cabinet ministers to court governors . We imposed a rigid discipline across government : no tweets , no jibes . No one called this president &amp;ldquo ; a moron , &amp;rdquo ; as Jean Chr&amp;eacute ; tien&amp;rsquo ; s official did George W. Bush ; we left that self-evident truth to Rex Tillerson. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;30527;TOOLONG ... title= " Lester Pearson and Lyndon Johnson did n't exactly build a warm relationship either . " alt= " Lester Pearson and Lyndon Johnson did n't exactly build a warm relationship either . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;30563;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1215057 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; For 500 days , this is how Prime Minister Justin Trudeau managed President Trump . And after the world&amp;rsquo ; s greatest charm offensive since the Khmer Rouge flirted with democracy , this was our reward : a twitter tantrum from the stateroom of Air Force One as it banked over the St. Lawrence River . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; To Trump , &amp;ldquo ; Justin&amp;rdquo ; is now dishonest , weak , crafty and treacherous . And Canada &amp;ndash ; the Mister Rogers of nations &amp;ndash ; is a &amp;ldquo ; backstabber&amp;rdquo ; and &amp;ldquo ; double-crosser , &amp;rdquo ; consigned to the penthouse suite in Hell . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Backstabber : This was how Nazis , conservatives and royalists described the republicans , Bolsheviks and Jews whom they blamed for Germany 's defeat in the First World War . Hell : This is where sinners suffer forever , no place for boy scouts and life insurers . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; All this heavy artillery trained on little ole Canada ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Now Trump threatens more tariffs . Relations have not been this bad since a livid Lyndon Johnson grabbed Lester Pearson by the lapels in 1965 and hissed , &amp;ldquo ; You peed on my carpet ! &amp;rdquo ; It takes talent to &amp;ldquo ; lose&amp;rdquo ; Canada , but Trump may just do it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For Canada , this may be a watershed , inviting us to begin to diversify our trade more strategically and pivot to Europe and Asia ( which we have contemplated for a generation ) . It may be the moment we say &amp;ndash ; with respect , of course &amp;ndash ; that enough&amp;rsquo ; s enough . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Three things are striking here . The first is the solidarity across politics . When Jason Kenney , John Baird and Doug Ford are &amp;ldquo ; standing shoulder to shoulder&amp;rdquo ; with you , you&amp;rsquo ; re doing something right . ( Only Conservative leader Andrew Scheer , who supported Brexit , has dissented . Picking a fight with Trudeau on trade is lunacy for him . Politically , Scheer is Simon and Garfunkel&amp;rsquo ; s elephant : kindly but dumb. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The truth is that economic nationalism sells and so does standing up to the Americans . If nothing else , we&amp;rsquo ; ll defend our dignity in the face of a president who is fonder of Kim Jong-un than of Justin . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The second is that Trump has handed Trudeau next year&amp;rsquo ; s election . The prime minister now has a cause and a constituency casting him as Captain Canada against the Americans , much as his father was against the separatists . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The third is our options are limited . We can impose measured sanctions , dollar-for-dollar , and hope Trump will not retaliate further . If he does , he will send our economy into recession . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One thing we know : Negotiating rationally with a studied ignoramus is impossible . More than erratic or mercurial , he is simply faithless . He believes in nothing and no one in love , friendship , business or politics . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trump sees the world only his way . He traffics in falsehood , humiliation and intimidation . Against all that , we never had a chance . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **30;30593;TOOLONG Cohen&lt;/strong&gt; is a journalist , professor and author of Two Days in June : John F. " data-portal-copyright= " JOSE JORDAN " **28;32405;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1171547 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Only weeks ago , scientists reluctantly moved the hands of the Doomsday Clock forward 30 seconds , to two minutes before midnight , a proximity to mankind&amp;rsquo ; s annihilation unmatched since 1953 , when the U.S. and Soviet Union set off thermonuclear devices in their respective test kitchens . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We&amp;rsquo ; ve never been closer to the figurative end of the world than this , although it should be noted that these same scientists , or at least ones just like them , have been known to bend the hands of time backwards , too , as they did in 1991 , when the end of the Cold War added a whopping 10 minutes to our countdown to oblivion . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fuelling the current bleak outlook of the Bulletin of Atomic Scientists&amp;rsquo ; Science and Security Board are , largely , three factors : climate change , the threat of nuclear war and what it describes as the &amp;ldquo ; breakdown in the international order&amp;rdquo ; led by an unreliable U.S. unwilling to assume its traditional mantle of world leadership , where &amp;ldquo ; international diplomacy has been reduced to name-calling. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a word : Trump . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Is there hope for mankind ? Of course . As the board notes , &amp;ldquo ; Humankind has invented the implements of apocalypse ; so can it invent the methods of controlling and eventually eliminating them . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Unbeknownst to these nervous scientists , help may be closer than we think , and it may come from a most unlikely source : Russia . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I know what you&amp;rsquo ; re thinking : &amp;ldquo ; Russia ? Isn&amp;rsquo ; t that an oligarchical kleptocracy headed by a shirtless horseman named Tom Thuggery , who is willing to poison enemies , bomb innocent civilians and annex entire nations while lining his and his cronies&amp;rsquo ; pockets with riches amassed from the blood , sweat and tears of the very people he&amp;rsquo ; s supposed to serve ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yeah , OK , you got me there . Guilty on all counts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But let&amp;rsquo ; s talk about that mantle of world leadership for a moment , and its place in the new world order . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The rise of globalization has been an inevitable fact for at least a century and a half , as efficient and almost instantaneous communications , from transatlantic telegraphs to the Internet , have leapt tall walls and seemingly impregnable iron curtains , fed Arab springs and erased geopolitical borders in their wakes . Meanwhile , following its late , great entry into the Second World War , the U.S. positioned itself as the world&amp;rsquo ; s alpha superpower . That whole race to put a man on the moon , you&amp;rsquo ; ll recall , was not so much about mankind&amp;rsquo ; s insatiable curiosity and thirst for knowledge ( blah , blah , blah ) as it was an exercise in being the first dog to piddle on our nearest neighbour&amp;rsquo ; s lawn . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; All that and democracy , too . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Its global hegemony , not to mention the unwavering rightness of its course , thus established , it was only natural that the president of the U.S. would come to be pretty much universally recognized as &amp;ldquo ; the leader of the free world&amp;rdquo ; and &amp;ldquo ; the most powerful man in the world . &amp;rdquo ; After all , the decisions he makes affect not just the 300+ million people in his electoral basket , but billions , everywhere , from Afghanistan to Zimbabwe . How different might the world look today if Harry S. Truman hadn&amp;rsquo ; t authorized the use of the atomic bomb in 1945 ? If Richard Nixon hadn&amp;rsquo ; t courted China in 1972 ? If Ronald Reagan&amp;rsquo ; s efforts to defuse the Cold War in the late 1980s had failed ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Speaking to the Press Club in Washington in 1969 , then-Canadian prime minister Pierre Trudeau likened Canada&amp;rsquo ; s close relationship with the U.S. to sleeping with an elephant . &amp;ldquo ; No matter how friendly and even-tempered is the beast &amp;hellip ; &amp;rdquo ; he said , &amp;ldquo ; one is affected by every twitch and grunt . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In other words , when the U.S. sneezes , the whole world catches a cold . And when President Trump announces tariffs on steel and aluminum , you can bet there&amp;rsquo ; s some kid in Duisburg , Germany who&amp;rsquo ; s not getting a second slice of black forest cake that night . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So let&amp;rsquo ; s look at one of the chief questions now being asked by special prosecutor Robert Mueller III , and others . Did Russia meddle in the 2016 U.S. presidential election ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Well , of course it did . That , at least , is the conclusion of the FBI , CIA , NSA and the Office of the Director of National Intelligence . And it must surely rankle America&amp;rsquo ; s collective sense of fairness and its values to see one country interfere in the affairs of another , or at least to see someone ELSE do it , and so well . After all , the U.S. has for decades effectively owned the podium when it comes to sticking oars in foreign electoral waters . It medalled for such subversion in Italy in 1948 , Iran in 1953 , Guatemala in 1954 , Japan in &amp;rsquo ; 58 , Chile in &amp;rsquo ; 64 and &amp;lsquo ; 73 , Israel in &amp;rsquo ; 92 , Serbia in 2000 , Honduras in 2009 &amp;hellip ; the list goes on . According to Carnegie-Mellon University researcher Dov H. Levin , the U.S. attempted to sway 81 foreign elections between 1948 and 2000 , compared to just three dozen similar Russian or Soviet undertakings . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And 2016 was hardly the first time the two superpowers were all up in each other&amp;rsquo ; s grills . In 1961 , then-Soviet premier Nikita Khrushchev boasted to John F. Kennedy that the U.S.S.R. helped him win the U.S. presidency a year earlier . In 1982 , meanwhile , KGB chairman Yuri Andropov ordered operatives to engage in &amp;ldquo ; active measures&amp;rdquo ; to discourage Ronald Reagan&amp;rsquo ; s 1984 re-election . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On the flip side , who can forget Time magazine&amp;rsquo ; s July 15 , 1996 cover , depicting Russian president Boris Yeltsin holding an American flag , underneath the headline &amp;ldquo ; Yanks to the Rescue : The Secret Story of How American Advisers Helped Yeltsin Win&amp;rdquo ; ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Did Russia meddle in the 2016 U.S. presidential election ? The very phrasing of question presupposes a certain negative response . The word &amp;ldquo ; meddle&amp;rdquo ; is so &amp;hellip ; so &amp;hellip ; well , meddlesome . How about &amp;ldquo ; participate&amp;rdquo ; ? Did Russia participate in the 2016 U.S. presidential election ? Yes , and really , why not ? By then it had already heard Hillary Clinton&amp;rsquo ; s sniffles and sneezes from her tenure as Secretary of State , and well knew it didn&amp;rsquo ; t want to catch the full-blown cold . Trump , a noted germaphobe , was the obvious choice . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But at least Russia engaged in the process . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Can Canada , its economic future now muddied under the threat of a shredded North American Free Trade Agreement , say the same ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Did Libya , Somalia , Syria and others , whose citizens now can not , because of a contentious travel ban , eat at any of Arby&amp;rsquo ; s 3,300 stateside restaurants , buy even a single Facebook ad in the run-up to the election ? Did even one CO2-friendly nation &amp;mdash ; I&amp;rsquo ; m looking at you , Chad &amp;mdash ; knock on a single door or put up a Bernie Sanders lawn sign ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Admittedly , Russia&amp;rsquo ; s contributions to the 2016 election were , in hindsight , a little heavy-handed and clumsy at times , but , to be fair , the country hasn&amp;rsquo ; t enjoyed as much practise with global democracy as have other nations . It&amp;rsquo ; s a bit like the excited youngster who , by noon on Christmas Day , has already broken most of her toys . They&amp;rsquo ; ll learn with time , and perhaps with our help . On Sunday &amp;mdash ; incidentally the fourth anniversary of Russia annexing Crimea &amp;mdash ; Russian voters head to the polls to decide which of seven presidential candidates they would like to see finish second to Vladimir Putin ( I kid you not : when he declared his candidacy a little over a year ago , Communists of Russia chairman Maxim Suraykin announced his intention &amp;ldquo ; if not to win , then , in any case , take second place . It can not be otherwise . &amp;rdquo ; ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One of Putin&amp;rsquo ; s loudest opponents , Alexei Navalny , was disqualified from the race due to an embezzlement conviction that he says was politically motivated , while polls suggest none of the other candidates enjoys more than 10 per cent support . But one of them , 36-year-old Ksenia Sobchak , was host of a popular Russian reality TV show ; what happened the last time the establishment underestimated a reality-TV host-turned-politician ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the last presidential election in Russia , in 2012 , Putin won with almost 64 per cent of the vote , and there are hopes among supporters that he might top 70 per cent this time . Meanwhile , there&amp;rsquo ; s not much time left to get involved . But who&amp;rsquo ; s to say ? It&amp;rsquo ; s not over until the polls close , and maybe not even then . As Joseph Stalin reportedly said almost a century ago , &amp;ldquo ; I consider it completely unimportant who in the party will vote , or how ; but what is extraordinarily important is this &amp;mdash ; who will count the votes , and how . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Do you really want to make the world a better place ? Then get involved . Think globally and &lt;em&gt;act&lt;/em&gt; globally . If enough people write a few Facebook messages here and there , post some Instagram photos of encouragement , create a little &amp;ldquo ; fake news , &amp;rdquo ; who knows what might happen ? Perhaps someone can hack their way to a few thousand emails worth leaking . One thing is certain : no good will come of sitting idly by and staring at the Doomsday Clock as its hands approach midnight . &lt;/p&gt; Bruce DeachmanFri , 16 Mar 2018 12:00:15 **45;32435;TOOLONG ... held in Syria after trying to bring sons home , arrives in Ottawahttp : **34;32482;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;32518;TOOLONG ... title= " Reyenne Annous ( L ) and Jolly Bimbachi ( R ) reuniting at the Ottawa airport . She wondered aloud whether she should take a bath before or after spending time with her family ; she got excited about Tim Hortons ; she joked that her time in Syria was a &amp;ldquo ; vacation&amp;rdquo ; and added that she&amp;rsquo ; s not planning any others . She took pictures with her family and cracked jokes about her lack of luggage . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But as happy a return as it was for Bimbachi , it was still an incomplete one , and it is clear that the absence of her sons in Canada is a point of acute sadness for Bimbachi. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I know they were super excited to come here , to come back home and be with their cousins , and their grandmother and grandfather , to be back in Canada , &amp;rdquo ; said Bimbachi , early Thursday morning . &amp;ldquo ; It didn&amp;rsquo ; t happen . " data-portal-copyright= " OMAR HAJ KADOUR " **28;32620;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1154484 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bimbachi , along with Sean Allen Moore , both of whom are from Chatham , Ont. , had been held captive for nearly a month by Hay&amp;rsquo ; at Tahrir al-Sham ( HTS ) , an al-Qaeda-affiliated group in Syria , after the pair attempted to smuggle Bimbachi&amp;rsquo ; s two sons , Omar and Abdal-Geniy , out of Lebanon and back to Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Her sons had been in Lebanon with their father Ali Ahmad , Bimbachi&amp;rsquo ; s ex-husband , since May 2015 . According to Bimbachi , Ahmad had taken the boys on a vacation to visit family in Lebanon when Bimbachi received a call from the boys&amp;rsquo ; uncle telling her that they would not be returning to Canada . The boys have been in Lebanon ever since . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In November , Bimbachi travelled to Lebanon , hoping to come to a custody arrangement in the Lebanese court system . When those negotiations broke down , Bimbachi connected with smugglers who , she had hoped , would escort them through Syria and across the Turkish border , where she would be able to get to the Canadian embassy and ultimately home to Ontario . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Those plans went awry , she says , when people connected to the boys&amp;rsquo ; father began posting on social media that Bimbachi was planning to convert the children to Christianity and offering a cash reward for their return . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It became dangerous for us to move , &amp;rdquo ; said Bimbachi in an video posted by SITE Intelligence Group . &amp;ldquo ; I don&amp;rsquo ; t know how the Hay&amp;rsquo ; at Tahrir al-Sham got involved , but I think it&amp;rsquo ; s a good thing they did . They came and they got us and we spent a few nice nights with my boys before they took them back to Lebanon . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The specifics of what happened to Bimbachi and Moore during the month of January remain somewhat unclear to her ; she&amp;rsquo ; s not certain where the smuggling ended and the captivity began , or who it was that she was being held captive by . As the group was travelling through Syria , her and her sons were &amp;ldquo ; bounced around&amp;rdquo ; from safehouse to safehouse , and eventually handed off to the Syrian Salvation Government . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Originally we were supposed to drive from Lebanon to the Turkish border , &amp;rdquo ; said Moore , in the same video . &amp;ldquo ; It went south in Lebanon , I just didn&amp;rsquo ; t know it yet . As soon as the walking started I knew something wasn&amp;rsquo ; t right . So we just kept on moving forward until we got here . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She and Moore were separated for part of the time , Bimbachi said , and while she was put up in safehouses , Moore was held &amp;ldquo ; in a jail . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I won&amp;rsquo ; t say that I wasn&amp;rsquo ; t scared , &amp;rdquo ; but added that she was &amp;ldquo ; well taken care of&amp;rdquo ; and never felt like she was in any serious danger . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Moore returned home to Chatham on Wednesday , said Bimbachi. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The two had flown together from Turkey to Toronto before going their separate ways . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;32650;TOOLONG ... title= " Canadian citizen Jolly Bimbachi , who was being held along with compatriot Sean Moore by a jihadist-dominated alliance in northwest Syria , speaks to the press at the Bab al-Hawa crossing with Turkey on February 5 , 2018 after they were released to Turkish authorities .
